(`forceLinting` acts only when a lint is due, which any change to the text arranges; see the entry on `relint`.)
- **2026-10-25** — **`relint(view)` lints on demand, and a fix keeps the editor's line break.** In `@codemirror/lint` 6.9.x, `forceLinting` runs only a lint that is already pending, and one becomes pending after a text change, a reconfigure of the linter, or `needsRefresh`. A change to what `template()` or `options()` returns is none of these, so Strict Fountain switched on changed nothing until the next keystroke. `fountainLint` gives `linter` a `needsRefresh` that is true for a transaction carrying a private effect, and `relint(view)` dispatches that effect and then calls `forceLinting`. The app calls `relint` after changing the template or the options. A fix's changes come from a parse that reads every line break as `\n`; the action replaces each `\r?\n` in an insert with `state.lineBreak` before it dispatches, since CodeMirror splits a line only at its own break and a bare `\n` in a CRLF editor would sit inside a line. Tests: the severity changes after `relint` with no edit; and under `lineSeparator` `\r\n`, "Insert a blank line" adds a real line and the text has `\r\n\r\n`.
- **2026-10-25** — **Tab starts the name list.** Phase 1 design §5.3 has names come up after Tab on a blank line. `fountainTab` puts in an `@` and turns on cue mode, which is too little for the completion to open on its own, so `fountain()` binds Tab to a command that runs `fountainTab` and, if that transaction carried `startCue`, calls `startCompletion`. The source then lists the whole cast for the `@`. A Tab that does anything else, such as cycling an extension, or that is not handled, opens nothing. `fountainKeymap` itself is unchanged, so an app that builds its own keymap from it does not get the list.
- **2026-10-25** — **`fountain()` adds lint only with a template.** Lint needs a template, so `fountain({ template })` adds `fountainLint`, with `lintOptions` for its options, and `fountain()` alone has no lint and no gutter. `onCursorElement` adds the callback. `fountain` is in `src/fountain.ts`, and `index.ts` stays a list of exports.
- **2026-10-25** — **A note or boneyard typed on a heading or cue line is left as typed.** The upper-casing filter is for the heading and the name, and a note after them is the writer's own words. While a `[[` or `/*` is open the parse has no region for it, so the filter reads the line before the cursor for the last opener with no closer after it, and also asks the parse whether the position is inside a note or boneyard that is closed or runs to the end. In either case nothing is changed. Typing goes back to upper case after the closer, so `INT. hall [[check it]] now` is `INT. HALL [[check it]] NOW`.
- **2026-10-25** — **`forceKind` keeps a byte-order mark.** `trim` counts U+FEFF as space, so the command took off the BOM at the start of a file along with the spaces. It now leaves a BOM at the start of the line out of the line's text and replaces only what follows, and the cursor's place is counted from there. The spaces around the text still go with the marker, as above.
- **2026-10-27** — **The element bar's commands write natural Fountain.** `makeElement(kind)` (in `elements.ts`) makes the cursor's line, or each line with text in a selection of several, an element the way a writer new to Fountain would want it read: a heading upper-cased with `INT. ` before it, a name upper-cased, a transition that ends in `TO:` left bare, and a forcing mark only where the line would otherwise read as something else. Whether Action needs its `!` is asked of the parser, by `reparse` of the line as it would be; Character asks `readsAsCue`, since a cue with no speech yet is action to the parse. Mod-Alt-1…7 run them too (below).
- **2026-10-27** — **What each element command does where it's less obvious.** Scene, Character, Transition, and Centered put a blank line above the line where there is none (Scene and Transition one below as well), since those are read only at the start of a paragraph; Lyric and Action do so only to take a line out of a speech. A heading gets `.` instead of `INT. ` only when the whole line is one of a few words for a scene that names no place (`FLASHBACK`, `MONTAGE`, `SERIES OF SHOTS`, `DREAM SEQUENCE`, each also after `END`, and `INTERCUT`, `BACK TO SCENE`, `LATER`, `MOMENTS LATER`, `CONTINUOUS`; review fix: `DREAM HOUSE` and `BACK TO SCHOOL HALLWAY` are places, and get `INT.`). Dialogue on a cue goes to the speech (or a new line for one); on action just under a cue with blank lines between, the blank lines go; on a parenthetical or lyric in a speech, its mark goes; on an empty line under a speech, nothing; anywhere else, a new `@` cue line goes above the line, in cue mode with the names list, for the writer to name the speaker. Parenthetical applies only on a cue, or a name with no speech under it yet (it adds a line under it), in a speech, or on an empty line under one; elsewhere it returns false and `canMakeParenthetical` says so. Pressing the button for the element a line already is takes off a forcing mark the line doesn't need (`@MARGARET` with speech under it becomes `MARGARET`), upper-cases a heading typed in lower case, and otherwise changes nothing and leaves the cursor where it is. No command acts on the title page or in a note or boneyard.
- **2026-10-27** — **Completion opens through an effect.** A state command can't open the list, so the commands that begin a heading, a cue, or a transition on an empty line put `askCompletion` (or Tab's `startCue`) in their transaction, and `withCompletion(command)` runs a command as a view command and opens the list when one is there, as `fountain()`'s Tab already did.
- **2026-10-27** — **Mod-Alt-1…7 run the element commands** (the maintainer's ruling after review; supersedes keeping them on `forceKind`). A key a beginner learns from a button's tooltip should do what the button does. The keymap moved to `bindings.ts` (it imports `elements.ts`, which imports the keymap's helpers), and `fountain()` runs each through `withCompletion`, so Mod-Alt-3 on an empty line opens the names as the button does. `forceKind` stays as a command with no key; the app lists it as "Force … (mark)". Mod-Alt-4 outside a speech returns false and leaves the key to the browser, as the bar's Parenthetical is off there.
- **2026-10-27** — **Review fixes to the element commands.** A whole-line selection (a triple click, Mod-L, which end at the next line's start) acts on the selected line, not the one after it. Upper-casing leaves a line's notes and boneyard as typed, as the typing filter does. A name with a capital inside a word (`McDONALD`, `McDonald`) keeps its spelling and gets the `@` it then needs; `Margaret` is still upper-cased. Character acts on the first line of a selection only, since a cue and its speech are rarely meant to be one name. Action on a page break leaves it.
- **2026-10-27** — **A cue the element bar begins loses its `@` when the cursor leaves it.** Character on an empty line and Dialogue's new cue still write `@`, since cue mode needs it to upper-case the name as it's typed, and also send `startBarCue`; `barCue` remembers the line, and a transaction filter takes the `@` off in the transaction that moves the cursor off it (Enter, a click, an arrow key) if the name reads as a cue without it. So a beginner's speeches read `MARGARET`. Tab's `@` is the writer's and stays, as before.
- **2026-10-27** — **`elementHint(kind, state)` says why a command does nothing,** in a sentence: on the title page, in a note or boneyard, on a line that is already dialogue (or the empty line under a name), Parenthetical where it doesn't apply, and Action on a page break. The app shows it in the bar's notice.
- **2026-10-27** — **A transition that ends in `TO:` completes over its `>`.** The completion's options for a `>` line replace the `>` too when they end in `TO:` (an `apply` with `insertCompletionText` from the `>`), so Transition on an empty line, then CUT TO: from the list, leaves `CUT TO:`.
- **2026-10-27** — **The element commands follow the template's structure** (final review, I-1). `structureOf(template)` reads the role map by resolving a sample block of each kind: the section depth whose role is `act_heading` is the act, the one that is `scene_heading_play` the scene, and a `disabled` role for `scene_heading` or `transition` takes those away. `fountain({ template })` provides it through the `elementStructure` facet (a function, as the template can change), so no template id is hard-coded. On a stage play Scene (and Mod-Alt-1) writes a `##` section set apart by blank lines, a new `act` kind writes `#`, and Transition (and Mod-Alt-5) returns false with `elementHint` saying a play has no transitions. A state with no template is a screenplay's.
- **2026-10-27** — **A key that does nothing says why** (final review, m-8): `fountain({ onHint })` is told the `elementHint` for Mod-Alt-1…7 where there is one, and the app shows it in the bar's notice. The key still goes back to the browser.
- **2026-10-27** — **The bar's `@` comes off only on the writer's own move** (final review, m-2): the filter acts only on a transaction with a user event other than `input.replace…`, so a file put back from disk or Revert to Saved is never touched. A cue begun from the bar and left with nothing typed loses its `@` too (m-3), leaving the empty line.

## `apps/web`

- **2026-10-25** — **Fonts.** The screen uses the same Courier Prime TTF files the PDF embeds, copied into `public/` at build time by `scripts/sync-assets.mjs`, so the screen and the printed page share their metrics. Inter Tight is self-hosted as one variable WOFF2 font (weights 400 to 800) in two character sets, latin and latin-ext, as Google Fonts splits it. `@nuxt/fonts` is off.
- **2026-10-25** — **Icons.** `@nuxt/icon` runs with `provider: 'none'` and a scanned client bundle, so every icon is in the build and none is fetched at runtime. An icon the scan can't see must be listed in `icon.clientBundle.icons`.
- **2026-10-25** — **A local server that reads `_headers`.** The browser tests run against the generated site through `scripts/serve.mjs`, which applies Netlify's `_headers` file, so they see the same headers, the content security policy included, that drafter.dev sends. Like Netlify, it serves a directory's `index.html` and finds `/name` as `name.html`.
- **2026-10-25** — **Styles may be inline; scripts may not.**
  - **Styles:** the policy is `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'`, because Nuxt UI (for its color variables), color mode, and the tour module add style elements at runtime. No style hash ever goes into `style-src`: browsers ignore `'unsafe-inline'` in a directive that also lists a hash.
  - **Scripts:** they stay locked to the hashes of the inline scripts each build produced, which `scripts/csp.mjs` computes after the build (technical design §14). Today that's Nuxt's import map, its config script, and the color-mode script.
- **2026-10-25** — **One pane below 1024px,** Tailwind's `lg`. On those screens the toolbar shows its icons, with their labels kept for screen readers. `useWideScreen()` holds the media query.
- **2026-10-25** — **Tabs activate manually.** Arrow keys move focus along a pane's tabs, and Enter or Space chooses one, so arrowing past a tab that's open in the other pane never swaps them. A tab points `aria-controls` at its panel only when that panel is showing, and panels are keyed by tab, since a tab's content is in one place at a time. Panels take focus (`tabindex="0"`), because they scroll and hold nothing else focusable.
- **2026-10-25** — **Nuxt UI's palettes are amber and zinc,** its closest built-in ones. Drafter's exact colors come from `assets/css/tokens.css`, and `test/tokens.test.ts` holds every text color in it to WCAG contrast.
- **2026-10-25** — **Nitro's `static` preset, pinned.** Netlify sets `NETLIFY=true`, and from that Nitro would choose its `netlify-static` preset, which writes the site to `dist/` rather than `.output/public`. Then the CSP step and Netlify's publish directory find nothing. `nuxt.config.ts` pins `static`, so local and Netlify builds are identical.
  - `pnpm e2e` deletes `.output` first and builds with `NETLIFY=true`, so the browser tests go through Netlify's preset selection.
  - Without the deletion, a stale `.output/public` from an earlier build hid the failure.
- **2026-10-25** — **Phones get WebKit too.** iPhones run WebKit whatever the browser, so the browser tests run the phone checks on an iPhone SE in WebKit as well as on a Pixel 7 in Chromium. The width tests load the page at 320 and 360 pixels and compare the page's width with the root's client width. Under mobile emulation, `innerWidth` grows to fit whatever overflows, so it can't show overflow. Below 400px the toolbar shows the square mark instead of the wordmark, which keeps it within 320px (WCAG 1.4.10).
- **2026-10-25** — **Forced colors.** In forced-colors mode (Windows High Contrast), the selected tab's background and underline are dropped, so a border marks it there.
- **2026-10-25** — **The theme lives in localStorage,** through `@nuxtjs/color-mode`, not in IndexedDB, where the Phase 1 design (§8) keeps settings. The theme must be read before the first paint, and IndexedDB is asynchronous.
- **2026-10-26** — **One editor, kept in module state.** `useEditor()` (`app/editor/session.ts`) creates the `EditorView` once, on first use in the browser, and keeps it for the visit, with the parse, the cursor's element, and the template as shallow refs. The Source tab's panel (`SourcePane.vue`) moves `view.dom` into its host on mount and back to a hidden holder in `index.vue` before it unmounts, so swapping panes or changing between one pane and two keeps the text, the undo history, and the cursor. A second view is not needed until the design asks for one (Phase 1 design §5.1).
- **2026-10-26** — **The Drafter packages resolve to their source through a Vite alias,** set in `nuxt.config.ts` as `vite.resolve.alias`: one regular expression that matches exactly `@drafter/<name>` for the six browser-side packages and points at `packages/<name>/src/index.ts`. It covers the client, the server bundle, and the layout worker, and the build passes with every package's `dist/` removed. A subpath such as `@drafter/render-html/style.css` isn't matched and resolves as usual. This replaces a global `source` export condition (`vite.resolve.conditions`), which reached every dependency: `@internationalized/date` and `number`, from Nuxt UI, list `source` first and would have compiled their raw TypeScript the first time a date or number input was used. Types still come from each package's built `dist/index.d.ts`: adding the condition to the type check pulls in raw TypeScript from other dependencies that use `source` (it fails inside `@internationalized/date`), so the packages are built before `typecheck`, as before.
- **2026-10-26** — **The editor's focus ring is on the whole editor,** gutters included, 2px in the goldenrod and inside the pane's edge: `&.cm-focused, &:focus-within`. CodeMirror's own is a 1px dotted dark line, invisible on the dark theme, and it adds `cm-focused` a moment after focus, which the "focus ring on every control" test catches. `:focus-within` is immediate. That test reads the outline of the `.cm-editor` around the focused text.
- **2026-10-26** — **`tabindex="0"` on the editor's text,** which is a tab stop already. Without the attribute, axe's `scrollable-region-focusable` rule fails on CodeMirror's scroller (`tabindex="-1"`): it doesn't count a contenteditable as the scroller's focusable content.
- **2026-10-26** — **Unsaved text is guarded by a `beforeunload` handler that exists only while it differs.** The handler is added when the text first differs from what the editor opened with and removed when it matches again (compared with `Text.eq`, so an undo back to the start counts), so a page with nothing to lose stays eligible for the back/forward cache. Plan 3's Task 4 replaces it with the leave warnings of technical design §8.2.
- **2026-10-26** — **The theme is CSS variables, with no dark flag.** `app/editor/theme.ts` sets every color from the design tokens, so the theme switch needs no editor state. CodeMirror's `&light` and `&dark` selectors can't be used in an app theme, so the rules that must outweigh its base theme (the selection and the chosen completion) repeat its selectors without them, and the app theme comes later.
- **2026-10-26** — **The element announcer speaks when the kind changes.** `ElementAnnouncer.vue` is an `aria-live="polite"` region that reads the kind of element in words when the cursor moves from one kind to another, and clears on a blank line. Moving between two lines of the same kind (dialogue to dialogue) says nothing. It lives in `SourcePane`, so it exists wherever the editor is showing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Source panel keeps its tab stop.** `panel-source` is still focusable (F6 lands on it, as for the other panels), and Tab goes on from it into the editor. Making F6 go straight to the editor is left to the task that rewrites the pane's focus handling.
- **2026-10-26** — **`goto` waits for the Source pane.** It selects at once, shows the Source tab (`revealTab` from the pane that holds the focus on a wide screen, the phone tab otherwise), and after Vue draws the pane scrolls the range to the middle and focuses the editor. `revealTab` leaves a showing tab where it is.
- **2026-10-26** — **Editor tests insert text with `keyboard.insertText`,** not a synthetic paste: Firefox ignores the `clipboardData` of a constructed `ClipboardEvent`, so the paste did nothing there. The `beforeunload` tests read whether the handler cancels the event, which works in every browser; the closing-the-page check (a dialog with edits, none without) runs in Chromium and Firefox, and WebKit's dialog check passes but it doesn't close the page without one.
- **2026-10-26** — **The layout worker gets the whole text with each request,** after a 50 ms trailing debounce, not change sets (technical design §5.7). Copying even a 200 KB string costs well under a millisecond, and the worker's copy can never drift from the editor's. Each request is numbered; the client shows a reply only if it is newer than the one on screen, so a slow old layout can't replace a new one. A failed layout leaves the last one up and is logged to the console.
- **2026-10-26** — **The layout lives in module state, like the editor.** `useLayout()` (`app/editor/layout.ts`) starts the worker on first use, which is the status bar's, so the page is known while the Script tab is hidden and the layout survives the tab leaving. It keeps the template each request was made with, so pages are drawn with the template that laid them out.
- **2026-10-26** — **The page bar follows the block before a blank line.** `cursorElement` is null on a blank line, which would blank the page. `useLayout` appends its own update listener (`StateEffect.appendConfig`) that reads `blockIndexAt(doc, head)`: the cursor's block, or the last block before it, or -1 for the title page. `layout.index[i].page` is a page *number* (0 is the title page), so pages are found by `number`, never by position.
- **2026-10-26** — **Fit width is CSS `zoom`, measured with a probe.** The pages sit in a region that `zoom` scales; a hidden probe of `--cols` characters outside that region gives a page's real width (`getBoundingClientRect` on a zoomed element differs between engines). A `ResizeObserver` on the pane and the probe rescales when the pane resizes, the font loads, or the template changes. 100% removes the zoom. The scale has no upper bound.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Script view's host is the scroller and is a tab stop.** The panel doesn't scroll (as for the editor), and the host has `tabindex="0"`, so axe's `scrollable-region-focusable` passes and the arrow keys scroll it. The panel keeps its own tab stop for F6, so Tab goes on from the panel into the pages. `render-html` is given the host as its container, so the 2-second pause after the reader scrolls covers the host's own wheel, touch, and keys.
- **2026-10-26** — **`fontsReady` is capped at 3 seconds.** `render-html` waits for the promise with no limit, so the pane passes `Promise.race` of `document.fonts.load('12pt "Courier Prime"')` and a timer; a font that never settles draws the pages in the fallback monospace instead of leaving the pane blank.
- **2026-10-26** — **After each layout the pane reveals the cursor's block,** since the layout lags the text: the reveal that ran when the cursor moved had no line for a block that was only just typed. `render-html`'s pause after the reader scrolls keeps this from fighting a reader who scrolled away. The title page (block -1) has no line, so moving the cursor into it scrolls the host to the top.
- **2026-10-26** — **The worker is an ES-module worker built by Vite from `new Worker(new URL(...), { type: 'module' })`,** one same-origin file under `worker-src 'self'`; `security.spec.ts` stays green. Parsing and layout sit in `app/workers/compute.ts`, which the worker file calls, so a unit test covers the reply without a worker.
- **2026-10-26** — **The 120-page typing test types at the top of the script,** the worst place, since every later offset moves, one letter per 60 ms, and fails on any `longtask`.
- **2026-10-26** — **The editor's scroll position and focus survive a move.** A `display: none` holder forgets the scroller's `scrollTop`, so `SourcePane.vue` takes `view.scrollSnapshot()` and `view.hasFocus` before it parks the editor, and the next Source pane to mount dispatches the snapshot after `requestMeasure()` and refocuses if the editor had focus. A pane swap runs every `beforeUnmount` before any `onMounted`, so the one hand-off in module state is enough.
- **2026-10-26** — **`decodeFile` takes the BOM off the text and reports it.** Plan 2's final review found that a BOM kept in the editor's text lets the writer type before it, and the saved file then has U+FEFF mid-text. `decodeFile` returns `{ text, lineBreak, bom }` with a leading BOM removed (only the first; a second stays as text), and `encodeFile(text, lineBreak, bom)` puts it back. The `bom` argument is required, so a caller can't lose a BOM by leaving it out. This replaces the global constraint's "so a byte-order mark survives" in the text: the decoder still passes `ignoreBOM: true`, so the BOM reaches `decodeFile` and is removed there, not by `TextDecoder`. A lone CR is read as a line break too, as CodeMirror does.
- **2026-10-26** — **The saved text is one snapshot per project, and `source` wins.** `setSavedText` writes the row `saved:<projectId>` in `snapshots`, replacing the last one; `removeProject` deletes every snapshot with that `projectId` and the project's file handle. It does not set `savedHash` or `savedAt`: hashing is asynchronous, which can't happen inside an IndexedDB transaction, so the caller hashes and calls `updateProject`. `createProject` and `updateProject` derive `title`, `templateId`, and `meta` from `source` and ignore a patch that names them alongside it, because the technical design (§8.1) says `source` wins; a patch with no `source` can set a title. Updating a project that is gone does nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The writer keeps failed text pending.** `createWriter` runs one write at a time and writes the latest text; if a write fails, the text stays pending and `flush` rejects with the error. A timed write's failure is not rethrown, because nothing awaits it. The `write` callback reports it (the "unavailable" state of technical design §8.3), and the next change or `flush` tries again.
- **2026-10-26** — **`titleOf` reads the title's printable text; `fileNameFor` drops, not replaces.** The Title field is read from its styled runs, so `_**BIG FISH**_` is `BIG FISH`, and its lines are joined with a space. `fileNameFor` removes the unsafe characters, control characters, and leading and trailing dots and spaces (a dot-first name would be hidden, and Windows drops a trailing one), cuts at 120 characters by code point so an emoji isn't split, and falls back to "Untitled".
- **2026-10-26** — **A failed layout is a line in the Script pane, and a worker that can't be made is no crash.** `useLayout` wraps `new Worker` in a try/catch and sets `failed` when it throws or when the client reports an error; a layout that arrives clears `failed`. The Script pane shows "Couldn't lay out the pages." only while `failed` is set and nothing has been drawn: after a first layout, the pages on screen stay and the error goes to the console, as before. No test-only hook is in the production bundle: the e2e tests block the worker file with a route and replace `window.Worker` with a function that throws.
- **2026-10-26** — **The pressed zoom button's cue is a 1px line.** Every zoom button has a transparent 1px bottom border, and the pressed one's is `--heading`, so the cue moves nothing and matches the selected tab's line.
- **2026-10-26** — **One editor, a fresh state per script.** `useEditor().load(text)` is `view.setState(EditorState.create({ doc, extensions }))` with the extensions the editor was made from, so each opened script has its own undo history and the compartments start from their initial values (Plan 4's settings, once they exist, are applied again after a `load`). `setState` tells no update listener and drops configuration added with `appendConfig`, so the session keeps its own listener set (`onState`), called after every change to the text or the selection and after `load`; the layout's cursor tracking uses it. The scroller goes back to the top. Nothing replaces text that isn't kept: `useScript().open` flushes the current script's recovery copy first, and if that fails it throws and the editor keeps its text.
- **2026-10-26** — **What a first launch does with the editor.** The editor starts on the sample's text at once, so a failed or slow database leaves a usable editor. When the database answers, the most recent script replaces that text, unless the text has been typed in meanwhile: then it is saved as a script of its own (no `sample`), and stays open. The same rule adopts text typed while IndexedDB was unavailable when it comes back. A returning visitor can see the sample for an instant before their script loads.
- **2026-10-26** — **The recovery copy's state.** Each launch reads `navigator.storage.persisted()` and whether Dexie opens. `persist()` is asked for when the first script is created and never awaited, since Firefox answers with a prompt that may wait for ever; the state is read again when the answer comes. A failed write sets "unavailable" without a second check, since the failure is the answer. It clears when a write succeeds (and the state is read again), or when a retry finds the text already written and the database open. Retries: the writer keeps failed text pending, so the next change writes it; a 30 s timer calls `flush` while the state is unavailable, or runs the launch again when there is no script open (a Dexie database that failed to open is closed first, or it stays failed). The warning "may be cleared" is dismissed in memory, for the visit.
- **2026-10-26** — **A closing page leaves its pending text in `localStorage` as well.** Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit start an IndexedDB write in `pagehide` but close the connection before it commits, so a reload within 750 ms of typing lost the last keystrokes even with a flush. On `pagehide` and when the page is hidden, the pending text is put in `drafter:unwritten` (id, text, time), synchronously; a write that finishes with no newer text removes it; the next launch writes it to its script if the script's stored copy is older, then removes it. It is one script's text, for a moment, and `localStorage` that is blocked or full is skipped. The recovery-copy write also became a single `put` of the project the session holds (`writeSource`) instead of `updateProject`'s read and write, so the flush has one request to make. Anything else that changes a stored project (Task 7's saved hash) has to update the session's `project` too, or the next write overwrites it. *(Superseded 2026-10-26 by "One stash for each script", which keys the stash by script, and by "The held project and the database change together", where `writeSource` merges into a fresh read inside its transaction instead of being a single `put`.)*
- **2026-10-26** — **"Unsaved changes" before a file exists.** `dirty` compares the editor's text with a baseline: the saved snapshot when the project has a `savedHash` (or its own text, when that hashes to it; unknown means changed), the sample's text for the screenplay sample, and the empty text for anything else. So a script that never had a file counts as changed from its first character, and still does after a reload, as technical design §8.2 asks; a new script that starts from a template will need a baseline of its own (Task 5). The leave warning is registered while `dirty` and the script isn't a sample; if nothing can keep the edits (the recovery copy is unavailable), a sample's edits count too. *(Superseded 2026-10-26 by "A new script has a baseline": the empty text is the baseline only for a script with neither a `baseline` nor a `sample`, not "anything else".)*
- **2026-10-26** — **`bom` on a project.** `Project.bom?: boolean`, unindexed, so no new database version. Task 8 sets it from `decodeFile`'s result when it opens a file; the sample and new scripts have none.
- **2026-10-26** — **`relint()` calls cm-fountain's `relint(view)`.** A bare `forceLinting` doesn't re-run the source. The remembered view is set by a plugin that never lets go, since the one editor lives for the visit and `setState` makes the plugin again on it.
- **2026-10-26** — **One tab edits a script, by Web Lock.** The tab that opens project `<id>` requests `drafter:project:<id>` with `ifAvailable: true` and holds it (a pending promise) until it opens another script or goes; a tab that doesn't get it shows the script read-only. Read-only is a `readOnly` compartment (`EditorState.readOnly` and `EditorView.editable`); `load` reapplies it, since `setState` resets the compartments. A read-only tab has no writer, doesn't stash on `pagehide` or hidden, doesn't restore a stash at launch (the stash belongs to the holder, which clears it), and doesn't ask before closing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The handover, in order.** The asking tab broadcasts `{ type: 'release', id }` and queues a normal lock request, with a 10 s timeout. The holder turns read-only first (so nothing is typed after the flush), flushes its recovery copy, then releases the lock; if the flush fails it turns editable again and keeps the lock, and the asker says so. The asker then reloads the project from IndexedDB, applies the localStorage stash only if the stash is for this script and `at` is strictly later than the project's `updatedAt` (the holder's flush stamps `updatedAt` later than any stash it made), and only then turns editable. Closing the holder's tab releases the lock, but the other tab stays read-only until it is reloaded or asks.
- **2026-10-26** — **No guard without Web Locks.** Web Locks and `BroadcastChannel` are feature-detected together. Without them every tab edits and writes, as before Task 5; there is no fallback such as a localStorage heartbeat. Two tabs launching on a first visit, before any script exists, can each create a sample script, since there is nothing to lock yet.
- **2026-10-26** — **The recovery warnings wait for the first check.** `recovery` starts as `'not-persistent'` only as a placeholder, so `warning` is null until `ready` (the first project is open and the storage has been checked, or the database failed). Without that, every launch flashed a false "may clear your recovery copy" banner that a screen reader announced. "Unavailable" is `role="alert"` (it matters most and is inserted dynamically); "may be cleared" stays `role="status"`. *(Superseded 2026-10-26 by "The warnings sit in live regions that are always there", for how the roles are applied: "unavailable" is no longer a node that is inserted as an alert.)*
- **2026-10-26** — **One stash for each script.** The localStorage stash is keyed `drafter:unwritten:<id>`, not a single key, so two tabs on two scripts never overwrite each other's. A launch goes through every stash: it writes one that is newer than its script's stored copy (if no other tab holds the script), and clears one for a script that is gone or whose stored copy is newer. A tab taking a script over reads only that script's stash.
- **2026-10-26** — **A write for a script that isn't open throws.** `persist` used to return quietly when the open script had changed, which told the writer its text was written. It throws now, so the text stays pending; `open` flushes first, so nothing a person typed reaches that path.
- **2026-10-26** — **A switch holds both locks until the new script is shown.** `guardControl.claim(id)` requests the new script's lock without letting go of the old one and remembers the old one; `finish()` releases it once the new script is in the editor. The script session sets the editor read-only from the first flush of a switch until then, so the old script is never editable without its lock, nobody else can take it while text could still be typed, and a script held elsewhere opens read-only with the old lock released only afterwards. Opening the script that is already open does nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The stash follows the writer, not the guard.** `flushQuietly` leaves the pending text in the stash whenever the tab has a writer. A tab handing a script over is read-only for the length of its flush but still has the writer, so closing it then still leaves its text; a tab that opened a script read-only has no writer and leaves nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **Anything that dispatches checks `readOnly`.** `@codemirror/lint` doesn't, so the lint action returns when `view.state.readOnly`. cm-fountain's commands already did.
- **2026-10-26** — **The read-only bar speaks through a status region that is always there.** A `role="status"` element that a `v-if` inserts may not be announced, so the paragraph is in the page from the start and only its text changes; the button sits beside it.
- **2026-10-26** — **The scripts menu is a disclosure, not an ARIA menu.** The toolbar's Open button has `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, and the panel is a named group of ordinary buttons, in the Tab order, with Escape to close and focus back on Open. A menu role would promise arrow-key navigation, which Plan 4's command menus will bring along with the app menu. The panel closes when focus leaves it or the page is clicked elsewhere. It is placed under the pressed button with `position: fixed`, measured from that button: Safari doesn't focus a button on a click, so the focused element can't be used.
- **2026-10-26** — **Menu order.** **New screenplay** (the default template) comes first, **New stage play** second; Plan 4 adds **New Markdown document** last. **Open a file…** joins the menu with Task 8, after the new items.
- **2026-10-26** — **A new script's text, with a date from the local calendar.** `newScriptText(kind, today)` writes the month name itself (no locale formatting, so the same everywhere) from `today`'s local year, month, and day. The screenplay's cursor is at the end; the stage play's is on the blank line before the metadata block, which is fixed text (`/* drafter`, `{"v":1,"template":"stage-play-standard"}`, `*/`) and ends the text with a line break.
- **2026-10-26** — **A new script has a baseline.** `Project.baseline?: string` (unindexed, no migration) holds the text a new script started from. `baselineOf` uses it while the script has no `savedHash`, so an untouched new script is not changed, leaving it asks nothing, and the baseline survives a reload. A script with neither a baseline nor a sample compares against empty text, as before. Task 7 sets `savedHash` on the first save, and the saved snapshot takes over.
- **2026-10-26** — **Samples are scripts of their own, kept out of the list.** Opening a sample reopens this browser's project for it (the first visit's screenplay, or one made on first use for the stage play), so edits to a sample are kept. The "In this browser" list shows only scripts that aren't samples; the samples have their own two items, marked as current when open. Reset sample (Plan 4) restores their text.
- **2026-10-26** — **Every switch is one path.** `open`, `newScript`, `openSample`, and removing the open script all go through `switchTo`: the editor is read-only from the first flush until the new script is shown, a failed flush stops it with nothing changed, the new lock is taken (or found held, opening read-only), the script is attached, and only then is the old lock let go. A new script is created after the flush, so a failure leaves no half-made script open.
- **2026-10-26** — **Removing a script.** The panel asks in a modal `<dialog>` that says the file isn't touched (and, for a script never saved to a file, that this deletes the only copy). Removing the open script first writes its pending text, removes it, then opens the most recent other script that isn't a sample, or the screenplay sample (made if it isn't there). The writer is discarded (`Writer.discard()`: it writes nothing more, even if a write in flight fails), so nothing brings the script back. Removing a script that isn't open takes its lock for the moment (`whileFree`); a script another tab holds, or that this tab only shows read-only, is not removed and the panel says so after the writer asks. The stash for a removed script is cleared.
- **2026-10-26** — **A new script left untouched stays in the list.** Switching away from an untouched new script keeps it as a script (Untitled Screenplay). Removing it is the way to tidy up; removing untouched ones automatically is left for later.
- **2026-10-26** — **A switch ignores requests made while another runs.** `switchTo` returns whether it switched and does nothing if one is under way; switches are counted (`switches`), and the editor is read-only while the count is above zero, so one ending can't make it editable under another. A double click on **New screenplay** is one script. The menu's items are disabled while `busy`. Removing the open script while a switch runs fails with a message rather than reporting it removed.
- **2026-10-26** — **A handover lets go of the lock it was asked about.** `handOver` captures the lock and script id when it starts and releases that one, and only clears the tab's held-lock record if it is still for that script, so a switch that finishes its claim during the flush keeps its new lock.
- **2026-10-26** — **The removal dialog also warns of unsaved changes.** For a script with a file, the dialog says changes since the last save are lost when the stored text differs from the saved text (`useScript().changed`); a script never saved keeps its own sentence.
- **2026-10-26** — **`FileAccess` is the only way to a file.** `storage/files.ts` defines it and `browserFiles`; `useFiles()` returns the one in use and `setFiles()` replaces it (a test's in-memory version, a desktop build's). A `FileRef` is whatever the access hands back and can be structured-cloned: the browser's is the `FileSystemFileHandle`, kept in the `handles` table as `{ id, ref }` and pointed to by the project's `fsHandleId`. `canSaveInPlace` is read at each call, so removing the pickers (as the download tests do) is seen at once. `pickToOpen` and `fromDrop` are in the browser version already (with the hidden file input as the fallback) for Task 8, which tests them. Text is written as UTF-8 bytes from `TextEncoder`, which keeps a U+FEFF as the three BOM bytes.
- **2026-10-26** — **What Save does.** On Chromium, Save writes to the file the script is bound to; a sample, a script with no file, a handle that is gone, or Save As asks with `pickToSave`, suggesting the script's file name, else `fileNameFor(title)`. If the bound file is gone (`FileGoneError`) it asks once instead. A write refused for permission, or any other failure, sets a message in the status bar (`role="alert"`) and rejects; nothing about the script changes. Without the API, Save and Save As download the whole script under the script's file name, else the title's, and then count as saved. The text written is read after the picker returns, as `encodeFile(text, lineBreak, bom)` of the project's own line break and BOM. Save requests queue, so two at once are a question and then a write in place.
- **2026-10-26** — **A save is recorded in the order hash, saved text, project.** `useScript().saved` hashes the text, writes the saved snapshot, then updates the project (`savedHash`, `savedAt`, `fileName`, `fsHandleId`) and the session's held copy together, and makes the text the baseline for "changed". The file has the text whatever happens next, so a failure to record it (the database being down) is swallowed: the recovery warning already says so.
- **2026-10-26** — **The held project and the database change together.** The recovery write and a save's bookkeeping go through one queue (`exclusive`), each starting from the copy the session holds when it runs; a save puts the new copy in the session before the database (`patch`). `writeSource` also merges into a fresh read inside its transaction, taking only `source`, `title`, `templateId`, `meta`, and `updatedAt` from the new text, so what another tab recorded isn't overwritten. That second part is for a save from a read-only tab: it updates the stored project's saved fields only (`updateProject`), never its text, and the holder's next write keeps them.
- **2026-10-26** — **Read-only tabs may save; samples become a new script.** Save and Save As don't change the editor's text, so a read-only tab writes the text it shows. Saving a sample creates a project (text, `fileName`, `savedHash`, `savedAt`, handle) through `switchTo` with `keep`: the editor keeps its state, history and cursor, takes the new script's lock, and writes into it from then on. The sample project is flushed like any script being left and is otherwise unchanged: it stays a sample with the text it had. *(Superseded 2026-10-26 by "Ruling 4: a read-only tab saves and opens nothing", for the claim that a read-only tab writes the text it shows; a sample saved still becomes a new script as described.)*
- **2026-10-26** — **The keys.** `Mod+S` and `Mod+Shift+S` are caught by a capture-phase `keydown` listener on the window (installed by the page) that prevents the default, stops the event, and ignores key repeat; the editor's keymap has the same two keys calling the same functions through `fileKeys`, in `extensions.ts`, since the editor can't import the save module. Plan 4's command registry takes over both and lists them.
- **2026-10-26** — **Status bar and tab.** The first item reads "Saved just now" (under a minute), "Saved N min ago" (to an hour, refreshed every 30 s), or "Saved at 3:42 PM" for a script saved to a file with no changes since; "Unsaved changes" once it has some. A script never saved keeps "Not saved to a file yet", and a sample its own line. A hidden `role="status"` says "Saved" when a save lands. The tab title is the title page's Title (else Untitled), " — Drafter", with "• " in front while there are changes.
- **2026-10-26** — **A save belongs to the script it started on.** `save()` takes the open script's id when it starts and passes it to `saved()`. If another script is open when the write ends, the record (hash, saved text, file name, handle) goes to that id with `updateProject`, and the open script's baseline and held copy are left alone; a sample saved this way becomes a script of its own without being opened. A Save asked for during a switch waits for it, then saves the script that is open.
- **2026-10-26** — **A save is announced from the save.** `saveAnnouncement` says "Saved", then is emptied two seconds later, so the next save changes the live region again. Opening a saved script or reloading says nothing, because nothing watches the script's `savedAt`. A refused write is aborted (`abort()`) rather than closed, so its own error comes through, and the message points to Save As.
- **2026-10-26** — **Opening a file goes through `switchTo`.** `openFile` makes a project (`openedFile`) and switches to it, so the flush, the tab guard, and "one switch at a time" all apply; a request made while a switch is under way is refused with "Another script is still opening". The project records `lineBreak`, `bom`, `fileName`, `fsHandleId` (when there is a handle), `savedHash` of the opened text with the saved snapshot, and `baseline` (the same text), so it opens clean. `savedAt` is the time it was opened: the text is what the file holds, and the status bar says "Saved just now" rather than showing the file's modified time, which it would print as a time of day with no date.
- **2026-10-26** — **Not UTF-8 means not opened.** Decoding with replacement turns a Latin-1 file's characters into U+FFFD and the next save would write that over the original. `decodeFile(bytes, { strict: true })` decodes with `fatal: true` and throws `NotUtf8Error`; `openFile` refuses with a plain message. Also refused: over 5 MB (5 MB itself is allowed), and a NUL byte in the first 8 KB. There is no extension check: a `.md` of prose is as good a script as a `.txt`.
- **2026-10-26** — **The same file is the same script.** Handles of the stored projects are compared with the picked file's through `FileAccess.sameFile` (`isSameEntry`); a match opens that project, with the recovery copy it holds (not the file's current text: a file changed on disk since is Plan 4's change check). A file with no handle (input, or a drop without `getAsFileSystemHandle`) can't be matched.
- **2026-10-26** — **The same-title question (§9.6) is asked only where saving downloads,** and never matches a sample. Titles compare trimmed and case-insensitive. Replace opens the matching script and swaps its whole text in one transaction (`input.replace`), so one undo brings the old text back, and records the file as that script's saved text; it is refused when another tab has the script. The old text isn't kept past the session's undo history, as the brief ruled.
- **2026-10-26** — **A title-less script is called after its file.** `derive` takes the project's `fileName` as the fallback, so the menu list and the tab title say "Rough notes" instead of "Untitled" for `Rough notes.txt`. `fromDrop` asks for the file and the handle at once, before anything is awaited (a dropped item goes stale when the handler returns), and uses the file when there is no handle.
- **2026-10-26** — **Ruling 4: a read-only tab saves and opens nothing.** Save, Save As, Open a file, New screenplay, and New stage play are disabled (the toolbar's Save with `aria-disabled`, so it can still say why), a hint says to choose "Edit here instead" first, and the keys and drops are refused with the same hint in the status bar. Switching to a script that already exists, and samples, stay available so a writer can leave. `can't save from a read-only tab` replaces Task 7's `saves in a read-only tab`.
- **2026-10-26** — **A file dropped on the editor is the page's, not CodeMirror's.** CodeMirror's own `drop` handler reads a dropped file's text and inserts it at the pointer, and it runs before `DropZone.vue`'s window listener, so a file dropped on the text was pasted into the open script and also opened as its own. `editorExtensions` adds `EditorView.domEventHandlers({ drop })` that returns true when the drag carries `Files`, which stops CodeMirror's handler and leaves the event for the window; text drags have no `Files` type and drop in as before. The end-to-end test drops on `.cm-content`, where the bug was (the old helper dropped on `document.body`), in Chromium and Firefox.
- **2026-10-26** — **Switching to a script applies a closed tab's stash.** `switchTo` claims the lock first; if the tab may edit, `withStash` writes the script's stash when it is newer than the stored copy and differs from it, then clears it (the rule `takeOver` already used, now one function). A tab that only shows the script leaves the stash for the holder. Before this, a tab that closed right after typing left the newest text in the stash and a second tab that opened the script from the menu showed the old stored text. If the write fails the stash stays and the stored copy is shown.
- **2026-10-26** — **"Edit here instead" is for the script shown when it was pressed.** `editHere` remembers the id, and once the lock is granted it lets go and returns if another script is shown by then; `takeOver(id)` loads that id, not whatever is current. While it waits (`taking`), the scripts menu doesn't switch, so a script is not taken over under a switch to another.
- **2026-10-26** — **A save writes, and is recorded against, the script it started on.** After the picker returns, `save.ts` checks that script is still open; if not, it writes that script's stored text (opening the other flushed it) or, if it is gone, the text the save started with, and never reads the editor of another script. A sample's save whose turn in `switchTo` comes after another switch has changed the open script is recorded with `savedElsewhere` (a script of its own, not opened), so "the editor's own state" is never given to a script it isn't the text of.
- **2026-10-26** — **The warnings sit in live regions that are always there.** `StorageWarning.vue` has a `role="status"` region for "may be cleared" and a `role="alert"` region for "unavailable", both rendered from the first paint with only their contents under `v-if`. A region whose role changes is not announced, and one a `v-if` inserts may not be either; two regions avoid both.
- **2026-10-26** — **A message shown again is heard again.** Vue draws only the last value of a ref set twice in one tick, so `openError` cleared at the start of `openFile` and set to the same text a moment later never left the page, and a screen reader said nothing the second time. `say(ref, message)` (`storage/say.ts`) clears the ref, waits for the empty text to be drawn, and sets the message; `openError`, `saveError`, and `readOnlyHint` are set through it.
- **2026-10-26** — **The check is `storage/check.ts`; the page shows it in `FileBar` and `BothChangedDialog`.** `compareVersions({ saved, file, browser })` gives `same`, `file-changed`, `browser-changed`, or `both-changed`: a side changed when its hash differs from the project's `savedHash`. A file and a browser that changed to the *same* text are `same`, since nothing can be lost between them; the check then records that text as saved (`saved()`), or the next edit would look like both changed. The file's hash is of its decoded text (BOM off, `\n` line breaks), the same text `savedHash` is of, so a sync that only rewrote line breaks is not a change. A file that is not UTF-8 text is never compared (the `not-text` bar, with Save As and Edit this browser's copy).
- **2026-10-26** — **The check runs in the save queue.** `inTurn` moved to `storage/turn.ts`, so a check on focus waits for a save in progress and never reads the file between the write and the record of it. Save's own check (`beforeSave`) runs inside its turn, asks for permission (a click is behind it), and stops the save when the file changed: the file's text is loaded, or the dialog opens. After either, the writer presses Save again knowing what is there. A gone file is left to Save's existing path, which asks where to save, but the bar shows too. Checks on focus and on opening join one waiting check, not one already reading, so a change made after a read began is seen. A tab that only shows a script (read-only) never checks; the holder does. If the text or the script changes while the file is read, the check starts again (three tries).
- **2026-10-26** — **`file-changed` goes through `replaceWith`.** The file's text replaces the editor's as one transaction (`input.replace`), so Mod+Z brings the previous text back, and the script is recorded as saved to the file (its line break and BOM too). "Updated from The Call.fountain." is a visible toast for eight seconds and a live region that is always in the page.
- **2026-10-26** — **`both-changed` keeps both.** **Open the file** keeps this browser's text as a new script titled `<Title> (this browser's copy)`, then replaces the editor's text with the file's (undoable). **Keep this browser's version** keeps the file's text as `<Title> (from the file)`, then records the file's text as the one seen, so the editor shows the changes since and Save writes them. The copy is made first; if that fails, nothing is replaced. Both are named for this script's title, so the names are the ones the writer expects. A script is called by its `Title:`, so a copy is named by rewriting that field in its text (`retitle`; a title page or a Title is added if there is none). The copy is not bound to a file. Escape answers neither: nothing changes, and the next check asks again (a focus while the question is open does not ask twice).
- **2026-10-26** — **Without permission a script opens read-only.** When the handle's permission is not `granted`, `fileLocked` (in the script session, with the guard and a switch in progress) turns the editor read-only, and the bar says "This script is saved in The Call.fountain." with **Check for changes**, which asks for permission and checks in one click. The recovery copy is what is shown; the file is not read. Showing another script clears the lock, and its own check sets it again if needed. Save asks for permission too, being a click.
- **2026-10-26** — **A file that is gone.** The bar says "Drafter can't find The Call.fountain. It may have been moved or deleted.", and the editor stays editable. **Save As** is the ordinary Save As. **Edit this browser's copy** lets go of the handle (`unbindFile`; the file and `fileName` are untouched), and from then on Save asks where. The bar goes when a check finds the file, or the script has a different file. The prompts use straight apostrophes, as the brief wrote them, so the names a test or a writer looks for are the same everywhere.
- **2026-10-26** — **The file check watches primitives, and Save.** `installFileCheck` watches the ready flag, the script's id, its handle id, and read-only as separate sources, so the recovery copy replacing `project.value` doesn't trigger it; it also runs after a Save or Save As that wrote (a Save As keeps the handle id, so nothing else would clear a stale "gone" bar).
- **2026-10-26** — **The both-changed copy is made from the text at the moment of the answer.** If it differs from what was compared (keystrokes already in flight when the dialog opened), nothing is replaced and the check starts again, so the question is asked about the text that would be lost. A save whose check runs out of its three attempts throws `CheckUnsettledError` and the status bar says nothing was saved; the background check just stops. Copies carry the line break of the version they hold (the file's, or the script's), and the file's copy its BOM.
- **2026-10-26** — **Export is a worker and the file seam.** `workers/export.worker.ts` parses, paginates, and calls `renderPdf` or `renderText` (`export-compute.ts`, which the tests drive without a worker); `storage/export-client.ts` keeps one worker for the visit, so the fonts it fetched stay in it, and makes it again if it dies. The PDF's bytes are transferred, not copied. `storage/export.ts` (`exportScript(kind)`) saves only through `useFiles()` (`pickToSave`, `write`, `download`).
- **2026-10-26** — **The save picker opens before the render.** `showSaveFilePicker` needs the click that started the export, which a long render could outlast; a cancelled picker then costs no render. The text is read when the render starts. Where `canSaveInPlace` is false the export downloads.
- **2026-10-26** — **The fonts come from the app's own origin.** The worker fetches the four Courier Prime files from `/fonts/courier-prime/` (the files `scripts/sync-assets.mjs` copies, so the page, the PDF, and the command line share their metrics), which `connect-src 'self'` allows; the policy is unchanged. A failed fetch is an error toast, and the next export tries again. Plain text never loads them.
- **2026-10-26** — **Export results are toasts that never throw.** `exportScript` resolves `'saved'` or `'cancelled'` (a cancelled picker, or a failure), sets `exportNotice` ("Exported The Call.pdf.") or `exportError` ("Couldn’t export The Call.pdf: …"), shown in a status and an alert region that stay in the page, and clears them after eight seconds. A second export asked for while one is under way joins it.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Export button is a menu for now.** The command registry and the app menu come with Plan 4's other tasks; until then `ExportMenu.vue` (a small panel like the Open menu: Escape closes and returns focus, focus leaving closes) calls `exportScript`. A Markdown document can't be exported yet; Markdown documents are a later task. The file is named `fileNameFor(title, '.pdf' | '.txt')` from the title as the editor shows it.
- **2026-10-26** — **An export is of one script, taken in one step.** It waits for a script switch under way (`whenIdle`, shared with Save, in `turn.ts`), then takes the text, the template, and the title together, so the file's name and its pages can't come from different scripts; the render uses that text even if the editor changes while the picker is open. A script that is empty or only blank lines is refused before any picker, with the error toast "Nothing to export yet.", since it would be a 0-byte file or a page-less PDF.
- **2026-10-26** — **Exports run one at a time.** A second request of the same kind as the last one is joined; one of the other kind waits for it, so the worker's replies are matched in order. A queued export's save picker opens after the first finishes and may be refused by the browser for want of a fresh click; the toast then says so.
- **2026-10-26** — **Export is allowed in a read-only tab.** It writes only to a file the writer picks (or downloads), never to the script's own file or record, and exports what the tab shows.
- **2026-10-26** — **One handler per key: the registry's `global` commands.** A command with `global: true` has its `keys` bound by one capture listener on the window (`installGlobalKeys`), which prevents the default and stops the event, so the browser's Save Page never opens and the editor's keymap never sees the key. That is File (New screenplay, Open, Save, Save As) and the palette. Every other command's keys belong to CodeMirror's keymap, which already has them; the registry lists them for the palette and Plan 5's shortcuts page. `Command` gained the optional `global` for this. Plan 3's second binding of Mod-s and Mod-Shift-s in the editor's keymap (and `fileKeys`) is gone, and `installSaveKeys` became `watchSaveError`. A held key runs a command once. A key whose command is `enabled() === false` is still the app's (default prevented, nothing run).
- **2026-10-26** — **Keys are matched by `event.key`, and by `event.code` with Alt held.** Option+N on a Mac types a dead key, so a letter or digit pressed with Alt is read from the physical key; every other key from its character, so other keyboard layouts get the character they typed. Modifiers must be exactly those the key names. `Mod` is Command on Apple platforms and Control elsewhere; the platform comes from `navigator.platform` (else `userAgentData.platform`), the one place the app looks at the platform, for the key names only.
- **2026-10-26** — **Nuxt UI's palette draws its labels with `v-html`; Drafter's palette takes that over.** `UCommandPalette` highlights the match by building HTML from the item's text (`labelHtml`) and rendering it with `v-html`, and a scene heading is the writer's text. Every item is drawn through the `item-label` slot with Vue's interpolation, so a heading such as `INT. <img src=x onerror=alert(1)> - DAY` is text (an e2e test checks that no `img` exists), at the cost of the match highlight. Its fuzzy search looks at each item's label only (the scene's line number is not searched, and the default limit of twelve results is raised so a long script's scenes all show).
- **2026-10-26** — **The palette closes first, then the command runs.** Choosing an item closes the palette and runs the command on the next tick, because the dialog traps focus while it is open and a command that moves focus (the search panel, a dialog, the editor) would lose it. Focus then goes where the command put it; if the command left it nowhere, or the palette was closed with Escape, it goes back to the element that had it when the palette opened (the toolbar button it was opened from, since Safari doesn't focus a button on a click). "Go to scene…" and "Go to line…" change what the open palette lists and keep it open. `Mod+K` toggles.
- **2026-10-26** — **Commands that edit work on the editor through `goto`, and hide in a read-only tab.** A command that changes or moves in the text first calls the session's `goto` at the cursor, which shows the Source tab and focuses the editor, and runs after that focus. Commands whose `enabled()` is false are not listed, and `enabled` is asked each time the palette opens: the Fountain commands, Undo, and Redo need an editable editor; New needs a tab that can start a script. Open and Save stay listed in a read-only tab so they can say why they won't (the status bar's hint).
- **2026-10-26** — **"Remove from this browser" in the palette removes the open script, after asking.** The Scripts menu keeps its own question for the scripts in its list; the palette's command asks the same question about the open script in `RemoveDialog.vue` (a modal `<dialog>`, with the same wording). It is not offered for a sample. A script open in another tab can't be removed, and the dialog says so.
- **2026-10-26** — **The palette puts right two lapses of Nuxt UI's list while it is open.** After some searches (seen on a phone, and only with the editor's text changed) no row is highlighted, so Enter would choose nothing: a `MutationObserver` on the palette sends the list the first ArrowDown it takes to mean "the first row" when focus is in the input and no row is highlighted. The input also keeps `aria-activedescendant` pointing at a row a search has removed, which axe reports, so the stale pointer is dropped. An empty result is a disabled `role="option"` saying "Nothing matches", because a listbox with no option fails axe's `aria-required-children`. The modal has no open/close animation (`transition` off): an Escape followed at once by Mod+K otherwise found the old dialog still closing, and the input unfocused.
- **2026-10-26** — **Settings are a row each in the `settings` table, read once and checked.** `createSettings()` (`app/settings/settings.ts`) reads the table on first use and `useSettings()` is the one the app shares; a test makes its own to stand for a new visit. A row whose key isn't a setting (including inherited names such as `toString`) or whose value the setting can't hold (a text size that isn't one of 0.9, 1, 1.15, 1.3, 1.5; a non-boolean switch) is ignored, and `set` ignores the same. A change a writer makes while the table is still being read is not overwritten by the read. If the table can't be read or written, the defaults and the change stand for the visit and `set` still resolves: settings are a convenience, and a failed write is not worth interrupting writing for. No change to the database's schema.
- **2026-10-26** — **Settings reach the editor through `applyEditorSettings`, run on every change and after every `load`.** `load` gives the editor a new state, so the spellcheck and line-number compartments return to their first values; `load` now calls `applyEditorSettings` (spellcheck, line numbers, and a `relint` for Strict Fountain) after it puts read-only back. Strict Fountain is not a compartment: `fountain()`'s `lintOptions` reads the setting at each lint, and a change calls `relint`. The page's `applySettings()` starts only after the table has been read, so the defaults never undo a first paint.
- **2026-10-26** — **Match system appearance sets the color mode's preference to `system`, and choosing a theme by hand turns it off.** The theme stays in localStorage (Plan 1), so the first paint has no flash, and the setting's row is only the switch. Turning the setting on remembers the explicit theme the writer had; turning it off puts that one back. After a reload, with the setting on, there is no remembered theme (the preference in localStorage is `system`), so turning it off keeps the appearance showing then. The toolbar's theme button and the Switch theme command set an explicit preference, and with the setting on that is the writer choosing a theme, so the setting turns off rather than the click doing nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The text size is `--text-scale` on the root.** The Source pane's font size and line height multiply by it (the editor measures its own lines, and `requestMeasure` is asked after a change). The Script pane's zoom multiplies by it at 100%, with CSS `zoom`, which does not change the layout, so pagination is the same at every size; Fit width fills the pane whatever the size. A returning writer sees the normal size for the moment before the table is read (no localStorage copy, unlike the theme).
- **2026-10-26** — **Line numbers are off to begin with (the maintainer's decision), in the leftmost gutter, and one setting has four ways to reach it.** The Source pane's bar has a "Line numbers" button (`aria-pressed`), the palette has **Show line numbers** and **Hide line numbers** (only the one that applies is listed, since a command's title is fixed), and the Settings dialog has the switch. The numbers are the 1-based lines that Go to line and the Problems pane use.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Settings dialog is a native modal `<dialog>` with a native checkbox (`role="switch"`) for each switch and a `<select>` for the text size.** Changes apply at once, and **Done** only closes it; the first control takes focus when it opens, and Escape closes it and gives focus back. It opens from the **Settings…** command (File, `Mod-,`, bound on the whole page so a browser that has its own meaning for the key doesn't act on it). The toolbar has no button for it yet; the palette and the key are the ways in.
- **2026-10-26** — **`DOCUMENT_TYPES` is the one list, and an entry has a `kind`.** `editor/template.ts` exports the list in the maintainer's order: Screenplay, Stage play, then (Phase 3) other Fountain templates, then Markdown (Task 6). Each entry is `{ kind: 'fountain', id, name, newKind, newTitle, newIcon, newKeys? }`; `DocumentType` is a union of one member today, and Task 6 adds the member for the Markdown document (a project kind, not a template) and its entry at the end. The toolbar chip's menu, the Scripts menu's New items, and the palette's New commands are all `DOCUMENT_TYPES.map(…)`, so they can't disagree. New screenplay keeps Mod+Alt+N (`newKeys`); the Scripts menu's first New item takes the focus when it opens.
- **2026-10-26** — **`useTemplate` is in `editor/use-template.ts`, not `template.ts`.** `template.ts` is plain data and functions that storage (`projects.ts`) and the unit tests import in Node; the switcher needs the editor session, so it lives apart, and `template.ts` doesn't import the session (which imports it).
- **2026-10-26** — **Choosing a template writes `template` through `setMeta` and keeps the rest of the block.** `choose(id)` sets `{ ...doc.meta, template: id }`, so other settings (`distinctNames`) stay. Choosing Screenplay again from a stage play writes `"template":"us-screenplay"` rather than deleting the key or the block: it's explicit, and a script that has been through the chip says what it is. Choosing what the script already is (the screenplay with no block, or the name already in the block) does nothing; an unknown name in the block is replaced even by the screenplay it falls back to. The change is `userEvent: 'input'` and `isolateHistory('full')`, so it is its own undo step and never joins typing. The line breaks are the editor's own, as for a lint fix.
- **2026-10-26** — **The chip's menu is off while `setMeta` can't write.** `setMeta` returns nothing for a script with an unclosed `/*`, and a read-only tab must not edit, so the items are disabled in both cases (the Problems pane already says what an unclosed boneyard is). The menu is a `UDropdownMenu` with checkbox items (Reka: `menuitemcheckbox`, focus lands on the checked item, Escape closes and returns focus to the chip). The chip's accessible name is "Template: Screenplay"; its visible text is just the name. It stays hidden below 1024px as before, where the status bar names the template; a phone switches template with the palette's **Template: …** commands (File group; only the template the script isn't in is listed, none in a read-only tab or with an unclosed `/*`), which run on the editor like the Fountain commands, so Mod+Z undoes them. Task 6 must keep them off for a Markdown project. The Scripts menu's New items make a different document and are no substitute.
- **2026-10-26** — **The session lints again whenever the template changes, from any cause.** `follow` calls `relint` (in a microtask: an update listener may not dispatch) when the template the text names differs from the session's, so Mod+Z, a typed block, and `load` get the stage play's turned-off rules (`heading-no-time`, `transition-not-last`) without waiting for the 300 ms an edit's lint is delayed by (a template change is also an edit, so the delayed lint would catch it; the relint is belt and braces, and its speed is not tested). The layout already re-requested on a template change.
- **2026-10-26** — **A choice from the chip puts focus in the editor.** Reka returns focus to the trigger when a menu closes; the chip's menu handles `closeAutoFocus` and, after a selection, prevents that and calls the session's `goto` at the cursor (Source tab shown, editor focused), so Mod+Z, an editor-only key, undoes the change. Escape and leaving the menu still return focus to the chip.
- **2026-10-26** — **Markdown is a kind of document, and its grammar is Lezer's.** A project has a `kind` (`'fountain'` or `'markdown'`; an unindexed field, so no migration, and a project without one is Fountain), and Markdown is an entry of `DOCUMENT_TYPES` of the other `kind`, not a Fountain template. The Source pane's `language` compartment holds `fountain()` for Fountain and `@codemirror/lang-markdown` (CommonMark, no HTML tag completion) for Markdown, so Fountain's keys, completion, folding, highlighting, and lint are off in a Markdown document because they are all in `fountain()`. Plan 2's "no Lezer grammar" rule is about Fountain, whose only parser is `@drafter/fountain`; a Markdown grammar is outside it, and `@drafter/fountain` still parses nothing but Fountain. The Markdown highlighting is Drafter's own `cm-md-*` classes read from the Lezer tree (the way `cm-f-*` are read from the Fountain parse), not a `HighlightStyle`, so the app needs no `@lezer/highlight` of its own.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Fountain parse stays in the editor's state for a Markdown document.** `parsedDoc` is outside the language compartment, so the session, the layout, and the title (all of which read it) need no second code path, and switching kind is one reconfigure that keeps the text, the history, and the cursor. The cost is an incremental `reparse` of Markdown text as Fountain on each edit, which nothing reads: the layout worker is skipped, the scenes, the problems, and the cursor's element are off for Markdown. A cheaper arrangement (no parse at all in Markdown) would mean every reader of `doc` also reads `kind`; it is left until profiling asks.
- **2026-10-26** — **Changing the kind is not an edit.** Making a Fountain script Markdown changes the project's `kind` and the editor's language and writes nothing to the text, so Mod+Z does not reverse it, and it asks first because Fountain's formatting, keys, and checks stop applying. Choosing Screenplay or Stage play in a Markdown document turns Fountain back on with the text kept (Stage play also writes its metadata block, as one undoable change, so undo takes the block back and the script stays Fountain). A sample is never made Markdown: it stays the example it is, and New Markdown document is the way in. A script bound to a file keeps its binding when its kind changes (the change unbinds nothing), but Save no longer writes into it: a `.fountain` file is not given Markdown, nor a `.md` file Fountain. When the bound file's extension is not the script's kind (`kindOfFileName`), Save acts as Save As (the download path too), suggesting the new kind's extension and the title or the old file's name, and the file it makes replaces the binding.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Preview is built, never parsed.** `renderMarkdown` turns the `mdast-util-from-markdown` tree into DOM with `createElement`, `createTextNode`, and `textContent`, and nothing else, so no HTML string from the writer's text is ever parsed by the browser (no `innerHTML`, no `v-html`). Raw HTML, block or inline, is shown as text. A link is drawn as its text with no `href` (no `javascript:` address can run, and the preview can't navigate the editor away); an image is drawn as `[Image: description]` (nothing is fetched, which the content security policy forbids anyway). Clickable links are deferred. The renderer reads its own small node type, not `@types/mdast`, so that package is not a dependency either, and `micromark`, which `mdast-util-from-markdown` drives, is listed there and not added on its own.
- **2026-10-26** — **A long Markdown document is parsed in a worker.** `mdast-util-from-markdown` takes about 2.5 s for 740,000 characters on this machine (measured; it is slower than linear), which on the main thread would freeze typing after every pause. Up to 10,000 characters the tree is made at once on the main thread (about 15 ms), so a short document's preview follows each key; beyond that a worker (`markdown.worker.ts`, the layout worker's pattern: one request per 250 ms pause, an older reply never replaces a newer one, positions stripped before the tree is sent) makes the tree and the word count while the pane says "Updating…". A browser without module workers parses on the main thread after the same pause.
- **2026-10-26** — **What a Markdown document is called, and what it exports.** Its title is its first heading (ATX or Setext, outside code fences, without emphasis or link markup), else the file's name; the title, the window's title, and the suggested file name (`.md`) all read the same line scan, which is not a parse because the recovery copy asks on every write. Export offers the Markdown file and plain text, and both are the text as it stands: Markdown is plain text, and a version stripped of its markup would lose what the writer wrote. The chip says `Document: Markdown (plain text)` to a screen reader where it says `Template: …` for a Fountain script, because Markdown has no template.
- **2026-10-26** — **Markdown's code is lazy.** `@codemirror/lang-markdown` (with the HTML, CSS, and JavaScript grammars it nests, about 70 KB gzipped) and `mdast-util-from-markdown` are `import()`ed the first time a document is Markdown, so a Fountain-only visitor downloads neither; a build puts them in their own chunks (`ATXHeading` and `KeyframeList` are in one, `codeFenced` in the other and in the worker, none in the entry or its preloads; an e2e test reads the responses to say so). The editor's `language` compartment starts empty for a Markdown document (plain text) and is reconfigured when the mode arrives, unless the editor has moved on meanwhile: every `load` and change of kind bumps an epoch the late arrival must still match, and the kind must still be Markdown, so a document turned back into Fountain before the chunk arrives never gets Markdown's mode (tested with the chunk held back). The preview shows "Preparing the preview…" until the parser has arrived.
- **2026-10-26** — **A change of kind that can't be stored is undone.** `script.setKind` changes the editor first, then stores the project's kind; if storing fails, the editor and the project go back to the old kind and the status bar says "Couldn’t change the document type. Nothing was changed." A load of another script clears the preview's tree and word count at once, so a long document never shows the last script's preview while its own is being parsed.
- **2026-10-26** — **The app menu is the mark, and every item is a command.** The Drafter mark in the toolbar is the trigger of a Nuxt UI `UDropdownMenu` (`AppMenu.vue`, labeled "Drafter menu"). Its items are made from the registry by id (`entry('save', …)`), so the label, the keys, and whether it is off come from the command, and there is no second copy of what an item does; the New items are `DOCUMENT_TYPES.map(…)` in the Scripts menu's order. The recent scripts are the one list that is not commands (they open a script by id, as the Scripts menu does), and the samples are commands (`open-sample-screenplay`, `open-sample-stage-play`, which the palette lists too). A command that is off shows disabled in the menu, where the palette hides it. The Export section lists only what the document kind exports (the PDF for Fountain, the Markdown file for Markdown, and plain text for both), because the other kind's item could never be on. Help (Keyboard shortcuts, Take the tour) arrives in Plan 5. The menu holds focus while it closes, so a command's own focus is lost to it; a chosen item that shows a script (a New item, a sample, a recent script, Revert to Saved) then puts the cursor in the editor, and any other leaves focus on the mark unless the command put it somewhere (a dialog).
- **2026-10-26** — **Export is three commands, and the toolbar's menu runs them.** `export-pdf`, `export-md`, and `export-txt` call `exportScript`; the PDF is on for a Fountain script, the Markdown file for a Markdown document, and plain text for both. The toolbar's Export menu still lists what applies to the kind, but its items run the commands, so the menu, the app menu, and the palette share one path.
- **2026-10-26** — **Revert to Saved is one change in the editor's history, and writes nothing.** `revertToSaved` replaces only the part of the text that differs from the text last saved (the session's baseline), so the cursor, the folds, and the scroll stay where they are, as a single transaction isolated from the typing before it (`isolateHistory`), so one undo brings every change back and redo reverts again. It is available while the script has changes (`dirty`), the file's saved text is known, the script's kind is still its file's (after a change of kind the saved text is the other kind's, and Save would act as Save As), and the editor is not locked (a tab that only shows the script, a switch under way, a file that can't be checked). A sample and a script never saved have no saved text, so there is nothing to revert. The message names the file, goes to a polite live region (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`) and to a toast that empties after eight seconds (`timedSay` in `storage/say.ts`, now shared with the export toast).
