- **2026-10-26** — **The palette's workarounds depend on Nuxt UI internals: re-check them on a Nuxt UI upgrade.** The `MutationObserver` that sends the list its first ArrowDown, the dropped stale `aria-activedescendant`, the disabled "Nothing matches" option, the `item-label` slot that avoids `v-html`, and the Fuse limit all rely on how `UCommandPalette` and Reka's list are built today, and on the palette's own class names. When `@nuxt/ui` or `reka-ui` is upgraded, run `palette.spec.ts` first, then check each workaround is still needed and still works (the plain-text scene heading test and the axe tests for the palette are the ones that would show a change).
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's id is a name, not free text.** `ScriptModule.id` must be lowercase letters and digits joined by single hyphens (`script-doctor`), as must a tab's `id`. The id is a settings key (`module:<id>:<key>`), an element id, and part of the tab's id (`module-<id>--<tab>`, whose double hyphen can't occur in an id, so two modules' tabs can't meet); a colon or a space in it would break one of the three. A default export that isn't a module of this shape is skipped with a console warning naming its folder and the reason, because one bad folder in a copy of the repository must not stop the others or the app.
- **2026-10-26** — **Two modules with one id fail the build, and the app skips the second if one gets through.** `scripts/modules.mjs` reads each `modules/*/index.ts` before the build and throws, naming both folders, when two declare the same id. It reads the id as text (the first `id: '…'` after `export default {`, or else in the file), so a module that builds its id some other way slips past it; `collectModules` then skips the second with a `console.error` naming both folders (it never throws: that would blank the whole app for one module's mistake). The text check is the build error the maintainer asked for; the runtime one is the backstop. `test/modules-folder.test.ts` loads the real `modules/` folder, so `pnpm test` fails on a module of the wrong shape or a repeated id before a build does.
- **2026-10-26** — **The host keeps what a module does in settings rows with fixed names.** Which modules are on is one row, `modules:enabled`, a list of ids, so a module can't turn itself on by writing a key of its own; a module's storage is `module:<id>:<key>`, and the colon-free id keeps one module's prefix from being another's. The settings store (Task 3) ignores keys it doesn't know, so none of these rows reach it. A damaged `modules:enabled` (not a list) reads as none on, and entries that aren't text are dropped. An id of a module no longer in the build stays in the list, so removing a folder and putting it back keeps the writer's choice.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's commands are listed under its name, and have no keys.** The host registers them as `module:<id>:<id>` with the module's name as their group and its icon, so the palette (which draws a group for each name it finds, after the five) shows them as a block, and turning the module off takes them out by id (`unregisterCommands`). Their `keys` and `global` are not carried over: the registry binds `global` keys on the whole page in the capture phase, and a module that could take Mod+S would break saving. A command's `enabled` that throws counts as off. Every `run` goes through the same guard as the toolbar button.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module on adds its button, tab, and commands; off takes them away, and the tab is closed where it was open.** The button and tab are computed from which modules are on; the commands are registered by `startModules` as the list changes. Turning a module off while its tab is showing puts a core tab back in that pane (the first of Source, Script, Problems that the other pane doesn't show; `dropTab`), and the phone's tab back to Script. Turning one on doesn't open its tab: it appears in the rows, as the brief says.
- **2026-10-26** — **Module problems are mapped through every edit.** A module sets its problems once, against the text as it was; an edit above would leave their lines wrong. The host keeps them as positions and maps each through the edit's `ChangeSet` (new `onChange` on the session, which also says when the text was replaced wholesale). An item whose whole text an edit replaced is dropped (the fix that was applied, for one); one whose text an edit touched stays, without its fix, which was written for the old text; the rest move with their text, fix included. All go when another script is opened. This keeps a fix from ever applying to text it wasn't made for, the guard cm-fountain's own fixes have.
- **2026-10-26** — **A change from a module is one undo step of its own.** `applyChanges` (and a module problem's fix) go through `applyTextChanges`: positions are checked (inside the text, not backwards, not overlapping; otherwise it throws and changes nothing), inserted line breaks become the editor's own, and the transaction is `userEvent: 'input'` with `isolateHistory('full')`, so one Mod+Z reverses exactly the module's change and never takes the writer's typing with it. In a tab that only shows the script it does nothing and says so with a toast.
- **2026-10-26** — **The context has two readers the brief's sketch lacks, `kind()` and `cursor()`.** A module that edits at the cursor (the fixture's "add a note") needs `cursor()`, and a module that behaves differently in a Markdown document needs `kind()`. In a Markdown document `doc()` is an empty Fountain `Doc` (the editor still parses the text as Fountain, which nothing may read as scenes: DECISIONS, Task 6) and `layout()` is null; `source()`, `title()` (the first heading), `template()`, and the changes work as anywhere. Modules need not branch on `kind()` to be safe: with no blocks they find no scenes.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's tab and settings are given the context as a `context` prop.** Both are ordinary Vue components, rendered by the host only while the module is on. The context's readers are reactive (they read the session's refs), so a tab that calls `ctx.title()` while it draws is drawn again when the script changes. The fixture's tab is a plain options object, not `defineComponent(...)`: a call at the top level could keep the file in a bundle that doesn't use it.
- **2026-10-26** — **The fixture is in the bundle only if `DRAFTER_TEST_MODULES=1`.** `nuxt.config.ts` defines `__DRAFTER_TEST_MODULES__` through Vite's `define`; `registry.ts` lists the fixture only when it is true (`typeof` guarded, so a run without the define, such as the unit tests, reads it as off). Without the flag the constant is `false`, the branch is dead, and the module is tree-shaken: a Netlify-style build has no "Count scenes" anywhere in `.output/public` (checked with the brief's grep). `pnpm e2e` and `pnpm e2e:a11y` build with the flag. The unit test turns the constant on with `vi.stubGlobal` to show that the flag is what decides.
- **2026-10-26** — **The icon bundle is made from `.ts` files too, and the build checks it.** Nuxt Icon's scan reads `.vue`, `.md`, and the like by default, not `.ts`, so an icon named only in a `.ts` file (the document types' `newIcon`s in `editor/template.ts`, `i-lucide-file-plus` and `i-lucide-drama`) was left out of the bundle and drew nothing: with `provider: 'none'` there is no error either. `nuxt.config.ts` sets `scan.globInclude` to include `ts`, and passes the icons named in the root `modules/` folder (outside the app, so never scanned) as the bundle's `icons` (`moduleIconNames`). `scripts/check-build.mjs` fails the build if an `i-lucide-*` name in `app/**/*.ts` is missing from the bundle, and `e2e/icons.spec.ts` checks that the New items' icons draw. The fixture's icon is covered by the scan, so it is in a production bundle as icon data (about 1 KB; nothing of the fixture's code or text is).
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's `run` is guarded, and its failure is a toast.** `runModuleAction` awaits the action and catches a throw or a rejection, showing "<Module> couldn’t finish: <reason>" in a toast (a `role="alert"`, emptied after eight seconds, in the toolbar's `ModuleButtons.vue`, as the export's is in its menu) and logging the error. `notify` is the same toast as a `role="status"`, so it is announced politely. The toast is hand-made like the app's other two (export, changed file), not Nuxt UI's, so the three agree.
- **2026-10-26** — **On phones the buttons are a function of the count.** The toolbar fits two module buttons at 320px beside the others (the six circles, the search and theme chips and the 8px gaps come to about 250px of 320), so one or two are round buttons and three or more are one round **Modules** button opening a menu of their actions (`buttonsInMenu`, which the unit test pins). A wide screen has labelled buttons for all, as the other actions. The decision uses the window's width, as the toolbar's own icons-only rule does.
- **2026-10-26** — **Turning on a module that uses the network asks in a dialog, and Cancel is where focus starts.** The question is the maintainer's sentence, built from the origins' hosts ("…sends your script to a.example and b.example when you ask it to. Turn it on?"), in a native modal `<dialog>` over Settings with the Markdown dialog's buttons. The switch stays off until Turn on; Escape and Cancel leave it off, and focus goes back to the switch. A module with no network origins turns on at once. The declaration is a promise and a prompt, not a mechanism: the CSP still allows only `'self'`, so nothing leaves until a module's own plan changes `scripts/csp.mjs` and the Privacy page, and its plan records the change to §2's "a script never leaves the device". The security spec checks the policy with the fixture on.
- **2026-10-26** — **Settings → Modules is hidden when there are no modules.** A build with an empty `modules/` folder (drafter.dev until Task 8's samples) has no Modules section in Settings, so there is no empty heading; `modules/README.md` is where a local installer learns how to add one.
- **2026-10-26** — **Modal dialogs are centered by one global rule.** Tailwind's reset gives every element `margin: 0`, which took from a modal `<dialog>` the user-agent's `margin: auto` that centers it, so the Settings, Remove, changed-file, same-title, and consent dialogs opened at the top left of the page. `main.css` has `dialog:modal { margin: auto; }` (unlayered, so it beats the reset's layer), and an e2e test checks the Settings dialog is centered at desktop and phone sizes.
- **2026-10-26** — **A run belongs to the script and the switch it began in.** A button's or command's run is given a context made for that run (`runContextFor`), which remembers the script generation (bumped when another script opens), the module's epoch (bumped when it is turned off) and the text it saw. `applyChanges` and `setProblems` return `false` and do nothing once the script or the switch has changed, and `applyChanges` also refuses when anyone but this run has edited the text since it began (its own earlier changes are allowed through), so a run that waited for a server can't change text it never saw or land in another script. The tab's and settings' context is made again for each script and each time the module is turned on, with the same guard minus the text check (it has no start to compare with). `commands(ctx)` is called again for each run of a command so the command gets its own context: a module's `commands` must be cheap and free of effects. The pure part is `runScope` (`modules/scope.ts`), unit-tested.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's tab and settings are drawn inside an error boundary.** `ModuleBoundary.vue` catches what a module's component throws while it draws (`onErrorCaptured`), shows "<Name>’s tab couldn’t be shown." in its place and as the alert toast, and stops the error there. The fixture breaks on purpose when `localStorage['drafter-fixture-throw']` is `1`, which the browser test sets.
- **2026-10-26** — **Consent is kept with the origins agreed to.** `modules:enabled` holds `{ id, origins }` for each module on: the network origins the module declared when the writer agreed. A module whose declared origins are not all among them (an update asked for more) is off, and Settings says it "now asks for network access you haven’t agreed to" until it is turned on again, which asks again (`consentedIds`, unit-tested). A module with fewer origins than agreed stays on. A bare id in the row (not written by this code) counts as agreeing to nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The modules folder is typechecked by its own tsconfig.** `modules/tsconfig.json` extends the app's generated one, maps `~` to the app and packages (`vue`) to the app's `node_modules`, and `pnpm --filter @drafter/web typecheck` runs `vue-tsc` on it after `nuxt typecheck`, so `pnpm -r typecheck` covers it. It is not in the app's own tsconfig because Playwright reads that one, and a catch-all path there sent the test runner's own import to a second copy of itself. A deliberate type error in a module made the typecheck fail (checked, then removed).
- **2026-10-26** — **A build checks itself.** `scripts/check-build.mjs` runs at the end of `pnpm build`: the fixture's text ("Count scenes", "Fixture: add a note") must be in a build made with `DRAFTER_TEST_MODULES=1` and in no other, so a production build can't ship it and a test build can't silently lack it. The unit test `ships no fixture in a production build` keeps its name for the flag it checks; this is the check of the output.
- **2026-10-26** — **The storage prefix is organization, not a boundary.** `module:<id>:<key>` keeps one module's rows apart from another's through the context, but every module is code in the page and can read the whole table (and the script). Anything a module stores, an API key included, is plain text in this browser, which `modules/README.md` says.
- **2026-10-26** — **A disabled command can say why.** `Command` gained the optional `why()`; a menu that lists a disabled command (the app menu) shows it as the item's description, so it is read with the item, and the palette still hides the command. Only Revert to Saved has one: the script has changes and a saved file, but its type was changed since, so the saved text is the other type's ("The saved file is a different type of document"). The other reasons it is off (nothing changed, never saved, a read-only tab) are plain from the rest of the screen and the status bar. `revertToSaved` no longer asks for `scrollIntoView`: it keeps the scroll, as the comment said; the editor takes focus afterward at the cursor.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's button can open its tab: `ModuleContext.openTab()`.** The sample brief has the Scene list and Cast buttons "open their tab", and the host only ran a button's `run`; a tab is otherwise always in the tab row. `openTab()` shows the module's own tab and focuses its panel: on a wide screen in the pane that isn't showing Source (so the editor stays), on a phone in place of the current tab; it does nothing for a module with no tab, or once the script or the switch changed (the same guard as `goto`). It is the one addition to the contract Task 8 made, documented in the types and the README.
- **2026-10-26** — **Scene length is eighths of a page, from line positions.** `layout().index[block]` gives each block's first printed line as a page and a line; the pages' lines are counted from the top of the first page, a scene runs to the next scene's first line (the last scene to the last printed line), and an eighth is a page's body lines divided by eight (54 lines, 6.75 for the US screenplay). The count is rounded and never below one, since a scene is never nothing, and it is shown as `3/8 page`, `1 page`, `1 1/8 pages`. While `layout()` is `null` the tab says "Laying out…"; while the layout lags the text (a scene it hasn't reached) a row has no page or length.
- **2026-10-26** — **The sample modules do nothing in a Markdown document, except say so.** Scene list and Cast have no scenes or characters there and say "Not available for Markdown documents."; Cast's copy command and Notes' remove command are hidden from the palette (`enabled`) and say the same if run. Notes does not look for `[[…]]` in Markdown: there it is usually a link to another page, and a Markdown document has no notes that never print, so removing "notes" could remove links.
- **2026-10-26** — **Notes asks with `ctx.confirm`, and a removal tidies the text.** One `applyChanges` call removes every note, so one Undo restores them all; a `false` return (stale run, read-only script) says nothing was removed. A note on lines of its own takes the lines and one blank line when two would meet; a note beside text takes the space beside it; notes with only spaces between them go together on one line, and across a line break only when they fill their lines (so `A [[a]]` / `[[b]] B` becomes `A` / `B`, never `A B`); a boneyard is left alone. (Fix round 1 replaced the first version's native `confirm`.)
- **2026-10-26** — **The modules' styles are Tailwind classes, and Tailwind reads the folder.** A module's tab is a plain component object (a test can draw it without a browser), so it can't carry a scoped style; its classes use the app's CSS variables (`var(--text)`, `var(--line)`) and `main.css` adds `@source` for `modules/` so Tailwind generates them. The icons the modules name were already bundled.
- **2026-10-26** — **The modules' tests are a Vitest project, with the app's `vue`.** `vitest.config.ts` lists `modules`; `vitest.shared.ts` aliases `vue` to the app's copy, since the root has none and a module imports `h` from it (the app's own project already deduped it). `modules/tsconfig.json` maps `@drafter/*` to the packages' sources, which `modules/` needs now that its fake context imports the parser and the layout.
- **2026-10-26** — **`ModuleContext.confirm` is Drafter's own modal dialog.** A module with something destructive to do needs to ask, and the native `confirm` is unstyled, may be suppressed by an embedder, and would make a module reach for a browser global. `confirm(message, { confirmLabel, cancelLabel })` (`modules/confirm.ts`, `ModuleConfirmDialog.vue`, the same shape as the Remove and Markdown questions: modal `<dialog>`, centered by the global rule, headed with the module's name, Cancel focused, Escape is No) resolves true only for Yes. One question is open at a time (a newer one answers the older No), and one is answered No when another script opens or its module is turned off, and resolves false at once for a context that is no longer live.
- **2026-10-26** — **A dialog's state is set by what the writer did, not by its close event.** The Settings dialog mirrored its openness in `settingsOpen`, which Escape (a native close) left true until the queued `close` event, so a Mod+, in that gap was ignored; and after Done a quick reopen was undone by the stale close. Escape's `cancel` is now prevented and goes through `settingsOpen`, and every mirrored dialog (Settings, Remove, the removal in the Scripts menu, the module consent, the Markdown, same-title, and changed-both questions, and the module question) ignores a `close` event that arrives while its dialog is open again. `e2e/settings.spec.ts` presses Escape and Done then Mod+, back to back (both failed before).
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's layout can lag its doc; the host and the module each guard it.** `layout()` is made in a worker, so after an edit it describes the previous text for a moment. The host now withholds the old script's layout when another script is opened (until a request made after the switch has come back), and `layoutFailed()` says the worker failed and no layout has come. Scene list treats a layout whose index length differs from the block count as "Laying out…". An edit inside a block (same count) can still show a slightly old page for a moment; that is the same lag the page bar has.
- **2026-10-26** — **A Markdown document exports as Save writes it.** Both of its exports (`.md` and `.txt`) are the text as it stands, so both now go through `encodeFile` with the project's line break and byte order mark, as Save does; before, they were written with `\n` and no mark, so an export of a Windows file differed from the file. A screenplay's text export is rendered, not the file's text, and keeps `\n`.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's fixes are checked when they are set, and no editor listener can starve the recovery copy.** `setModuleProblems` took a fix's changes on trust, and mapping one past the end of the text through the next edit threw inside the editor's update listener, before the listener that schedules the recovery copy ran, on every keystroke after. Now (a) a fix is kept only if its changes pass `applyTextChanges`'s own check (`checkedChanges`: whole positions inside the text, none backwards, none overlapping; stored sorted), and otherwise dropped with its problem kept; (b) a run's `setProblems` drops every fix once the text has changed since the run began (`!scope.mayChange()`), since they were written for text that moved; (c) the session calls each `onChange`/`onState`/`onUpdate` listener in its own `try`/`catch` (`console.error`), so one that throws never stops the others. `test/session.test.ts` and `test/module-context.test.ts` load the real editor session in jsdom, with only the panes (Nuxt state) stood in for.
- **2026-10-26** — **A module's tab and settings are drawn afresh when another script opens.** A tab component's `setup` runs once and the sample modules (and, likely, third-party ones) keep the `props.context` they were given; after a switch that context is no longer live, so its `goto` did nothing and the Scenes and Cast rows stopped working. The host fixes it for every module rather than asking each to read `props.context` anew: `contextGeneration()` (reactive) names the script the contexts are for, and `AppPane` keys a module tab's `ModuleBoundary` by `${tab.id}:${generation}` (Settings → Modules keys a module's settings by the generation), so the component is mounted again with the new context. Making a tab's `goto` not depend on the generation was the other way; it was not taken, since `setProblems`, `applyChanges`, and `confirm` on the old context would still refuse.
- **2026-10-26** — **The Markdown question is about the script it was asked about.** The app's keys still work under the modal question, so Mod+O or Mod+Alt+N could open another script and "Make it Markdown" then changed that one. `chooseType` remembers the project id before asking and changes nothing if it differs after the answer (as the changed-both question does), and `MarkdownDialog` answers the question No when the editor loads another script, so it never stays up over the wrong one.
- **2026-10-26** — **Fix all and the changed-file replace are undo steps of their own.** Both dispatched with plain `userEvent`s and no `isolateHistory`, so a keystroke typed within CodeMirror's join delay, touching the text they wrote, joined them, and one Undo took back both the typing and the fix (or the file's text). They now carry `isolateHistory.of('full')`, like Revert, a template choice, and a module's `applyChanges`: one Undo takes back the typing, the next the fix or the replace.

That is a reload the writer didn't choose, with unsaved text in the tab. `client.registerPlugin: false` turns it off, and `app/plugins/service-worker.client.ts` calls `registerSW` from `virtual:pwa-register` (provided by `vite-plugin-pwa`; typed in `app/update/virtual.d.ts`, because the app doesn't depend on that package itself). `onNeedRefresh` sets `needRefresh`; `onNeedReload` is an empty function, which replaces the library's own reload; and the plugin watches `controllerchange` itself. The first change of controller on a first visit (`clientsClaim`) is not an update; a later one reloads this tab only if it had asked (`applying`), and otherwise sets `replaced`, which leaves the offer up and makes Reload a plain reload. Workbox's `controlling` event carries `isUpdate` only if a worker controlled the page when it loaded, so on a first-visit tab that is left open it would never reload at all; `controllerchange` doesn't have that gap.
- **2026-10-26** — **`clientsClaim: true`, `skipWaiting: false`.** The first worker takes over the page at once, so its lazy chunks come from the cache. A later one waits (`skipWaiting` only on the `SKIP_WAITING` message that Reload sends), so the page the writer is in keeps the files it started with.
- **2026-10-26** — **The update toast is Drafter's own, not Nuxt UI's.** `UpdatePrompt.vue` is a fixed, non-modal region above the status bar, in a polite live region that stays in the page. With changes that aren't in a file, **Reload** swaps the buttons for the question (the brief's text) and moves focus to **Save first**; Escape and **Cancel** go back and put focus on **Reload**. It appears only when the palette, Settings, a native dialog, a menu, and the tour are all closed (the tour's `modalOpen()` and `tourShowing`, polled), and once shown it stays. The flow (`app/update/flow.ts`) is plain code with injected parts, so it has a unit test.

It is disabled while it saves.

They run one at a time (`mode: 'serial'`). - **2026-10-26** — **Task 7 review fixes: a Reload always reloads, a download is waited for, and two tabs are tested.**
  - **Controller changes** (`createControllerWatch`, `update/state.ts`): the tab whose writer asked reloads on any change of controller, first or not. Before, a tab loaded with no controller (a hard reload, while another tab's old worker still controls and the new one waits) took its own change for the first-visit claim and did nothing, and a second Reload posted skipWaiting to a worker that was no longer waiting. A change counts as the first-visit claim only when no update has been offered (`needRefresh`); any other change in a tab that didn't ask sets `replaced`, which keeps the offer and makes Reload a plain reload.
  - **Save first where Save is a download** (no `showSaveFilePicker`: Firefox, Safari): `save()` returns `saved` the moment the download starts, and a reload within milliseconds loses the blob and Safari's "allow downloads?" question. The flow then goes to a `downloading` stage, "Your script is downloading. Reload when it has finished.", with **Reload** (now plain, nothing is unsaved) and **Later**; nothing reloads by itself.
  - **"Reload anyway" flushes the recovery copy first** (`useScript().flush()`), reloads after, and skips the browser's own question only from the moment of the reload (`allowLeaving()`, in its own import-free `storage/leaving.ts`, is called by the plugin just before `location.reload()`, not seconds earlier; importing it from `storage/script.ts` in the plugin put the editor, and the lazy Markdown mode with it, in the first load, which `markdown.spec.ts` caught in Firefox). With no recovery copy (`unavailable`) the button is "Reload and lose them".
  - **Navigation fallback denies file-like paths** (`/\/[^/?]+\.[^/]+$/`) and `/docs`, so `/og-image.png`, `/fonts/courier-prime/OFL.txt`, and `/404.html` are never answered with the app. **For Task 8:** the `/docs` pages must be precached with and without a trailing slash (`/docs/saving` and `/docs/saving/`), or the worker must be told to leave them to the network; today `/docs/...` is denied the fallback, so a docs page that isn't precached under the address used is a network error offline, not the app.
  - **The manifest is listed once** in the precache (the plugin adds it; the glob no longer matches `.webmanifest`); `check-build` fails on a file listed twice.
  - Tests: unit tests for the watch, the download stage, the flush order, and the labels; e2e for the download, two tabs (one reloads; the other keeps its text and its offer, and its own Reload reloads it), the hard-reload tab, and the file-path fallback.
- **2026-10-26** — **No periodic update check while a tab stays open.** The module's `periodicSyncForUpdates` is 0 and nothing else polls: Phase 1's design says the app makes no network requests after it loads. A new version is noticed when the browser checks `sw.js` on the next visit or reload (it is never cached: `Cache-Control: no-cache`), and the writer is offered it then.
- **2026-10-26** — **Runbook: taking the worker away, and recovering from a bad deploy.**
  - **Remove the worker:** set `pwa.selfDestroying: true` in `nuxt.config.ts` and deploy. `sw.js` then becomes `vite-plugin-pwa`'s self-destroying worker, which takes effect as soon as a browser installs it, that is, at its next check of `sw.js` (the next visit or navigation, or the browser's own periodic check), not only on a later visit. It calls `skipWaiting()`, and on activation unregisters itself, deletes every cache, and navigates every open window (`client.navigate`). **Open tabs therefore reload at once**, without the toast and without asking: the leave prompt may fire for a script with changes, and text not yet in a file survives only in the recovery copy (IndexedDB), which the worker does not touch. Keep it deployed for as long as people may return with the old worker, then the module can be removed. (`scripts/check-build.mjs` expects a precache and will fail such a build: set `precache` to false in its main block for that deploy.)
  - **A bad deploy:** deploy a fixed build. Browsers check `sw.js` on the next visit and install the fixed worker, which WAITS: a tab already open keeps the bad files until its writer chooses **Reload** in the toast or closes every tab of Drafter (the waiting worker then takes over when the last one closes). There is no way to push it into open tabs, because nothing may reach the network after the app loads. A browser that is offline keeps the bad version until it is online again. A writer who is stuck on a bad version can reload with the browser's hard reload (which skips the worker for that load), or clear the site's data (the recovery copy in IndexedDB goes with it, so save scripts to files first).
- **2026-10-26** — **Focus mode is a second layout, not CSS over the first.** `index.vue` draws either the panes (with the toolbar and status bar) or one `<main data-focus-layout>` with a `SourcePane` and the `FocusMarker`; the toolbar, tabs, bars, status bar, and other pane are not in the page at all, so a screen reader and the Tab key can't reach them. State is `useFocusMode()` (`useState`, shared by the toolbar button and the `focus-mode` command, group View, `Mod-Shift-Enter`, `global` so it also leaves from the editor). The same `SourcePane` lends the one editor a host, so text, history and cursor are kept; the editor is focused on entering, and on leaving it is focused if the Source is showing (else the Focus button gets focus back).
- **2026-10-26** — **Esc leaves only when nothing used it** (`leavesOnEscape`, `utils/focus.ts`). A window listener in the bubble phase runs after CodeMirror's own keymap, which marks a handled key with `preventDefault` (closing the completion list or the search panel), so it ignores a prevented Escape, one with a modifier or in an input method composition, one from inside a dialog, menu or the palette, and any Escape while a `dialog[open]` or `[role=dialog]` is in the page. One consequence: CodeMirror's Escape also collapses a selection, so with text selected the first Esc collapses it and the second leaves. Binding Escape ahead of CodeMirror and teaching it about each of CodeMirror's panels was judged worse.
- **2026-10-26** — **The corner marker takes over what the status bar says.** It shows **Saved** or **Unsaved changes** (the script's `dirty`, so a sample or new script nobody has edited reads "Saved"; the status bar's "Sample…" and "N min ago" wording is not repeated) and **Read-only** in a tab that can't edit. It also holds the save announcement (`role=status`) and the save, open, read-only and type alerts, because hiding the status bar must not hide a failed Save. It has a **Leave focus mode** button: a phone has no Esc and no toolbar. The `sprint` slot is where Task 4's countdown goes. The line-number and fold gutters are hidden in focus mode.
- **2026-10-26** — **Moving the editor into the focus layout puts the cursor back.** Safari drops the cursor from the page's selection when the editor's element is moved, while CodeMirror still believes it is where it was and so doesn't write it again; typing then landed at the start of the script. On entering or leaving, after focusing the editor, the page moves the selection and back, which makes CodeMirror write it. The pane swaps of Plan 2 move the editor the same way; whether they have the same fault in Safari wasn't checked.
- **2026-10-26** — **Showing another tab leaves focus mode.** Show Script, Show Preview, Show Problems and a module's `openTab()` have nothing to show in the focus layout, so they leave it first. Opening, saving, and the palette's other commands work in it as they do outside.
- **2026-10-26** — **Reduced motion: the layout fades in for 0.18 s, and not at all under `prefers-reduced-motion`.** The e2e test checks the animation is `none` under the preference and not `none` without it.
- **2026-10-26** — **Typewriter scrolling is a ViewPlugin that dispatches `scrollIntoView(head, { y: 'center' })` in a microtask** (`editor/typewriter.ts`, in its own compartment, reconfigured by `applyEditorSettings` so a load keeps it). A listener may not dispatch during an update, and a microtask lands before CodeMirror's measure step, which keeps only the last scroll target, so the writer's own minimal scroll is replaced and the text moves once. The room to center is the `.cm-content` padding, half the scroller's height above and below, set as an inline style by a measure that runs again when the pane's geometry changes (so a resize or a text-size change keeps it) and removed when the setting is turned off; in focus mode it replaces the 56px under the last line, which half a pane covers.
- **2026-10-26** — **What counts as the writer's own change.** A transaction centers when it changed the text or the selection, its user event is `input`, `delete`, `undo`, `redo`, `select`, or `move` (not `select.pointer`, not `input.replace`, which is how a file's text is put in), it is not annotated `appChange`, and the editor has focus. The app's own dispatches carry `input` as well (a template block, Fix all and a problem's fix, a module's `applyChanges`), so those three mark theirs with `appChange` (exported from `editor/typewriter.ts`); the focus test covers the rest, since typing needs the editor focused and a button, menu, or dialog that makes a change holds focus. A first version looked for a key, `beforeinput`, or `input` event in the editor a moment before, and it failed on a phone: on Android CodeMirror reads typing from the page's mutations, and neither event reaches a plugin's observers (found with Pixel 7 emulation). Loading is `setState` and no transaction, the palette's Go to line scrolls itself, and wheel scrolling changes neither the text nor the selection, so none of them is touched. A change the app makes while the editor happens to have focus (a palette command that returns focus first) and carries no mark would center; that is harmless, since the browser scrolls the cursor into view then anyway. `test/typewriter.test.ts` checks the rule with a real `EditorView` in jsdom.
- **2026-10-26** — **A click or a drag with the mouse (`select.pointer`) doesn't center.** The line under the pointer is the one the writer is looking at, and a drag that scrolled the text under the pointer would fight it. The brief's "keyboard/mouse" is taken as the writer's own keys and typing, and not the wheel.
- **2026-10-26** — **The scroll is instant, and nothing needs `prefers-reduced-motion` for it.** CodeMirror scrolls by setting `scrollTop`, and the scroller has no `scroll-behavior`, so the scroll never animates, with the preference or without it; an e2e test checks the computed `scroll-behavior` is `auto` under reduced motion and that the text is centered at once.
- **2026-10-26** — **The setting has three doors.** The Source bar's **Typewriter** button, Settings → **Typewriter scrolling**, and the palette's **Turn on / Turn off typewriter scrolling** (only the one that applies is listed, as with line numbers) all set the `typewriter` row of the settings table. The bar is not in focus mode, so the setting is turned on before entering it, from the bar, the dialog, or the palette.
- **2026-10-26** — **`restoreSelection(view)` is the one answer to Safari dropping the cursor.** Moving the editor's element in the page loses the cursor from Safari's selection while CodeMirror believes it is unchanged. The helper (`editor/restore-selection.ts`) dispatches the selection away and back, both with `addToHistory: false` and no user event, and focus mode's enter and leave and `SourcePane`'s remount-with-focus (a resize between the phone layout and the two panes) both use it. The webkit test types in the middle of a line after widening the window, and failed with "Xhello world" before.
- **2026-10-26** — **The corner marker carries two more things the hidden bars said.** `warning === 'unavailable'` (the recovery copy can't be kept; an alert, as in the bar) and a file trouble (`troubleMessage`, shared with `FileBar`; a status, with "Leave focus mode to deal with it", since the actions live in the bar). The softer "this browser may clear your recovery copy" warning stays in the bar only: it is dismissible advice, not a loss in progress.
- **2026-10-26** — **The focus command is off under a native dialog and while composing.** `enabled` is false when `dialog[open]` is in the page (the page is the dialog's while it is open; the palette is not a native dialog, so it still lists the command) or `view.composing`. The key is still the command's, so the browser's own use of it doesn't run. `leavesOnEscape` also ignores key code 229, which Safari sends for the Escape of an input method after its composition has ended (`isComposing` is already false then).
- **2026-10-26** — **The Focus button has an id for the focus-restore.** Leaving focus mode with the Source hidden focuses `#focus-button`, not `[data-tour="focus"]`, which belongs to the tour.
- **2026-10-26** — **Counts: what a word is, and what the title page does.** `countScript` (`stats/count.ts`) counts the text that prints. A word is a run of characters between spaces, the same rule as a Markdown document's count (`wordsIn`, shared in `markdown/words.ts`), so `-` in a scene heading is a word. The blocks that never print (sections, synopses, notes, the boneyard, page breaks) and the metadata block (a boneyard) add none. The title page's values count (it prints; the brief leaves it out of pages only), so a new script's template is a few words. Pinned by hand on the sample: 261 words (11 on the title page, 250 in the body), 2 scenes, 1.4 pages, 1 minute.
- **2026-10-26** — **Pages are fractional from where the last page's text ends, not from the cursor.** The brief says "from the cursor's line position on the last page"; a number that moved with the cursor would not be growth. So pages are the full pages before the last one plus how far down the last page its last printed line is (its 23rd of 54 lines in the sample is 0.43), to one decimal; the title page is left out; minutes are that, rounded, at a page a minute. Every page but the last counts as full, whatever it holds, so a page that breaks early doesn't shorten the script. Pages are 0 without a layout (the goal treats them as not known yet).
- **2026-10-26** — **Counts are remembered by the identity of the parse and the layout** (`WeakMap`s), so the status bar and the panel share one pass. `reparse` makes new blocks at every edit, so there is nothing to reuse between keystrokes: the pass is one walk of the blocks (a 120-page script is a few thousand), after the parse that already walks them. The layout may lag the text by a moment; pages are then a keystroke behind the words, which is fine for a count.
- **2026-10-26** — **A layout counts only once it is the open script's** (`stats/counts.ts`). The layout on hand belongs to the script that was open until one asked for after the switch comes back, and to the text before it was Markdown after a change of kind. Like the module host's guard, it keeps a floor (`requestedSeq() + 1`) raised by a synchronous listener on `onChange(null)` and a synchronous watch of `kind`, so the floor is set before the layout's own watcher asks again. A Markdown document's words are known only once its tree is (the last one's are reset to 0 on a load, which would otherwise be taken as the day's start).
- **2026-10-26** — **The goal and the days live in the settings table, outside `Settings`**: `goal:<projectId>` is `{ unit, target }` and `day:<projectId>:<YYYY-MM-DD>` is `{ pages?, words? }`, the counts the day began with. The `Settings` store ignores them (they are not keys of the shape), like `module:<id>:<key>`. A damaged row is dropped field by field. Removing a script removes its goal and its days (`removeProject`).
- **2026-10-26** — **Today is the local date, taken lazily, with an injected clock.** `createGoal` takes `now()`; the day's start is the script's counts the first time it is seen on a date. A timer set for the next local midnight and every change of the counts look at the clock, and the window coming back to the front does too, so a day is noticed even if a sleeping computer ran no timer. At a rollover the start is the count at that moment, in memory at once (no flicker), and the table keeps the first start written, so a second tab agrees with the first. A unit whose count isn't known yet (the layout hasn't come back) is added when it is, without holding the other.
- **2026-10-26** — **A cut counts against the goal, and shows as 0.** `progress.done` is the real growth and can be negative; the status bar and `progressLabel` show `max(0, done)`, and the panel says how much was cut. Because it is derived (now minus the day's start), writing the text back brings it back. Pages are rounded to a tenth at both ends so the difference has no float noise.
- **2026-10-26** — **Old days are pruned after 30 days.** `KEEP_DAYS = 30`: when a script is opened and when the date rolls over, `day:` rows whose date is more than 30 days old, for any script, are deleted. Goals and the other rows are never touched.
- **2026-10-26** — **A sample keeps no goal.** A sample is one record the writer shares with everyone who opens it, and saving it makes a new script with a new id, which would lose a goal set on it. The panel says so, and `canSet` is false; no day's start is kept for a sample either. A Markdown document's goal is in words only (the unit is fixed and the control disabled), and a stored pages goal on a script made Markdown shows no progress.
- **2026-10-26** — **A goal is whole words (1 to 1,000,000) or pages in tenths (0.1 to 1,000).** `isGoal` checks it for both `setGoal` and a stored row. The status bar says "1 page" and "1 word" when the target is 1, and otherwise the plural exactly as the brief writes it.

The panel is drawn inside the status bar and positioned above it, as the recovery explanation is. It has a section for the goal and a marked place for Task 4's "Start a sprint" section; the status bar has one for the sprint's time left.
- **2026-10-26** — **A selection change scrolls only when a key that moves the cursor made it.** Select All, a handle dragged on a touch screen, and an arrow key all carry the user event `select`, so the event can't tell them apart. The plugin listens for `keydown` in the capture phase of the editor's element (before the keymap dispatches) and, for an arrow, Home, End, or Page Up or Down, marks the selection changes of that task as keyed (`centersOn(tr, navigated)`). A change to the text (typing, deleting, undo, a move of lines) needs no key. A selection taller than the pane (`exceedsViewport`) is not chased either, so Shift-End across a script stops scrolling once it is bigger than the pane. A soft keyboard has no arrow keys, so moving the cursor by touch or by the handles never scrolls; typing does.
- **2026-10-26** — **Settings are applied by what changed.** `applyEditorSettings` reconfigures a compartment only when its extension differs from the one it holds, and the extensions are made once (`typewriterExtension`, `lineNumbersExtension` and `spellcheckAttribute` return the same object for the same answer). Before, every change rebuilt every compartment, and rebuilding typewriter scrolling removed its padding and added it back, which could clamp the scroll position in a long script. A load still applies everything, since a fresh state starts from the compartments' first values. The unit test is what fails without it: the end-to-end test of the scroll position passes either way in Chromium, because the plugin puts its padding back in the same measure.
- **2026-10-26** — **On a phone, the middle is the middle of what can be seen.** The soft keyboard on iOS and Chrome for Android shrinks `window.visualViewport` and not the layout viewport, so the scroller's own height still runs under the keyboard. `viewportInsets(scroller rect, visualViewport)` gives the rows hidden at the top and bottom; they are given to CodeMirror as `EditorView.scrollMargins` (a target's margins move where "center" falls: a bottom margin of the keyboard's height puts the line half that much higher), and added to the padding above and below the text so the first and last lines can reach the middle of the visible part. The plugin measures again on the visual viewport's `resize` and `scroll`. Without a visual viewport, and on a desktop, the insets are 0 and nothing changes. The emulated phones in Playwright have no soft keyboard and don't shrink the visual viewport, so the unit tests give the math and a fake `visualViewport` to the plugin; a real keyboard has not been tried.
- **2026-10-26** — **The corner marker says "Updated from …" too.** The changed-file notice (`updatedNotice`) is shown by the file bar, which focus mode removes; the marker now has a live region for it (always in the page, so a later message is announced) and a visible copy, for the 8 seconds the notice lasts.
- **2026-10-26** — **Entering focus mode with typewriter scrolling on centers the line.** The scroll after the editor is moved is `center` when the setting is on (it was `nearest`, which left the line wherever the new pane happened to show it). Esc's check for an open dialog uses `:modal` for native dialogs (a `<dialog open>` that is not modal doesn't take the Escape), and still counts any `role="dialog"` or `role="alertdialog"` of the page's own; a browser that throws on `:modal` counts every open dialog as modal, as before.

Traced with a log of every `focus()` and DOM change: the app focused the editor correctly, and then the test's `showSource` tapped the Source tab, which took the focus. The test had waited for the bar to be gone with a role query (`getByRole('region', …)`), but while the More menu is closing the page behind it is `aria-hidden`, so the query found nothing although the bar was still in the page (36 of 40 runs in the probe); the new script had not come yet, the editor looked hidden, and the tab was tapped. Lane A's extra rendering only made the window wider. The test now waits for the new script's text first. Separately, and because it was a real (if tiny) gap, the app now makes the swap and the editor appear in one render: `switchTo` takes `shown`, called in the same step as the swap, and `newScript` passes `editor.goto(cursor)` (the focus still follows in `nextTick`). Before, a render showed the new script with no bar and the old tab; `e2e/first-visit.spec.ts` watches the page's changes and fails if any state has no sample bar and no editor showing (it failed on every phone run before the change). The New items of the app menu and the Open menu are tested for focus on every viewport.

Escape in a step is the tour's alone: its dialog is a `[role=dialog]`, so `leavesOnEscape` is false for it; this is tested by turning focus mode on while a step is up (the backdrop and held keys mean a writer can't) and pressing Escape once.
- **2026-10-26** — **A sprint is timed by the clock, not by ticks.** `createSprint(clock, onEnd)` keeps `endsAt` and works out the time left as `endsAt - clock.now()` each time it is asked (`tick`), so a background tab whose timers are throttled to a tick a minute is right the moment it ticks again. The end is the first tick at or after `endsAt`, however late, and `onEnd` runs once (the sprint is idle by then). The app's sprint ticks every 500 ms while running and also when the tab becomes visible, and only changes what the page shows when the displayed second or the status changes. It uses `Date.now()`, so setting the computer's clock back mid-sprint would lengthen it; accepted. Pause keeps what was left, and a pause asked for after time was up is the end instead.
- **2026-10-26** — **A sprint belongs to the session, and reports on the script it began on.** It is kept in memory only (a reload ends it, with no notice), and it keeps running if the writer opens another script. At the end it compares the open script's words (and pages) with what the script it began on held, and says so honestly: if a different script is open, or the document's kind changed (screenplay to Markdown), it says "Sprint done. The script changed while it ran, so there is no count." (Coming back to the script it began on before time is up gives the count.) The same message is used when the starting words weren't known (a long Markdown document's words arrive from the worker). Writing in two scripts during one sprint is not summed; that would need a count for a script that is not open.
- **2026-10-26** — **What the end says.** "Sprint done: 312 words (1.2 pages)." for a screenplay; pages are left out when the layout hadn't come back at either end or the page change went the other way from the words; "Sprint done: no words added." for none; "Sprint done: 45 words cut (0.3 pages)." for a net cut (the goal counts a cut against the day, so a sprint says it too); a Markdown document says words only ("Sprint done: 312 words."). It is the one live region that hears of a sprint (`role=status`, polite, in `SprintNotice`, in both layouts and always in the page) and the toast, and empties after 8 s. Starting, pausing, resuming, and stopping say nothing: the button the writer used changes in front of them.
- **2026-10-26** — **The countdown is plain text in a group.** `SprintTimer` is a `role=group` named Sprint holding the text and the buttons: not `role=timer`, not `aria-live`, not a status. It shows `15:00 left` (`1:30:00` from an hour, seconds rounded up so 0:00 means time is up), and `Paused · 15:00 left`. Pause becomes Resume in the same button, so focus stays; Hide leaves a single **Show sprint** button (focus moves to it, and back to Pause on Show) so the countdown can always be brought back; Stop, or the end, moves focus to the editor if it was in the group (the button it was on is gone). The same component is in the status bar and in focus mode's marker. The Counts panel starts a sprint (15, 25, 45 minutes, or any whole number from 1 to 180 in a field, with a message for anything else) and closes, and while one runs it says the controls are in the status bar.
- **2026-10-26** — **Reduced motion: whole minutes.** Nothing in the countdown animates in any case (no ring, no bar, no transition). Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the text also changes once a minute and reads `15 min left` (rounded up), instead of counting each second.
- **2026-10-26** — **The palette starts the three set lengths; any other length is from the panel.** Commands: Start a 15/25/45-minute sprint, Pause sprint, Resume sprint, Stop sprint, Hide sprint countdown, Show sprint countdown, each shown only when it applies (a start only while none runs). A palette entry that asks for a number would need an input the palette does not have.

A browser lets a page make sound only after the writer has interacted with it, and the click that starts a sprint is that: with the setting on, the context is made there (`prepare`), and `play` at the end uses it, or makes one if the setting was turned on mid-sprint. A context the browser keeps suspended is asked to resume and otherwise left alone; a missing Web Audio, a refused resume, or a constructor that throws are not errors. The context is closed 1.5 s after the tone, and by Stop. With the setting off no context is ever made. The e2e test stands in a fake `AudioContext` (a real one left open can hold a WebKit page from closing).
- **2026-10-26** — **The toast does not take clicks.** `SprintNotice` has `pointer-events: none`: on a 320 px phone it sits over the status bar's buttons (found when it blocked a click on the Counts button), and it is only to be read. Its text is in the live region either way.
- **2026-10-26** — **A read-only tab keeps no goal.** `setGoal` does nothing while the two-tab guard has this tab read-only (`readOnly` is a dependency of `createGoal`; `locked` is exposed), and the Counts panel disables the goal form there and says why. Starting a sprint is still allowed: it writes nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **The goal's progress keeps the project's id, not its object.** The goal restarts (reads its goal and the day's start from the table again) only when the open script's id or sample flag changes, as two primitive watch sources. It used to watch one getter that returned a new array, which fired on every `project.value` reassignment with the same id; autosave and patches do that after each pause in typing, which blanked the status bar's progress for a moment each time.
- **2026-10-26** — **Word counts by the kind of document: the day's start is not rolled over.** The day's starting words are what the script held in whatever kind it was first seen as that day. If the writer converts a screenplay to a Markdown document mid-day, the word rule changes (the title page, and the elements that print, count in a screenplay; Markdown counts runs between spaces) and the day's progress takes the difference with it. Rolling the baseline over at a kind change would hide a real cut or a real addition just as well, and a kind change is rare, so it is left, and recorded here.
- **2026-10-26** — **A replaced or pasted-in text counts as growth.** The day's start is taken the first time this browser sees the script on a date. Loading a changed file over the script (the changed-file replace) or pasting in a long text is therefore counted as growth since that start, like any other change in the script's size; the goal measures the script, not keystrokes. A file whose text was already in the script on opening counts for nothing.
- **2026-10-26** — **Typewriter scrolling follows macOS's Control moves.** On a Mac, CodeMirror binds Control-b, f, n, p, a, e, and v to cursor moves; they now count as navigation keys (`movesCursor`), as the arrows do. Control-d and Control-k change the text and are not moves; on other keyboards Control with those letters is something else (Find, Select All), so the extra keys are Apple-only.

The page style is appended to `main.css` and uses only the tokens in `tokens.css` (dark by default, `.light` when chosen; the color-mode script in the head is hashed like the app's). The links, including **Back to Drafter**, are plain `<a href>`s, not `NuxtLink`s: the app is a page of its own (`ssr: false`), and a full load restores the writer's scripts from the recovery copy. The loading template's description now links the three pages.

A short "Other keys" list names F6 and Esc, which are not commands. `e2e/docs.spec.ts` compares the page with `COMMAND_DATA`.

Each runs `window.open(href, '_blank', 'noopener')`, from the click or key that chose it, so the editor tab is untouched: no leave prompt, the text as it was. (The first version used `location.assign` in the same tab; the controller ruled for a new tab.) The page's **Back to Drafter** is a plain `href="/"`, since the pages must work without JavaScript; in the new tab it opens a second editor, which the two-tab guard makes read-only for the script that is open in the first. `e2e/docs.spec.ts` waits for the new tab (`context.waitForEvent('page')`) from the menu (mouse and keyboard) and the palette, and checks the editor tab's text and that no dialog appeared.
- **2026-10-26** — **The service worker holds the help pages under both addresses.** The module turns `docs/x/index.html` into the entry `docs/x`, which answers `/docs/x` and not `/docs/x/`, and the page fallback leaves `/docs` alone (Task 7's note), so `pwa:beforeBuildServiceWorker` in `nuxt.config.ts` adds a manifest transform that lists each `docs/x` again as `docs/x/` (same revision). `workbox.ignoreURLParametersMatching` adds `_b`: a prerendered page fetches `_payload.json?_b=<build id>` while it hydrates, which was a failed request offline (the cached payload now answers it). The precache is 62 entries (up from 42): the four pages twice, their payloads, and the pages' own chunks. `scripts/check-build.mjs` fails a build in which a `docs/<name>/index.html` isn't cached with and without the slash, and `e2e/offline.spec.ts` opens each page offline both ways with no failed request.

The browser table scrolls inside its own focusable region on a phone; the shortcuts tables wrap to fit 320px.
- **2026-10-26** — **The help pages' accessibility check waits.** *(Superseded 2026-10-28: written in the accessibility pass, below.)* The brief's "has no accessibility violations on any help page" test is not written (the maintainer's deferral of accessibility checks to the final stage). Keyboard reachability from Help is tested.

Every link and each canonical address and `og:url` use the trailing slash (`helpHref`: `/docs/saving/`): a static host serves `docs/saving/index.html` at the slash address, and Netlify redirects the bare one to it (Netlify's default; the live site was not available to confirm, since drafter.dev serves the version before these pages), so a link to the slash form is one request and the canonical is the address a visitor ends on. The route and the service worker's entries are the bare `/docs/saving`, and both addresses are cached.

Brave is its own column, with "Varies" for what we haven't verified (keeping the recovery copy, the risk of its being deleted, installing); other Chromium browsers "behave like Chrome unless they have turned this off, as Brave has". Privacy says that on opening, the browser asks drafter.dev for `/sw.js` to look for a new version and downloads new files if there are any, with nothing from a script in the request, and names the two stores: IndexedDB (scripts, settings, goals) and localStorage (the theme, whether the tour was seen). Install steps: Chrome's address-bar button or Cast, save, and share, then Install page as app; Edge's Apps, then Install this site as an app. The Shortcuts page's "Other keys" lists F6 (side-by-side panes only, not focus mode), Esc, and the editor's own redo (Ctrl+Y), find next and previous, and completion keys.

Rather than add a second menu that repeats the toolbar, the app menu (the mark) holds what the row lacks: it has the New, Open, Save, Export, and Settings items, and now a **Template** section. `e2e/phone.spec.ts` keeps the claim honest: at 320px in every engine, every toolbar button is on the screen in a 24px target, the page does not scroll sideways, each action opens or runs, and the template is chosen from the app menu.

The section is a label (**Template: Stage play**, or **Document: Markdown (plain text)**) and three checkbox items. A choice puts focus in the editor, as the chip's does, so Mod+Z undoes it. The tour's template step on a phone now says "choose it in the Drafter menu's Template section, or from the palette".

The chip's text, its accessible name ("Template: Screenplay (fallback)"), the status bar, and the app menu's label all say "(fallback)", and both menus begin with the note "This script asks for “…”, which Drafter doesn't have, so it's shown as a screenplay." (a label that wraps, so it fits a phone). Nothing is written until the writer chooses a template: choosing Screenplay replaces the unknown name in the block (as one undo step), and until then the block is left alone, so a file that names a template from a later Drafter survives a round trip. "A way to keep the unknown name" is therefore not needed: not touching the block is how it is kept. A Markdown document has no template and shows no fallback.

No user-agent string is read. The WebKit test is an inference (WebKit has gesture events and no install prompt), not a promise: a Chromium without the prompt (an installed app, or a future change) would get the general advice, a harmless wrong answer, and any WebKit build without gesture events would too (not tried). `e2e/recovery.spec.ts` checks the sentence in each Playwright engine.

The page records every pointer event it is sent and the test ends by checking there were none. It found no gap in the app (new script, a scene with a cue, a parenthetical and dialogue, go to scene, fix a problem, stage play and back, save by download, and PDF export all work from the keys; the toolbar's Template, Save, and Export and the sample bar's New screenplay work by Tab). The Tab-only tests record pointer events too, and the PDF is checked whole (`%%EOF`, one page).
- **2026-10-26** — **A key costs no count of the whole script.** The daily goal used to watch the live counts synchronously, so every key counted the words of the whole script (2.1 ms a key on a 200,000-character script in Node, against 1.2 ms for CodeMirror's incremental reparse, and about four times that on a mid-range phone). Two changes: `wordsIn` tests each character with a comparison on its code (`isSpace`, the same set `/\s/` matches, checked against the regular expression for every UTF-16 code unit) instead of a regular expression, which more than halves a count; and `useCounts` has `steady`, the counts as of the last pause in typing (`STEADY_MS`, 300 ms, CodeMirror's lint delay), which the goal and the status bar's progress read. `steady` catches up at once when another script is loaded or the kind changes, so the goal never takes a day's start from the last script, and it keeps its object while the counts are the same (so does `known`), so nothing that watches them runs for an edit that changes no count. Each edit still looks at the date (`useGoal` listens to `onChange` and calls `tick`), and capturing a day's start reads the steady counts, which cost nothing. The Counts panel and a sprint's start and end read the live counts, once. `test/count-budget.test.ts` holds a key's counting to under a fifth of its reparse (it was 1.7 times it; now under 1%).

The Focus step is focus mode only, and says typewriter scrolling is the **Typewriter** button on the Source pane's bar, where it is; the Focus button never turned it on.

It stays where it is, hidden in focus mode as before (a toast over the page is what focus mode is without); moving it out of `.top` would make its region a row of the shell's grid.
- **2026-10-26** — **Back to Drafter closes a help tab that Help opened** (`layouts/docs.vue`; this replaces the plain link in the new tab, and the `noopener` of "Help opens in a new tab"). Help's commands now call `window.open(href, '_blank')`, so the help tab keeps the editor tab as its opener; the opener is the same origin, and the help pages run nothing but Drafter's own code, so nothing is exposed that wasn't already. On a click (no modifier, the main button) or Enter on Back, the page looks for an opener that is open, same-origin, and at `/`; if there is one, it focuses it, calls `window.close()`, and follows the link only if the window didn't close. A page opened as an auxiliary window by script can close itself in every browser, even after the reader moved to another help page in it, which `history.length === 1` would not allow. The site sends `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, so `document.referrer` can't tell a tab Help opened from one the reader opened. A page loaded any other way (typed, bookmarked, in a tab of its own, or with JavaScript off) keeps the plain `href="/"`. Tested in all five projects: the tab closes, and the editor tab is the only one left, as it was.

It is `lastSprint` in `stats/sprint.ts`, in memory for the visit, like the sprint itself. Starting a sprint clears it, so a sprint that is stopped leaves no result, as it says none at its end.
