- **2026-10-29** — **Two panes side by side start their text at the same height.** The Source has two bars over its text (the slim bar and the element bar) and every other tab one, so the Source's first line sat a bar lower than the other pane's. `.panes.wide` (`pages/index.vue`) is now one grid of four rows (tabs, slim bar, a second bar, the text) and each pane takes all four as a subgrid (`AppPane.vue`'s unscoped block, under `@supports (grid-template-rows: subgrid)`), so each row is as tall as the taller pane's part. A tab with a bar of its own (the Source with its element bar showing, `useElementBarShown`; the Outline, whose filter is that bar, `data-pane-bar`) puts it in the second row and its text under it, the part under the bar scrolling itself (the Outline's tree, as the editor already did); any other tab's slim bar takes both rows, its words at the top and its background carried down. In the text row, the top of the first line's glyphs (a Range's rectangle, so lines of different heights compare as they look) is level with the Source's, which has the editor's 12px of padding and half a line's leading (about 14px at 100%): the Script's and Compare's top padding is `calc(12px + 2px * --text-scale)`, so the top edge of the page stands level with the Source's first line (the page's own top margin above its first line belongs to the page, and moving the Source down an inch to meet it would waste the room); the Preview's sheet starts at the top of the pane with `calc(11px + 2px * --text-scale)` of padding over its first line; the Outline's filter loses its vertical padding so it is as tall as the element bar; a tab's "nothing here" line (`.empty`) and the first problem lose a few pixels over them. Cards and Notes were level already. A phone's one pane and focus mode keep their layout. WebKit sizes a subgrid's row by a bar's `min-height` rather than its content (the slim bar came out 24px over 35px of buttons), so the side-by-side bars take `min-height: auto`. The panes choose `barred` in script rather than with `:has()`: Chromium checks `:has()` against every change in the subtree, and typing a 120-page script (`script.spec.ts`) took more than 90 seconds with it. Scroll sync is unchanged: it puts the same part of the script at the two panes' tops, which now stand level, though where the Source's top is in the blank lines before a new page's first line, the Script still shows that page's top margin (the interpolation between the two lines' anchors spans the page break). **A title page (the maintainer's Call):** "aligned" means both panes' content starts at the same height, never that the Source's first line is pushed down to meet a title page's words, which a printed title page sets low on its page; with a title page in the Script, the top of that page stands level with the Source's first line, and the Source's first line stays at most the editor's own 12px of padding below the top of its pane (CodeMirror may leave the pane scrolled a few pixels into that padding), whatever the title page is. On a fresh first visit the Source starts at its top with no band above `Title:` and, at the top, the Script shows its first page from the top: checked in Chromium, Firefox, and WebKit and in headless Chrome at 1440, 1600, 1920, and 2200 wide, on `main` and on this branch; the empty band in the maintainer's screenshot did not reproduce, and nothing in the app adds room above the first line except typewriter scrolling's top inset for a visual viewport scrolled down (a phone's keyboard). `e2e/pane-align.spec.ts` checks a screenplay either way round, Markdown, the element bar hidden, and the Outline, and a first visit.
- **2026-10-29** — **Scroll sync keeps the last line in view at the end of the text (final review).** With the cursor past the last line (the empty line a script ends with), sync's keep-the-cursor-in-view measured it against the bottom of the Script's content, the last page's blank lower half, so in a short window (WebKit at 720px, anything at 600px) the writer's line was above the pane. `domPane.scrollTo` now measures a kept offset past the last anchor against that anchor's bottom. `script.spec.ts` runs "follows the cursor" at 720px again and adds a 600px case (Ctrl+End, then typing at the end of the last line). Side by side, the Outline's tree scrolls under its filter rather than the panel, so the panel's keydown passes the scrolling keys (arrows, Page Up and Down, Space, Home, End) to the tree when focus is on the panel itself (F6). The `.empty` padding change is scoped to a tab's first child, so Notes' "No notes yet" under its bar keeps its 24px.

## `apps/desktop`

- **2026-10-27** — **A workspace package, `@drafter/desktop` (private), with its main process and preload in TypeScript built by tsdown to CommonJS** (`dist/main.cjs`, `dist/preload.cjs`). A sandboxed preload can't be an ES module and may `require` only `electron` (and a few Node modules), never a file of ours, so the preload is built on its own into one self-contained file that shares no chunk with the main process (`tsdown.config.ts` has one build per entry). The main process is CommonJS too, so the two are built alike; Electron 45 could load an ES-module main, but nothing gains from it. `electron` is never bundled (`deps.neverBundle`).
- **2026-10-27** — **Electron 45.0.0, pinned exactly,** as Electron recommends (its Chromium and Node change with each release). Electron 45 has no install script: the binary downloads the first time `require('electron')` runs (the `electron` command, the e2e setup) or on `pnpm --filter @drafter/desktop exec install-electron`. So nothing goes into pnpm's `onlyBuiltDependencies`, and pnpm 10's blocking of build scripts doesn't affect it. Its `@types/node@^24` dependency also became the `@types/node` that apps/web's Vite resolves its optional peer with; the app's typecheck and build are unaffected.
- **2026-10-27** — **The web build for the desktop is drafter.dev's, with `DRAFTER_DESKTOP=1`:** no `@vite-pwa/nuxt` module (so no service worker, no Workbox runtime, no manifest, and no manifest link), the plugin that registers the worker left out (`app:resolve` in `nuxt.config.ts`, since it imports the module's `virtual:pwa-register`), and `__DRAFTER_DESKTOP__` defined true (as `__DRAFTER_TEST_MODULES__` is). `scripts/check-build.mjs` checks such a build has no `sw.js`, `workbox-*.js`, or `manifest.webmanifest`, in place of the precache check. `apps/desktop/scripts/build-web.mjs` runs it into `apps/web/.output/public` and copies it to `apps/desktop/web/` (git-ignored). The desktop and web e2e suites both build into `apps/web/.output`, so one worktree runs one at a time.
- **2026-10-27** — **One place in the web app knows about the desktop: `app/desktop.ts`.** `desktop()` returns `window.drafterDesktop` only in a desktop build (`isDesktopBuild()`), so drafter.dev's own build ignores a bridge and keeps none of the desktop code. `DesktopBridge` is the bridge's one type; the preload (`apps/desktop/src/preload.ts`) imports it and builds an object of that type, so the two can't drift.
- **2026-10-27** — **No "may be cleared" warning on the desktop.** The desktop app's profile is never evicted, so `StorageWarning.vue` hides the not-persistent warning when `desktop()` is set. The "unavailable" alert stays: a recovery copy that can't be written at all is a real failure there too. (Electron reports `navigator.storage.persisted()` as true anyway, as the spike found; the e2e test forces it false to check the rule.)
- **2026-10-27** — **app://drafter/ serves the build's files and nothing else.** `resolveAppUrl` refuses, before and after decoding, a `..` or `.` segment, an empty segment (`//`), an encoded slash or backslash, a NUL, a drive letter, and any other host, user, or port, and the result must be inside `web/`. A directory (`/docs/saving/` or `/docs/saving`) is served from its `index.html`; anything missing is a plain 404, never the app (the app is one page, so there is no SPA fallback, and Nuxt's `404.html` is the app's shell). Only GET and HEAD are answered. The scheme is privileged as standard, secure, fetch-enabled, and CORS-enabled, without service workers.
- **2026-10-27** — **The CSP goes on pages and scripts** (`headersFor`), not only on HTML as Task 1's brief first said: a worker takes its policy from its own script's response, not the page's, so without it the export and layout workers would run with no policy at all. drafter.dev sends the policy on every path, so this keeps the workers as they are on the web. Fonts, images, and styles get none (the policy governs nothing in them). The policy is read from the build's `_headers` (the `/*` rule) when the app starts, unchanged, since its script hashes change with each build; a build without one doesn't start, with a message.
- **2026-10-27** — **Navigation.** Every window's contents get the same rules (`guard` in `window.ts`, from `web-contents-created`): `will-frame-navigate` lets only app://drafter/ through, and hands an https address in the main frame to the system browser; `window.open` of an app page opens a second app window with the same options, preload, and rules (Help's pages), of an https address opens the browser, and of anything else does nothing; redirects off the app and `<webview>` are refused. Only https goes to the browser: never http, `file:`, `mailto:`, or another program's scheme. An unpackaged app writes those addresses to `DRAFTER_E2E_EXTERNAL_LOG` instead, so tests never open a browser. A refused navigation leaves Playwright's frame waiting for it, so `shell.spec.ts` reads the page with `evaluate` after one.
- **2026-10-27** — **The sandbox, and how it's tested.** `app.enableSandbox()` sandboxes every renderer, and each window also sets `sandbox`, `contextIsolation`, no Node integration (in frames and workers too), `webSecurity`, and no `<webview>`. The preload reports `process.sandboxed` and `process.contextIsolated` to the main process (`drafter:renderer-report`, accepted only from an app page), which keeps them for the e2e suite (`globalThis.drafterShell.renderers()`). The test also checks that nothing passed `--no-sandbox` or set `ELECTRON_DISABLE_SANDBOX`, that macOS and Windows report the OS sandbox on every renderer (`app.getAppMetrics()`; Linux doesn't report it), and that the page has no `require`, `process`, `module`, or `Buffer`. Locally this runs on macOS. On Linux, Chromium refuses to start without a working sandbox unless told `--no-sandbox`, so the same test under CI's xvfb (Task 7) is the Linux check; Ubuntu 24.04's user-namespace restriction (which the spike's AppImage started under without checking the sandbox) is checked there against the packaged app, where an AppArmor profile in the `.deb` may be needed.
- **2026-10-27** — **No spellchecker on Linux and Windows, for now.** *(Superseded the same day: Linux has spellcheck, offline, with a bundled en-US dictionary; see "Spellcheck on Linux, offline" below. Windows is decided with its own plan.)* Chromium's spellchecker there downloads Hunspell dictionaries from Google's servers, a request the desktop app may not make (its one request is the update check). macOS uses the system's spellchecker, which downloads nothing, so `spellcheck` is on there. Bundling dictionaries with the app (or asking first) is left to a later task; until then the Spellcheck setting has no effect on Linux.
- **2026-10-27** — **Profiles for tests.** An unpackaged app honours `DRAFTER_USER_DATA` (Electron's `userData`: IndexedDB, localStorage, and the single-instance lock), and the e2e harness also gives each test its own HOME and XDG_CONFIG_HOME, so tests run in parallel without sharing a recovery copy or a lock. A packaged app ignores the variable.
- **2026-10-27** — **One instance.** A second launch quits at once and focuses the first one's window (Task 3 opens its files there). The app quits when its last window closes, on every OS for now (macOS's convention of staying open waits for the macOS build).
- **2026-10-27** — **Files by path, through the main process** (Task 2). The desktop `FileAccess` (`apps/web/app/storage/desktop-files.ts`, installed by `useFiles()` when `desktop()` has a bridge) keeps a `FileRef` of `{ name, path }`: plain data that IndexedDB keeps as it is, and that still reads and writes after a restart with no permission to ask for again (Electron has the File System Access API too, but its handles would ask after every restart). `canSaveInPlace` is true, `permission` always `granted`, `sameFile` compares real paths (`realPath`, with every symlink followed). The main process is the only part of the app that touches the disk: `src/files.ts` (pure Node, unit-tested on real temp folders) does the work and `src/files-ipc.ts` answers the page, only from an app page (`fromApp`), after checking each argument (an absolute path of at most 4096 characters with no NUL; bytes as a `Uint8Array` of at most 256 MB; a save extension from the four; a watch id). Reads stop at 64 MB, and refuse anything that isn't a regular file (a FIFO would hang). An error reaches the page as a code alone (`gone`, `not-allowed`, `disk-full`, `too-big`, `not-a-file`, `failed: EIO`), never a stack or a path; the web app turns `gone` into `FileGoneError`, `not-allowed` into the `NotAllowedError` Save already explains, and the rest into a new `FileProblemError`, which Save shows as "Couldn’t save the file: the disk is full. The file on disk is as it was."
- **2026-10-27** — **Atomic saves.** A write goes to `.<name>.drafter-tmp-<12 hex>` in the same folder (so the rename never crosses file systems), opened `wx`, written in full, `fsync`ed, closed, and renamed over the file; then the folder is `fsync`ed where the OS allows (not Windows, where the rename is still atomic). The file keeps its permission bits exactly (`chmod` after `open`, since the umask applies to `open`'s mode); a new file gets 0666 less the umask. On any failure (a read-only folder, a full disk, bytes that stop coming) the temp file is removed and the file is untouched. Two choices: **a symlink is written through** (its final target is replaced, and the link stays a link, as an editor that "saves through" a link would; a dangling link creates its target), and **a file made read-only is refused**, not replaced, though its folder would let a rename do it. Known costs, accepted for atomicity: the replaced file gets a new inode, so a hard link to the old one keeps the old text, and the file's owner becomes the writer (if someone else owned it and let the writer's group write it), and extended attributes and ACLs aren't copied.
- **2026-10-27** — **The OS's dialogs, starting in the folder last used.** Open and Save are Electron's `dialog.showOpenDialog`/`showSaveDialog`, attached to the window, filtered by type with an "All files" choice; Save asks before replacing a file (`showOverwriteConfirmation`) and lets the writer make a folder. They start in the folder last used, kept by the main process as `lastFolder` in `desktop-settings.json` in the app's userData (written atomically too, other keys kept), else Documents, else home; a folder that has gone is not offered. Linux's dialogs don't add the chosen type's extension, so a name typed without one gets it (`Notes` → `Notes.md`) unless a file already has that name (the writer agreed to replace only the name they typed). The e2e suite answers the dialogs from a queue (`DRAFTER_E2E_DIALOG=<json file>`, each question logged to `<file>.log`), honoured only when `!app.isPackaged`, like the other test hooks; `dialogHook` is unit-tested to ignore it in a packaged app, and Task 7's packaged-app test can check the same end to end.
- **2026-10-27** — **The open file is watched, by its folder.** `installFileCheck` (apps/web) asks the `FileAccess` to watch the open script's file, when it has a `watch` (only the desktop's does), and moves the watch when another script opens or the file changes; a change runs the same check as on focus and before Save, if the window is showing (`visibilityState`; a change while it is hidden is checked when it shows again, as before). The main process watches the file's **folder** for the file's name (`fs.watch`), not the file: many programs save by renaming a new file over the old one, and a watch on the file itself would hear nothing after the first such save, while the folder's watch sees every replacement without re-arming. An event counts only if the file's inode, size, or times changed (macOS delivers an event from just before the watch began, and some systems name no file), and the check runs 300 ms after the last one. A symlink is watched at its target. Each page may hold 32 watches; they stop when it asks, reloads, or closes. Our own saves also wake the check, which finds the file the same and does nothing.
- **2026-10-27** — **A dropped file opens by its path** (`webUtils.getPathForFile` in the preload, since Electron removed `File.path`), so it saves in place like an opened one; a file with no path on disk opens unbound, as in a browser.
- **2026-10-27** — **The page may read and write only files the writer chose** (a review finding on Task 2: the file calls took any absolute path, so a page made to run someone else's script could have read `~/.ssh` or rewritten `~/.bashrc`). The main process keeps the granted paths (`src/grants.ts`), each with its real path (every link followed, the folder's too): a path is granted only by the main process itself, when the Open or Save dialog returns it, when the preload hands over a dropped file's path (`webUtils.getPathForFile`, which only a real file on disk has; granted only if it exists and is a regular file named `.fountain`, `.md`, `.markdown`, or `.txt`, and the read that follows waits for it), or when Task 3's command line or second launch names one (`registerFileIpc()` returns `grant(path)` for it, with the same rule as a drop). `read`, `write`, `realPath`, and `watch` refuse any other path with `not-allowed: not chosen`, which Save shows as "Drafter isn’t allowed to write that file. Choose Save As…"; a refused drop opens from its bytes, unbound. Every use works the real path out again and refuses (`not-allowed: moved`) if it changed, so a chosen file swapped for a link to another file, or a folder on its way swapped for a link, is never followed; the write then goes to that checked real path, so no link is followed between the check and the rename. A write also goes only to a regular file named `.fountain`, `.md`, `.markdown`, `.txt`, or `.pdf`, even when chosen (a file opened through "All files" may be read but not written; Save As puts it somewhere else). So the Save dialog's answer always ends with one of those: a name typed without one gets the chosen type's extension, and if that name is taken a number is added (`Notes (2).md`), since the writer agreed to replace only the name they typed. Grants are kept in `granted-files.json` in the profile (written atomically), so scripts bound to files still open and save after a restart; at most 1000 are kept (the oldest go first), and entries whose file is gone are dropped when the list is read (a file put back there later isn't the writer's). The IPC handlers' logic is in `src/file-handlers.ts`, free of Electron, so the unit tests call it as a page would.
- **2026-10-27** — **Windows, later: trailing dots and spaces.** Windows drops a trailing `.` or space from a path's last part (`index.html.` opens `index.html`), so on Windows `resolveAppUrl` and the file grants could see one file under two names (and `headersFor` would pick the type by the name asked for). Not reachable on Linux or macOS; the Windows port must refuse such names (or normalise them) before it ships.
- **2026-10-27** — **Spellcheck on Linux, offline, with a bundled en-US dictionary** (Ruling D5; replaces "No spellchecker on Linux and Windows, for now" above). `src/spellcheck.ts`, on `ready` before any window: on macOS nothing (the system's checker downloads nothing); elsewhere the `.bdic` files in `dictionaries/` are copied into `<userData>/Dictionaries` (Electron's `chrome::DIR_APP_DICTIONARIES`, `electron_paths.cc`), where Chromium's `SpellcheckHunspellDictionary` looks before it downloads anything, languages are set to `['en-US']` (the one dictionary there is), and `setSpellCheckerDictionaryDownloadURL` points at the bundled folder as a `file:` URL, so even a dictionary that's missing is never fetched from Google (whether Chromium's downloader can read a `file:` URL is unverified, and doesn't matter: either it reads the bundled file, or it fails, with no network either way). If the app has no dictionary, spellcheck is turned off (`setSpellCheckerEnabled(false)`) rather than fetched. `webPreferences.spellcheck` is now on everywhere; the Spellcheck setting turns it off in the editor. **Chromium wants `.bdic`, not `.dic`/`.aff`**: the download URL serves `<base>/<lang>-<version>.bdic` files, the format Chromium's `convert_dict` makes; this repo has no Chromium build, so the file is Electron's own build of Chromium's en_US (DEPENDENCIES.md has the source, checksums, and licence). The versioned name (`en-US-10-2`) follows Chromium's table, so an Electron upgrade may need the matching file from that release's `hunspell_dictionaries.zip`; the e2e test (no `spellcheck-dictionary-download-begin`, mode `bundled`) catches a mismatch on Linux CI. **Verified only on macOS so far** (where the system checker runs; the test right-clicks a misspelled word until `context-menu` reports `misspelledWord`, after focusing the window and leaving the editor, since Chromium checks a field when it's left and only in the focused window). The Linux path (seeded dictionary, no download) is verified when Task 7's CI runs this suite on ubuntu-latest. Other languages: only English is checked on Linux for now. Task 7's packaging must ship `dictionaries/` (it sits beside `web/` in the app).
- **2026-10-27** — **Deny by default** (Ruling D6, `src/permissions.ts`). The default session's permission request and check handlers allow only `persistent-storage` (storage/health.ts calls `navigator.storage.persist()`) and `clipboard-sanitized-write`, and only to app://drafter/; everything else (media, geolocation, notifications, MIDI, full screen, `fileSystem`, HID/serial/USB, display capture, …) is refused; `setDevicePermissionHandler` refuses every device; `setDisplayMediaRequestHandler` answers `null`; the HID, USB, and serial pickers are cancelled before they show. On macOS an unpackaged Electron's `getUserMedia` waits on the system's own microphone prompt before the app is asked, so the e2e test checks it only on Linux (the macOS app's Info.plist and entitlements are Plan 6's last platform).
- **2026-10-27** — **A backstop for "one request of its own"** (`src/network.ts`): `webRequest.onBeforeRequest` cancels any http, https, ws, or wss request that comes from a page (it has a `webContentsId`); the CSP (`connect-src 'self'`) already stops a page's own, so this catches what the CSP doesn't cover (a load the main process starts in a window, a future mistake). The main process's own requests (Task 6's update check) have no page and pass; so does app://. Electron allows one `onBeforeRequest` listener per session, so a later task that needs one extends this one.
- **2026-10-27** — **app:// sends drafter.dev's Permissions-Policy and Referrer-Policy** (from `_headers`' `/*` rule, `forwardedHeaders`), on every response. **Not Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy**, though drafter.dev sends it: it protects a page from cross-origin openers, and no cross-origin page can open or be opened by the app; and with `same-origin` each window's first load (from its initial about:blank) moved to a new browsing context group, which raced Playwright's attach to the window: e2e launches timed out now and then (1–2 of ~19 per run), and none in runs without it.
- **2026-10-27** — **app:// never follows a link out of `web/`**: after the URL rules, the file's real path must be inside the real `web/` folder, else 404 (a symlink in a build, or a tampered install, can't serve `~/.ssh`). Links that stay inside are served.
- **2026-10-27** — **A second launch reopens the editor** when its window is gone but another window keeps the app running: `second-instance` opens a new editor window (tracked as the main one, forgotten when it closes) instead of doing nothing, and otherwise restores, shows, and focuses it. (A help page that the editor opens with `window.open` closes with it, Electron's default `outlivesOpener: false`, so today this takes a top-level window of another kind; Task 4's Help may open those.) `pnpm build:web` also deletes `apps/web/.output` after copying the desktop build, so a build without a service worker is never left where a web deploy or preview might take it.
- **2026-10-27** — **Files the system names** (Task 3, `src/open-files.ts`). Only existing regular files with a script extension (`.fountain`, `.md`, `.markdown`, `.txt`, any case) are granted and opened; any other plain argument is said in the status bar ("Drafter can’t open “x”: it isn’t a script…", "it isn’t there", "it isn’t a file", "it can’t be read"). The launch's `argv` also holds Electron and Chromium flags and the executable, the app folder (`electron .`), and in an AppImage the AppImage's path: arguments starting with `-` are ignored (including macOS's `-psn_…`), as are the executable, `app.getAppPath()`, and `$APPIMAGE`, compared as resolved paths; relative paths resolve against the launch's own cwd (a second launch's `workingDirectory`). A flag that takes its value as a separate argument would show up as a refused file; Chromium's switches use `=`. Main grants each file (`grantScript`, the same rule as a drop) before the page is told of it; the page never learns of a path it may not read.
- **2026-10-27** — **The page is told through an inbox, not at launch**: a launch's files arrive long before the page is listening, so `OpenInbox` holds them (and a second launch's, if the editor window is being reopened) until the preload's `onOpenFile(onPath, onProblem?)` sends `openReady`, then delivers in order, once. The bridge method is named exactly `onOpenFile` (Task 7's packaged test checks it); the status messages come on the same call (`onProblem`) so the page can't register for one and miss the other. Files are sent before refusals, because opening a file clears the status bar's message. Requests are handled one at a time in the order they came, on both sides (main's queue, the page's promise chain), so two files named together don't hit "Another script is still opening".
- **2026-10-27** — **Opening a named file is Open**: the page reads it through `FileAccess.fromPath` (the desktop's `picked`) and gives it to `openFile`, so a file already open (same real path, links followed by `sameFile`) just shows its script, a changed-elsewhere recovery copy is handled as for Open, a read-only tab refuses, and the script on show with unsaved changes stays in the browser store. The window is restored, shown, and focused on each request. macOS's `open-file` calls the same code (it can arrive before `ready`, so requests wait for the shell to start); it is untested until the macOS build.
- **2026-10-27** — **Task 2 review fixes.** (1) A drop or a named file is granted by its **real** path's extension (`grantScript` and `Grants.grant(path, extensions)`, one `targetOf`): a link `x.md` → `~/.ssh/id_ed25519` is refused. Dialog picks are still granted whatever their kind (the writer chose them). **Residual risk, accepted:** main cannot tell that a drop came from a person, because the preload runs in the renderer's process; a compromised renderer could still be granted any existing script, Markdown, or text file (or link to one) the writer's account can read. It takes a renderer compromise first (CSP, no `v-html`, the network block), and only script-type files are reachable, never keys or shell profiles. (2) The listeners for a page's end/reload are added once per page (a `WeakSet`), not on every reload. (3) The temp file's name is cut so name + `.drafter-tmp-<12 hex>` fits 255 bytes (whole characters). (4) After a successful write, that file's `.drafter-tmp-*` leftovers older than a minute are removed (same prefix and 12-hex suffix only). (5) A grant is pruned on load only when its **folder exists** and the file doesn't; a drive or share not mounted yet keeps its grants (the file reads as gone until it returns). (6) The not-allowed message says "read" or "write" for what was attempted. (7) `clipboard-sanitized-write` is no longer allowed: nothing calls `navigator.clipboard` (CodeMirror's copy and paste are the browser's own); add it back with the first feature that does. (8) A file opened through "All files" whose real extension isn't a script's opens **unbound** as a new script (`PickedFile.notice`: “… opened as a new script. Drafter saves only .fountain, .md, .markdown, and .txt files, so Save will ask where to put it.”), so Save asks instead of failing. A comment in `write` that overclaimed ("no link is followed between the check and the write") now says what is and isn't guarded.
- **2026-10-27** — **The menu bar is built in the main process from the page's command list, and every item runs the page's registered command** (Task 4; `src/menu.ts`, `src/menu-main.ts`, `apps/web/app/commands/desktop-menu.ts`). The page sends `{ id, title, group, keys, enabled }` for each built-in command (`menu.setCommands`), main lays out File, Edit, View, Go, and Help (`menuTemplate`) and sets the application menu, and a chosen item sends its id back (`menu.onCommand`); the page runs `commandById(id).run()` unless the command is off. No behaviour lives in the menu, so the menu, the palette, the keys, and the Drafter menu cannot disagree. The layout (which command goes in which section or submenu: Open Sample, Template, Export, Make, Sprint) is data in `menu.ts`, and a command it doesn't know still appears, at the end of its menu.
- **2026-10-27** — **Menu keys are shown, not registered** (`registerAccelerator: false`, with the accelerator set from the command's `keys` by `accelerator()`). The page's own handlers keep the keys (the registry's capture handler for the global ones, CodeMirror's keymap for the rest), so Mod+S saves once; Electron offers the key to the menu only if the page leaves it unhandled, and with `registerAccelerator: false` there is nothing to offer it to. Quit alone registers its key (Ctrl+Q): nothing in the page uses it. Not verified on macOS, where menu key equivalents are handled by the system before the page (Plan 6 Task 8, macOS).
- **2026-10-27** — **Enabled state is sent by the page, debounced, and polled.** `installDesktopMenu` sends the list at once and again (100 ms after a change) when a Vue watch on the commands and their `enabled()` sees one; it also looks every 500 ms for a change nothing reactive shows (`enabled()` reads the editor's state, `view.composing`, and open dialogs), and sends only if the list differs. Main rebuilds the menu only if the list differs from the last, and sets it with `Menu.setApplicationMenu`, which on Linux closes a menu that is open at that moment (rare: `enabled` changes with what you type, not while you read a menu). When the editor's page is destroyed, every command shows disabled until another sends its list. A command that runs from a menu while it is off (the menu is up to 600 ms behind) does nothing.
- **2026-10-27** — **What main takes from the page** (`parseCommands`): the sender must be an app page (`fromApp`), and the payload a list of at most 400 commands, each with a string id of 1 to 80 characters (once only), a title up to 200, one of the five groups, a boolean `enabled`, and up to four keys of up to 40 characters; only those fields are kept, and any other shape is ignored whole, leaving the menu as it was. Those limits bound the menu's size. `&` in a title is doubled, so a title cannot set a mnemonic. An accelerator Electron would not take makes `accelerator()` return nothing, and if building the menu still throws, it is built without keys.
- **2026-10-27** — **Script modules' commands are palette-only on the desktop for now.** Their group is the module's name, not one of the five menus, and they come and go at run time; the page sends only the built-in commands (`COMMAND_DATA` ids), so a module cannot add a menu item (or take a built-in one's place) by naming its group "File". A module submenu is a later, small change if wanted.

About Drafter is a native message box with the version and a "Visit drafter.dev" button (opened with `openExternal`, https only); Open the Releases Page opens `https://github.com/cschweda/drafter/releases` the same way. The e2e suite (unpackaged only) has About write what it says to `DRAFTER_E2E_ABOUT` and press the button. On a Mac About is in the app menu (`Drafter`), and File has no Quit.
- **2026-10-27** — **Quit is a plain item whose click calls `app.quit()`,** not Electron's `quit` role: on macOS a role's native handling ignores a programmatic click, and the plain item is the same everywhere and testable. Task 5's before-quit handler therefore sees every Quit from the menu. The close button and Ctrl+Q go the same way (`window-all-closed` calls `app.quit()`).

Alt+F/E/V/G/H open the menus; Alt alone or F10 puts focus on the bar (Electron's own behaviour). The app's keys are Mod+..., Ctrl+Alt+[ and ], and F8, and the editor's own Alt keys (Alt+Up/Down, Alt+L for Select line) are not menu letters, so no mnemonic takes a key the app uses. **Not verified on Linux:** Playwright sends keys to the page directly (it bypasses the menu), so only a real Linux keyboard (or xdotool in CI) can show that Alt+letter and Ctrl+Alt+1 to 7 behave; Task 7's CI should check Ctrl+Alt+N and Alt+F. If a mnemonic ever steals a key, remove the `&` from that menu.
- **2026-10-27** — **Cut, Copy, Paste, Select All, and Full Screen are Electron roles** (they are the platform's, with its keys); there is no Reload or Developer Tools item. Edit's Undo, Redo, and Find are Drafter's commands, which run the editor's.
- **2026-10-27** — **Task 3 review fixes.** (1) A command-line argument is said to be refused only if it is a script that isn't there, a script-named folder, or an existing file that isn't a script; a folder or a missing name with no script extension is left out in silence, because it is most likely the value of a switch (`--user-data-dir /x`). **Residual:** a switch's value that is an existing file is still read as a file (`--log-file a.png` is said to be refused; `--log-file notes.md` would open it), and Chromium's own switches use `=`, which has no such case. (2) After a bare `--` every argument is a path, so `-draft.fountain` opens; the `--` itself is not one. (3) The inbox forgets its page when the editor's window begins a navigation (`did-start-navigation`, main frame, not in the same document) and holds what arrives until the next page sends `openReady`, so a file named during a reload is not sent to a page that is going away. (4) `openReady` is accepted only from the editor's own window (`main.webContents`), not from a help window, which can't take the inbox over. (5) Several refusals are one message (`refusalsMessage`: the first three by name, then "and N more"), because the status bar shows one. (6) A grant is dropped on load only if its file is gone from a folder that is there **and has something in it**: an unmounted drive leaves its mount point behind, empty (`/media/me/DRIVE`, `/mnt/x`), which would otherwise drop the grants of everything on it (a folder the writer emptied keeps its grants too, which matters only if a file of that name comes back). Chosen over listing mount-point patterns, which differ by distribution and by Windows. (7) Old temp files (`.<name>.drafter-tmp-<12 hex>`, over a minute old) are cleared for any file in the folder, once per folder per run of the app (`sweptFolders`), not at every save; one an interrupted write leaves after that is cleared at the next start.
- **2026-10-27** — **Closing and quitting** (Task 5; `src/close.ts` the Electron half, `src/close-flow.ts` the rules, `apps/web/app/commands/desktop-close.ts` the page's half). Main stops the editor window's `close` and the app's `before-quit` (File → Quit, Ctrl+Q, `window-all-closed` aside) and asks the editor's page; with nothing to lose it closes at once, with something it shows the native box and the writer decides. **The page doesn't ask, main does, and the web's `beforeunload` question is off on the desktop** (`desktop()` in `script.ts`'s handler): a `beforeunload` that vetoes keeps `window.close()` from finishing, which hung `app.close()`. A reload on the desktop therefore no longer asks; the recovery copy (flushed on `pagehide`, plus the stash) restores what was typed. `will-prevent-unload` is ignored for the same reason. The bridge is `onCloseRequest(handlers)` with `state()`, `save()`, and `flush()`, not the brief's single `() => Promise<'close' | 'stay'>`, because the dialog is native (main shows it) and only the page can say whether it holds unsaved text and run the Save command. Main asks over `closeRequest`/`closeReply` (a request id, a reply that must come from the editor's main frame, `fromEditor`) and treats a page that reloaded, crashed, or went as an answer of nothing.
- **2026-10-27** — **What counts as unsaved on a close** is the web's own "would the browser ask" test (`script.wouldLose`, the old `asking`): changes to a script of the writer's own, or to anything while the recovery copy can't be written; never a sample (its edits are in the recovery copy) and never a read-only tab. Save is the page's Save command (`storage/save.ts`): a script with no file asks where through the same Save dialog, and the window closes only when the text reached a file (`'saved'`); `'cancelled'` or an error (the status bar says which) leaves it open. The recovery copy is flushed (`script.flush()`, bounded to 2 s) before the window goes in every case that closes it, after a Save too (a script saved to a file keeps its recovery copy current).
- **2026-10-27** — **A page that doesn't answer in 3 s** (it is hung, or still starting) is handled by whether it ever said it listens. A page that hasn't registered (`closeReady`: still loading, mid-reload, crashed, gone) closes at once, since a page that hasn't started holds nothing. A page that registered and then goes quiet gets a second box: “Drafter isn’t responding.” with only **Don't Save** and **Cancel** (Cancel the default), saying closing may lose the last few seconds of typing while the rest stays in the recovery copy. Save isn't offered (it needs the page); Don't Save then destroys the window (a hung renderer would never finish unloading) after trying the flush for 2 s. Chosen over closing silently (it could lose typing) and over waiting forever (the window could never be closed). The e2e suite shortens the 3 s with `DRAFTER_E2E_CLOSE_ASK_MS` (unpackaged only, 50 ms or more).
- **2026-10-27** — **No re-entrancy** (`CloseFlow`): a second close or quit while the box is up does nothing (the box is the answer); a quit during a Save waits for it and then quits again, which asks afresh if something is still unsaved (the Save failed or was cancelled) and otherwise just quits. A quit that arrived while only the box was up is not remembered: choosing Cancel there is the writer's answer to it.
- **2026-10-27** — **The OS ending the session** (SIGTERM, SIGINT, SIGHUP, `powerMonitor` `shutdown` on Linux and macOS, a window's `session-end` on Windows) asks nothing: the recovery copy is flushed (2 s at most) and the app quits (`app.exit(0)` after 3 s if a window won't go). A hung page loses only what it hadn't written. The shutdown and Windows paths are untested here (they need the OS); the signal path is tested.
- **2026-10-27** — **The e2e hook for the box** (`DRAFTER_E2E_CLOSE`, unpackaged only, like the Open/Save hook): a JSON array of `save`, `discard`, or `cancel`, each box shown logged to `<file>.log` and each question the page was asked to `<file>.requests`. **An empty queue answers Don't Save**, not Cancel, unlike the real box's default, because Playwright's `app.close()` is `app.quit()` and a test that ends with unsaved text would otherwise never exit. A test that cares asserts the log.
- **2026-10-27** — **Task 4 review fixes.** (1) The menu's command list is taken only from the editor window's own page, main frame (`fromEditor`: `event.sender === main.webContents && event.senderFrame === sender.mainFrame`), not from any app page: a help window that navigates to `/` (its “Back to Drafter” link when the opener is gone) is a full editor page that calls `installDesktopMenu`, and would otherwise have become the page menu clicks go to. The same check guards the close requests' ready and reply messages. `setUpMenu` takes the editor window as a getter, as `setUpClosing` does. (2) `parseCommands` refuses a list that names an id the menu has of its own (`quit`, `about`, `releases`), whole, like any other malformed list.
- **2026-10-27** — **Updates (Task 6).** (1) **Update source is pluggable** in `apps/web/app/update/state.ts`: the toast, Later, and the unsaved-changes question are the web's own; `updateKind` (`reload` | `restart` | `get`) picks the words and the last step. The service worker sets nothing (kind stays `reload`); `desktop-source.ts` sets it from the bridge. The bridge's `onAvailable` callback gets a second argument, `how` (`restart` or `get`), beyond the brief's `(version)`, so the page knows which button to show. (2) **AppImage** (`APPIMAGE` set on a packaged Linux build): `autoDownload` is on but only a check that found a newer version starts it; `autoInstallOnAppQuit` is off; "ready" is offered when the download is done, not when it is found. **Restart** runs the web's question, the page flushes the recovery copy, then the main process tells the close flow (`quitNow`) so it doesn't ask again, and calls `quitAndInstall(false, true)`. (3) **.deb** (packaged, no `APPIMAGE`): no download; the toast is "available" with **Get it**, which opens `https://github.com/cschweda/drafter/releases/latest` (`shell.openExternal`, https only). electron-updater's `DebUpdater` and its `pkexec` are not used: both modes use `AppImageUpdater`, which only checks and (AppImage) downloads; for a `.deb` it is let through its `APPIMAGE` test with `forceDevUpdateConfig`, and `downloadUpdate` is never called. (4) **One check**, `CHECK_DELAY_MS` (5 s) after the editor's first page has loaded, and only after the page has said Check for updates is on (`setEnabled`; the setting lives in the page's IndexedDB, so main learns it from the page). Off, or never said: no request at all, and no periodic checks. Failures are logged and silent. (5) **Unpackaged** (development): no check unless `DRAFTER_UPDATE_FEED` is a valid http(s) URL; the feed, `DRAFTER_E2E_UPDATE_DELAY_MS`, and `DRAFTER_E2E_UPDATE_INSTALL` (replaces `quitAndInstall` with a log line and a quit) are honoured only when `!app.isPackaged`. The test feed uses a config file written to userData (electron-updater needs `updaterCacheDirName` from a config) and turns differential downloads off. (6) **The network backstop** (`network.ts`) cancels only requests that have a `webContentsId`; the updater's requests (Electron `net`, from the main process) have none, so it needs no exception, and an e2e test checks that a page's own request to the same server is still cancelled. (7) **Packaged non-Linux builds** check nothing until their plans. (8) **Only the editor's page** may listen, enable, install, or open the release page (`fromEditor`); install rejects when nothing was downloaded, and the release page opens only after a `.deb` offer.
- **2026-10-27** — **Task 5 review fixes.** (1) `DesktopCloseState.recoverable` (false when the page's recovery state is `unavailable`); with it false the dialog's detail is "These changes are in no file and no recovery copy, so Don't Save loses them."; a page that omits the field is not believed (no promise). (2) Save has no time limit, but `CloseFlow` races it against the window's `unresponsive` event and the page going away (`watchPage`): unresponsive gives the "isn’t responding" box (Don’t Save flushes within 2 s and closes; Cancel stays), gone stays. (3) A main-frame navigation clears the listening link, so a close during a reload closes at once. (4) `PageLink` (now `page-link.ts`, no Electron import) removes its listeners when replaced. (5) `backToEditor` on the bridge: a desktop help window's "Back to Drafter" focuses the editor's window (opened again if gone) and closes the help window; the plain `href` stays for the web and no-JS. (6) CI note: the SIGTERM no-dialog check must run under xvfb on Linux (Electron's own POSIX handlers emit before-quit there).
- **2026-10-27** — **Fix round 1 (Tasks 5 and 6).** (1) **Quit approval is one-shot** (`Closing.allowQuit`): Restart approves the next quit; `before-quit` turns the approval into `leaving` only when a quit really starts. `Updates.install()` resolves `failed` (and revokes) if the updater emits `error`, `quitAndInstall` throws, or the app still runs `INSTALL_WAIT_MS` (3 s) later; the IPC then rejects `install-failed` and the toast says the install failed. (2) **AppImage is proven, not assumed** (`runsFromAppImage`): `APPIMAGE` an existing file, `APPDIR` set, `process.execPath` inside `APPDIR`; the unpackaged test feed still plays an AppImage by `APPIMAGE` alone. (3) **A second close or quit during a Save** probes the page with `state` (3 s); no answer ends the Save as hung and shows the "isn’t responding" box; a page that answers (a Save As dialog is open) is left alone. (4) **autoDownload is off everywhere**; a found version starts `downloadUpdate(token)` (AppImage only) and `setEnabled(false)` cancels it. (5) The dialog says "Don't Save" with a straight apostrophe, as its button does. (6) **Known and accepted for now:** a help window can still become an editor through an in-page `app://` link before the page hydrates (pre-existing); its Back link now closes it once hydrated.
- **2026-10-27** — **Packaging is electron-builder 26.18.0 (exact), configured in `apps/desktop/electron-builder.config.cjs`.** `pnpm desktop:dist` builds the main process, the web build, and the packages into `apps/desktop/release/` (not `dist/`, which tsdown cleans): `Drafter-x86_64.AppImage` (one name for every version; see the entry on the AppImage's name), `Drafter-<version>-linux-amd64.deb`, and `latest-linux.yml` (sha512 of both, for the updater; it also puts `app-update.yml` in the app). electron-builder 26 works from the pnpm workspace as it is (it finds `electron` in `apps/desktop`), so there is no `.npmrc` change and no `pnpm` setting; the app has no runtime dependencies yet, so it packs only `dist/main.cjs`, `dist/preload.cjs`, `web/` (without `og-image.png`), and `package.json` into `app.asar`. x64 only: the AppImage is 127 MB and the `.deb` 103 MB, as in the spike. arm64 is left for later (it needs an arm64 runner to be tested, `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, and the builder side is one more entry in `arch`).
- **2026-10-27** — **Names.** Binary `drafterdev`, product Drafter, app id `dev.drafter.app`, the `.deb` package `drafterdev` (`drafter` is the CLI's), installed in `/opt/Drafter` with `/usr/bin/drafterdev` through `update-alternatives`. The desktop entry is `drafter.desktop` (`desktopName` in package.json and `syncDesktopName`, so it matches the window class), with `Categories=Office;TextEditor;`, `MimeType=text/x-fountain;text/markdown;`, and `Exec=… %U` so files open through argv. File associations: `.fountain` as `text/x-fountain` (new to the system, so the package carries the MIME definition), `.md` and `.markdown` as `text/markdown` (the system has it). The icon is `brand/icon-512.png`, used in place; no image dependency and no copies.
- **2026-10-27** — **Ubuntu 24.04 and the sandbox: what CI found.** On `ubuntu-24.04` runners `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns` is 1, as on a user's machine. (1) The **`.deb`** runs sandboxed under it: electron-builder's own `after-install` script puts an AppArmor profile in `/etc/apparmor.d/drafterdev` (`profile drafterdev /opt/Drafter/drafterdev flags=(unconfined) { userns, }`) and loads it, and the renderers have seccomp mode 2 and no `--no-sandbox`. We use that profile as it is. (2) The **AppImage**'s `AppRun` (electron-builder generates it) tests `unshare -Ur true`, and when that fails it **adds `--no-sandbox` itself**, so on 24.04's default an AppImage would run every renderer unsandboxed with no message. No profile can fix that, because the test runs in the launcher's bash, and the launcher offers no way to turn the test off. (3) `app.enableSandbox()` **hides `--no-sandbox` from `app.commandLine`** (`hasSwitch` is false) while the switch still reaches Chromium's children, so Task 1's test that nothing passed it was vacuous; it now also reads `process.argv`. (4) Playwright's Electron driver adds `--no-sandbox` on Linux unless `chromiumSandbox: true`, which `e2e/helpers.ts` now sets.
- **2026-10-27** — **A packaged app with the sandbox off explains, recommends the .deb, and asks once** (`src/sandbox.ts`, `askAboutSandbox` in `main.ts`; replaces this section's first version, which exited). `--no-sandbox` in argv or on the command line, or `ELECTRON_DISABLE_SANDBOX` set to anything (empty too), in a packaged app: after the single-instance lock, a message box (and stderr) says the sandbox is off, why (the AppImage launcher does it where user namespaces are blocked, as on Ubuntu 24.04 and later), that the .deb runs sandboxed, and points to `docs/desktop-install-linux.md`; the buttons are **Continue without the sandbox** and **Quit** (the default). Continue writes `sandbox-continue-<reason>` in userData, keyed to the reason (the switch, or the environment variable), and starts the app; the next launch for that reason doesn't ask. Quit exits 0; a failure of the dialog exits 1. `DRAFTER_ALLOW_NO_SANDBOX=1` skips the question (and leaves no marker); no other value does. An unpackaged app (development) is left alone. The AppImage's desktop entry passes no `--no-sandbox` either (`appImage.executableArgs` is empty). Remembering the answer means a later launch with the sandbox off is silent; that is the controller's ruling (D8), and the file is the writer's to delete. CI checks the question (stderr text, the process alive, no `app://` page, no renderer), Continue (by leaving the marker in the test's HOME), another reason's marker not counting, the opt-out, and the AppImage on the restricted runner; the button click itself is not driven (a native dialog under xvfb).
- **2026-10-27** — **Fuses.** The packaged binary has RunAsNode, EnableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable, and EnableNodeCliInspectArguments off, EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation and OnlyLoadAppFromAsar on, and GrantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges and LoadBrowserProcessSpecificV8Snapshot off (`electronFuses`). EnableCookieEncryption stays off: the app keeps nothing in cookies (IndexedDB and localStorage), and turning it on is one-way and needs a keyring. Asar integrity validation is only enforced on macOS and Windows; on Linux the fuse is set for when those builds arrive and does nothing now. RunAsNode off means `process.fork` doesn't work in the main process (use utility processes); nothing here forks. The tests read the fuse wire out of the binary (the `.deb`'s and the unpacked build's), and start the packaged app with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, `NODE_OPTIONS=--inspect=0 --require /nonexistent`, `--inspect`, `--inspect-brk`, and `-e`: it starts as the app, runs no script, and prints no "Debugger listening". **Chromium's `--remote-debugging-port` is not covered by any fuse** and still works on the packaged binary; the packaged tests use it. Only a person who can start the program with their own arguments can use it, which is also a person who could run anything else as themselves.
- **2026-10-27** — **The packaged tests drive the app over CDP, not with Playwright's Electron driver,** because that driver needs `--inspect`, which the fuses switch off. `e2e/packaged.spec.ts` (`pnpm e2e:packaged`, its own config; the default config ignores it) starts the binary with `--remote-debugging-port=0`, reads the endpoint from stderr, and connects with `chromium.connectOverCDP`. The sandbox is read from `/proc`: no process in the tree has `--no-sandbox`, and every renderer has seccomp mode 2 and `no_new_privs`. Processes are found by parent links and by the launch's own HOME in their environment, since Chromium's helpers rewrite their command lines. The `opens a file from the command line` test skips itself until the shell has files (Task 3).
- **2026-10-27** — **The workflow** (`.github/workflows/desktop.yml`). On pushes to `main` and `phase-1-desktop`, tags `v*`, and by hand; never on pull requests. It builds, packages, and tests the packages with the restriction on (the `.deb`, then the AppImage's question), then lifts it (`sysctl`) for the AppImage and the shell's own tests, whose Electron comes from `node_modules` and has no AppArmor profile. Packaging and the packaged tests run on every push, not only on tags, so a broken package shows up before a release. Third-party actions are pinned to full commit SHAs; the Electron and electron-builder downloads are cached. A tag must equal `apps/desktop/package.json`'s version or the run fails. The `publish` job (only for a tag, after `linux` passes) is the only one with `contents: write`; it makes the tag's release as a draft, fills and checks it, and publishes it (see Releasing, below).
- **2026-10-27** — **The AppImage uses electron-builder's default (FUSE 2) runtime,** which needs `libfuse2` (`libfuse2t64` on Ubuntu 24.04, not installed by default). The newer static runtime (`toolsets.appimage` 1.0.x) would not need it, but it is newer and its launcher has the same `--no-sandbox` test; revisit when the AppImage is next looked at. Left alone for the first release.
- **2026-10-27** — **Releasing: a draft is made, filled, checked, and only then published** (`.github/scripts/publish-release.sh`). The person pushes the tag; the `publish` job (after `linux` passes) runs the script: it checks that `latest-linux.yml` agrees with the files (`apps/desktop/scripts/check-update-feed.mjs`: every url exists in the folder, sha512 and size match, the AppImage is there, `path` is one of the files), makes the release as a draft or reuses the tag's draft, uploads with `--clobber`, checks each file is on the release as an uploaded asset of the same size, and then `gh release edit --draft=false` (`--prerelease` when the tag contains `-`). A release already published is only refreshed and checked. Running it again does no harm. Tried against this repository with a throw-away tag: draft made, files uploaded, listing checked, editing a draft by tag works, a corrupted file is refused. The same feed check runs after packaging, and `scripts/check-asar.mjs` lists `app.asar` and fails on source maps, tests, scripts, `node_modules`, the share image, or a missing bundle or web build.
- **2026-10-27** — **The .deb's dependencies are electron-builder's defaults,** which it keeps current for the Electron it packs (the list written by hand earlier lacked `libgbm1` and `libasound2`). The desktop entry's `StartupWMClass` is compared in CI with the class of the real window (`xwininfo -root -tree`) of the installed app.
- **2026-10-27** — **Sandbox advice is narrow (fix round 2).** The question's text recommends the .deb and points to `docs/desktop-install-linux.md`; it no longer gives the `sysctl` that turns the restriction off for the whole system, which the doc now only mentions as something Drafter doesn't suggest (and never as something to make permanent). The AppImage's narrow fix is an AppArmor profile for the AppImage's own path (`profile drafter-appimage <path> flags=(default_allow) { userns, }`): CI loads it on the 24.04 runner with the restriction still on and the packaged AppImage launches and runs sandboxed (renderers at seccomp 2, no `--no-sandbox`), because the launcher's own `unshare` test runs under the profile too. The profile names one file, so it must follow the AppImage when it moves (an update keeps the path, so it needn't follow an update). The question also names the file where "Continue" is remembered (its full path), and a failure to write it (`rememberAnswer` returns false) doesn't stop the app: it starts, and asks again next time.
- **2026-10-27** — **A published release is never uploaded to** (fix round 2, `publish-release.sh`). If the tag's release is already published, the script compares its assets with the local files (`scripts/check-release-assets.mjs`: name, size, state, and the API's `sha256:` digest when it gives one) and fails if they differ; only a draft is uploaded to, then compared the same way, then published by id (`gh api -X PATCH … -F draft=false -F prerelease=…`). The release is found with `jq --arg`, a published one before a draft. Tried against this repository with a throw-away tag: draft path, then the published path (the listing altered to say published): same files pass without any upload, a changed file of the same size is refused by its digest.
- **2026-10-27** — **The release procedure is written down** (final review): set the versions and the changelog section on `main`, push the tag, let `desktop.yml` create, fill, check, and publish the release, then push `main` (which deploys drafter.dev, whose download link goes to the latest release), and only then edit its notes (README, *Contributing*, **Releasing**). A release made by hand first, if published, would never get its packages (the script refuses to upload to a published release), and the desktop apps would see a release with no `latest-linux.yml`.
- **2026-10-27** — **The spellcheck dictionaries go into `app.asar`** (`files` has `dictionaries/**/*`), where the main process looks for them (`join(app.getAppPath(), 'dictionaries')`, copied into `<userData>/Dictionaries` at start). Electron's asar-aware `fs` reads and copies from the archive (tried), so they aren't unpacked. `scripts/check-asar.mjs` requires a `dictionaries/*.bdic` in the package whenever `apps/desktop/dictionaries/` exists in the source, and `packaged.spec.ts` checks that a launch seeds the profile's `Dictionaries` folder, under the same condition, so both are inactive until the spellchecker is merged and then apply on their own.
- **2026-10-27** — **The download is described on the Saving page and in the README, and links to GitHub, not to a file.** The Saving page's section links to `releases/latest` and to the install guide on `main` (the page is prerendered, so it needs no script and adds no inline script or network request); it names no version or file, so it doesn't go stale between releases. It says what the update check does only as far as the app does it ("checks for a new version when it starts, you can turn this off, and asks before installing"). The page shows the section inside the desktop app too, where its links open in the system browser; hiding it there would mean a script on a prerendered page, which isn't worth a paragraph.
- **2026-10-27** — **The lanes' merge (Plan 6).** (1) **The desktop entry passes file paths:** the `.deb`'s Exec is `/opt/Drafter/drafterdev %F` (`deb.executableArgs: ['%F']`; electron-builder otherwise appends `%U`). The AppImage's entry is `AppRun %U`, which electron-builder writes itself and won't let a config change, so the app also takes a local `file://` URL on its command line as the file's path (`src/open-files.ts`); other URLs and other hosts are ignored. (2) **One extension per file association:** an `ext` list (`['md', 'markdown']`) becomes the single glob `*.md,markdown` in the `.deb`'s MIME file, which matches nothing; `.md` carries `text/markdown`, and `.markdown` none of its own (shared-mime-info already maps it). CI checks the MIME file and that `xdg-mime` types a `.fountain` file `text/x-fountain`. (3) **The packaged app's `node_modules` is exactly electron-updater's dependency closure** (sixteen packages), each with its licence file; `scripts/check-asar.mjs` computes the closure from `package.json` and fails on anything else, on a missing licence (`lazy-val`, which ships none, is named), and on a dictionary without `README_en_US.txt`. (4) **`pnpm build:web` swaps `web/` whole:** it copies into `web.next-<pid>`, then renames the old folder aside and the new one in, and deletes the old; a copy that fails leaves the old build. A launch during a rebuild finds a complete build (before, the folder was cleared and refilled, and a launch in between found no `_headers` and refused to start for want of its content security policy). (5) **`app.setName('Drafter')`** before anything reads the name, so however the app is started its profile folder, the macOS Hide/Quit items, and About say Drafter (package.json's `productName` already did for `electron .`); the unpackaged macOS app's bold menu-bar title still says Electron, from Electron.app's own bundle, and only a packaged build changes it. (6) **Real keys in CI:** a packaged test sends Alt+F, Enter and Ctrl+Alt+N through the X server with xdotool (XTEST, after `windowfocus`, as there is no window manager under xvfb) to the installed `.deb`: Alt+F, Enter must make a script (the File menu opened with New screenplay selected; had the keys reached the page, Enter would be a new line), and Ctrl+Alt+N exactly one more. (7) **SIGTERM on the packaged app** (the `.deb`): it quits within seconds with no question and the text is there at the next start (the AppImage is skipped: its launcher stands between the signal and the app). (8) **The spellcheck dictionary is copied into the profile before `ready`**, not at it: an strace of the packaged app in CI showed Chromium's spellchecker opening `<userData>/Dictionaries/en-US-10-2.bdic` on its own thread as soon as the session existed, while the copy was still being written, so a first start had no dictionary and asked to download one (refused). The file name and folder were right; only the timing was wrong. (9) `fs.promises.realpath` (the protocol's containment check) works on paths inside `app.asar` (tried with Electron 45 on macOS; the packaged tests' "launches, and shows the editor" confirm it on Linux).
- **2026-10-27** — **A right-click menu in the editor's window** (final review, `src/context-menu.ts`; `main.ts` shows it on the editor's `context-menu` event, never in a help window). On a misspelled word: up to five of Chromium's suggestions (`replaceMisspelling`), or a disabled *No Suggestions*, then *Add to Dictionary* (`session.addWordToSpellCheckerDictionary`), then a separator; in an editable place Cut, Copy, Paste, Select All as Electron roles, each enabled as `editFlags` says; elsewhere Copy and Select All only when there is a selection, or no menu. The template is built without Electron so it is unit-tested; an unpackaged app keeps the last menu (`drafterShell.contextMenu()`) so the e2e suite can choose from it, since a native popup can't be clicked by Playwright.
- **2026-10-27** — **The first New of a kind opens with its sample (the maintainer's decision).** `newScript(kind)` asks `metSample(kind, projects)` (`storage/first-samples.ts`) inside the switch; if the writer hasn't met that kind's sample, the new project's text and baseline are the sample's (`SAMPLE_TEXT[kind]`), so it opens unchanged, and it carries `blank: { text, cursor }`, the new-document text New would have made. It is the writer's own project (no `sample`), so it is edited, saved, counted, and given a goal like any other, and the shared sample record is untouched. The cursor goes to the first scene heading for a screenplay (as on a first visit) and to the top for a stage play and a Markdown document, where their notes begin.
- **2026-10-27** — **"Met" is a settings row per kind, or a project that shows it.** `sample-seen:<kind>` with the value `true` is written once the sample has opened in a New (`rememberSample`; a failed write is let go); the `Settings` store ignores these rows, as it does the goals'. A kind is also met when this browser holds that sample (the first visit's screenplay, or one opened from Samples) or a document of that kind (a writer from before this change, or a row that couldn't be written), so nobody who already writes stage plays is handed the sample. A row that can't be read counts as met, so a returning writer whose storage is failing gets the usual blank document; a row with any other value is no answer, and the projects decide.
- **2026-10-27** — **A first New screenplay starts blank.** Every first visit opens the screenplay sample and stores it, so by the time a writer chooses New screenplay they have met it, and the screenplay sample project says so. A browser whose first visit kept typed text instead of the sample has a screenplay of the writer's own, which says the same. The rule is the same for every kind; it is the first visit that makes the screenplay's answer always "met".
- **2026-10-27** — **Start blank is one isolated change, and then the blank text is the baseline.** `startBlank()` replaces the whole text with `blank.text` in one `input.replace` transaction with `isolateHistory.of('full')` (as Reset sample does), puts the cursor where New would have, makes the blank text the baseline (so the emptied document is unchanged, like a new one), and clears `blank` in the copy held at once and in the stored copy in turn. One Undo brings the sample's text and anything typed in it back, and the document then counts as changed; the bar does not come back, since the offer is spent. `canStartBlank` is true only while `blank` is set, the document has no file (saved, it is plainly the writer's), and the tab can edit with no switch under way.
- **2026-10-27** — **The Starting text bar.** `StartBlankBar.vue` is in the top stack after the sample bar (the two never show together: one needs a sample, the other a document of the writer's own), a region named "Starting text", so it is on every tab of a phone. It says "Your first stage play starts with a sample that shows how one is written. It's yours: keep it, change it, or start blank." and has one **Start blank** button that runs the `start-blank` command; on a phone it is "Sample text" and the button. The command is in the palette (File) and in the Drafter menu's Help section before Reset sample, listed there only while it applies, so Reset sample stays the menu's last item.
- **2026-10-27** — **The Markdown sample is a sample like the others.** `SampleKind` gains `markdown` (`SAMPLES`: Screenplay, Stage play, Markdown, the document types' order); its project is made with `kind: 'markdown'`, Reset sample puts the cursor at the top (it has no scenes), and like the other samples its kind can't be changed (`canChooseType` refuses a Fountain type for a sample). The Open menu's Samples group is now drawn from `SAMPLES`. The sample's text is `samples/markdown.md`, imported raw like the Fountain ones.
- **2026-10-27** — **The browser tests start with the samples met.** Like the tour's "seen" key, `playwright.config.ts` restores a `drafter` database (Dexie's version 1, IndexedDB's 10, with the stores and indexes Dexie makes) holding the three `sample-seen:` rows, so the many tests that make a stage play or a Markdown document still get a blank one. `e2e/first-samples.spec.ts` starts with only the tour's key.
- **2026-10-27** — **Scroll sync maps through source offsets** (`editor/scroll-sync.ts`). Each pane says which source offset is at its top and puts an offset there. The Source uses CodeMirror's line blocks (`lineBlockAtHeight`, `lineBlockAt`), in proportion within a wrapped line. The Script pane uses its lines' `data-from`, which come from the layout's spans, and the Preview its blocks' `data-from`, which `renderMarkdown` now sets from the Markdown tree (the worker keeps a block's start as `from`, a number, and still drops `position`). An offset between two anchors stands between them in proportion, with the start of the text at the top and its end at the bottom, so a note or a section that doesn't print still has a place. Measured on the Script and the Preview with `getBoundingClientRect`, so CSS `zoom` (Fit width and 100%) is counted.
- **2026-10-27** — **One pane leads; the other's scroll events are its echo.** The Source leads whenever it scrolls, whatever moved it (the wheel, keys, typing, typewriter centring, a jump). The Script pane or the Preview leads only after the writer's own input on it: a wheel, a touch, a scrolling key, or a press (which covers its scroll bar, and a click on a line, whose jump in the Source is then not echoed back). Its other scrolling (the Script pane following the cursor) moves nothing. While one pane has led within the last 300 ms (`LEAD_MS`, renewed by each of its scroll events, so momentum keeps it), the other's scroll events are ignored, so a programmatic scroll can't come back as a scroll to answer. Work is one `requestAnimationFrame` per burst: read the leader's top, read a handful of anchors, write one `scrollTop`.
- **2026-10-27** — **Sync and the cursor-follow agree, instead of taking turns.** When the keys or the app moved the Source (not the wheel or a finger) and the cursor is in the Source's view, the other pane goes to the synced place moved as little as it takes to keep the cursor's line in view (`keepInView`, 40 px of room). So typing with typewriter scrolling still centres in the Source, the Script pane follows, and its existing reveal of the cursor's block has nothing to undo. The Script pane's reveal after each layout stays; its first drawing, the second after it while the font settles, and any change of scale (zoom buttons, text size, a resize at Fit width) are put in step with the Source instead when sync is on.
- **2026-10-27** — **A phone keeps the place across tabs.** With one pane showing there is no other pane to move, so the controller keeps the last leading top (`place`), and the Source's top when it is put away. A Script pane or Preview that opens is put there once drawn; a Source that opens after the writer scrolled the other pane is put there two frames after it mounts, once CodeMirror has measured it in its new place (after the existing scroll snapshot). Focus mode has no other pane, so nothing syncs in it; leaving it, the Script pane's first drawing is put in step.
- **2026-10-27** — **The setting has three doors and is on by default.** `scrollSync` in the settings table (`true` by default, validated like the other booleans): the Source bar's **Sync scrolling** button (`aria-pressed`) beside Line numbers and Typewriter, Settings → **Sync scrolling**, and the palette's **Turn on / Turn off scroll sync** (only the one that applies is listed). Turning it on puts the other pane in step at once.
- **2026-10-27** — **No layout thrash on a long script.** The Script pane's lines are listed once per drawing (`invalidate` after each), and a binary search measures only the dozen or so it lands on. In Chromium on this machine, scrolling the Source of a 121-page script 400 px at a time took 33.3 ms (two frames, median) per step with sync on and with it off, 35.3 ms against 33.5 ms at the 95th percentile. A CodeMirror line block's bottom edge counts as inside it (`lineBlockAtHeight`), so a top exactly on a line's bottom is that line's end, not the end of the text, which an early version took it for.
- **2026-10-27** — **Start blank is offered only on the untouched sample (review fix).** It had stayed with the project, so a sample the writer had rewritten into their own work still offered to empty it. `canStartBlank` now also needs `!dirty` (the baseline is the sample's text while `blank` is set), so the bar goes at the first keystroke; and the first recovery write whose text differs from that baseline clears `blank` in the same write (`writeSource`'s new `fields`), so the offer is gone for good, after an Undo and after a reload. A change of kind (`setKind`) and a template applied (`useTemplate().choose`, through `forgetBlank()`) clear it too, since the blank text was made for the other type. Clearing it in the held copy is at once; if the stored copy can't be written, the status bar and focus mode's marker say "Couldn't update this browser's copy. Start blank may be offered again." (`blankError`).
- **2026-10-27** — **Start blank is in the Drafter menu's File group,** after Revert to Saved and only while it applies, matching its palette group; it is no longer in Help.
- **2026-10-27** — **The first visit writes `sample-seen:screenplay`** when it opens the screenplay sample, so the row says what the projects already did.
- **2026-10-27** — **A press on the Script pane or the Preview leads until it is let go (review fix).** A press (`pointerdown`, which is how a scroll bar's thumb is taken) holds the lead until `pointerup` or `pointercancel` on the window, then for `LEAD_MS` as usual; a 300 ms grant from the press alone had handed the lead back to the Source when the writer paused on the thumb mid-drag, so the drag that followed moved nothing. The wheel, a finger, and the keys keep their 300 ms grant, renewed by each scroll.
- **2026-10-27** — **The Preview is put back in step after each drawing.** It has no cursor-follow of its own, so every re-render (each key in a short document, each pause in a long one) runs `placeView()`, which leaves it alone while the writer is scrolling it; the Script pane does this only for its first drawing and changes of scale, and otherwise follows the cursor.
- **2026-10-27** — **The seeded test database is checked against `db.ts`.** `drafterDatabase` moved to `e2e/seed.ts`, and `test/e2e-seed.test.ts` compares its name, version (`verno * 10`), stores, keys, and indexes with Dexie's own, so a new version of the database fails a unit test instead of every browser test.
- **2026-10-27** — **The loading screen's mark moves, by CSS alone** (`app/spa-loading-template.html`, the maintainer's request). The mark is inline SVG (brand/mark.svg's shapes, with its gradient's id made unique), so its six lines can be animated: each scales from its left edge and fades a little (`transform`, `opacity`), the left half then the right, in a 1.4 s loop, and a goldenrod (`#E5B04A`) drop shadow breathes around it. The two messages are in one `role="status"` paragraph that keeps its space from the start (no layout shift), each `visibility: hidden` until a zero-length animation with an `animation-delay` of 3 s or 15 s makes it visible, so no script is needed and the content security policy is unchanged; a `<noscript>` style hides them where the app can never load. Under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` the mark and its lines have no animation, and the words still appear (a change of visibility is not motion). The colours are the app's tokens, dark unless the color-mode script's class on `<html>` (the stored theme) is `light`, or, before that class exists, the system prefers light, so nothing flashes when the app mounts. A screen reader may not announce text revealed by CSS alone; the status is there to be read.
- **2026-10-27** — **No second loading state after mount.** The app has nothing empty to cover: `index.vue` draws the whole shell at once, and the editor already holds the screenplay sample in memory before the session has read IndexedDB (`ready`), so text shows the moment the loading screen goes; the Script pane says it is updating until the first layout. `e2e/loading.spec.ts` checks the editor has text as soon as the loading screen is gone.
- **2026-10-27** — **A press on the view can't hold the lead for ever (review fix).** Its end may never reach the page (a native scroll bar's drag in some browsers, a release outside the window, the window losing focus), which left the view leading and the Source unable to move it. Presses are now tracked by pointer id, and the hold ends when the last is let go, when the window loses focus or the page is hidden, when the writer turns to the Source (a press, the wheel, or a key there, which also gives the Source the lead at once), or `HOLD_MAX_MS` (3 s) after the view last scrolled during the hold. A hold that runs out leaves no lead behind; one that ends by a release or a lost focus leads for `LEAD_MS` as usual.
- **2026-10-27** — **The slow-start hint is "Taking longer than usual. Try reloading."** The desktop build uses the same loading template and loads nothing over the network, so "check your connection" would mislead there; one wording that holds in both is simpler than a build-time branch.
- **2026-10-27** — **The AppImage has one name for every version: `Drafter-x86_64.AppImage`** (the `.deb` keeps its version). electron-updater writes the new AppImage over the old file at the same path, but with a versioned name the release's file and the writer's file differ, and a launcher, an AppImage tool's menu entry, or the AppArmor profile above, which all name the file, would be left pointing at a name that no longer means the current version. With one name, a download, an update, and a profile all agree. Don't put the version back in the AppImage's name. The cost: the release page lists an AppImage without a version in its name; the release itself says which it is.
- **2026-10-27** — **Typewriter scrolling has no room above the text** (supersedes the half-pane above in Plan 5's typewriter entry). With it, turning the mode on at the start of a script showed an empty half-pane over the title page, which looked broken. The opening lines now sit at the top, and centering begins once the cursor is past the middle; the room below stays, so the end of a script, where a writer usually types, still reaches the middle.
- **2026-10-27** — **On a sample, a type of the other kind opens that type's sample** (`sampleFor` in `editor/use-template.ts`). A sample still never changes kind; Markdown on the screenplay or stage-play sample opens the Markdown sample, and a template on the Markdown sample opens that template's sample. Screenplay and Stage play on a Fountain sample still switch its template in place, as before.
- **2026-10-27** — **Writing stats reuse the daily goal's day start** (docs/drafter-stats-design.md §2). The goal already keeps what each script held when the day began (`day:<id>:<date>`, for every script of the writer's own, goal or none), so there is one record of it; the goal exposes it as `dayStart`. The writing-time row is `stats-day:<id>:<date>` → `{ ms, words, pages }`, but `words` and `pages` are the day's **growth** (the counts at the last write, less the day's start), not the start itself: the goal's day rows are pruned after 30 days, and the history needs each day's growth for good. Rows are kept without a time limit and removed with the script (`removeProject`). A day with growth but no writing time (a file reloaded) is not a day written.
- **2026-10-27** — **Every stretch counts at least 30 seconds, not only a one-edit stretch.** The design gives a single edit 30 seconds; read literally, two keys a second apart would count 1 second and one key 30. So a stretch counts from its first edit to its last or 30 seconds, whichever is more, and the 30 never reaches past the edit that starts the next stretch (after a hidden tab that can be sooner than the 2-minute gap). The time is credited from the stretch's start and split at local midnight as it falls (`stats/writing-time.ts`).
