- **2026-10-27** — **The writer's edits are told from the app's by a new `onEdit` hook on the editor session**, which hands listeners each update's transactions; `writersEdit` (in `stats/writing.ts`) counts a text change with an `input`, `delete`, `undo`, `redo`, or `move` user event, and not one marked `appChange` or one with no user event (a load). Undoing an app change is the writer's act and counts. (Revised after review the same day: see below. `input.replace` is no longer excluded.)
- **2026-10-27** — **Writing time is written at most every 5 seconds** (`WRITE_MS`, timed from the first unwritten edit), when the page is hidden (`visibilitychange`) or closed (`pagehide`), and when another script opens. The status bar's time changes only when the minute it shows does, or on a write, so a key costs the status bar nothing. A switch of script writes the old script's growth as last measured on its own text: the next script's text is loaded before it becomes the open one, so counts after a new load (`editor.loaded`) are never measured against the old script's start.
- **2026-10-27** — **Characters are one name whatever their capitals**, shown as first typed, so a forced `@McCLANE` and a later `MCCLANE` are one row; extensions and the dual `^` are already outside the parsed name. Every cue is a speech, one with only a parenthetical under it too (no words); words of dialogue leave out parentheticals and notes. A speech before the first scene heading is in no scene. The table is sorted by speeches, then words, then who spoke first.
- **2026-10-27** — **The scene heading split has four places and three times.** `INT.` is interior; `EXT.` and `EST.` (an establishing shot is an outside view) are exterior; `INT./EXT.`, `EXT./INT.`, `I/E`, and `E/I` are "Interior and exterior" (the parser reads `EXT./INT.` as `EXT.` and a place beginning `/INT.`, so it is read from the heading's text); no prefix (a forced `.` heading) is other. The time is the first part after the place, split at ` - `, that begins with DAY or NIGHT, so `NIGHT (1987)`, `NIGHT - CONTINUOUS`, and `DAY - LATER` are read, and a place's own words (`DAY CARE`) are not; `DAWN`, `MOMENTS LATER`, and a missing time are other. Shown for the Screenplay template only, as the design says; a stage play has no INT./EXT. headings.
- **2026-10-27** — **Nothing is dropped from the status bar on a narrow screen.** The design allowed dropping the time item; the bar already wraps onto a second line, and the stats tests see both items on the two phone sizes they run, so both stay.
- **2026-10-27** — **Show stats sits beside Settings… in the Drafter menu**, in that group, since the menu has no View section and Stats isn't help; in the desktop app's menu bar it is in View, after Sprint. The window's figures for the script (pages, characters, headings) are shown on a sample too; Today and the last 30 days say "Stats are kept for your own scripts, not the samples." The window takes focus itself when it opens, so it starts at its top rather than scrolled down to Close, and focus goes back to where it was (the Counts button, when opened from the panel).
- **2026-10-27** — **The 30-day chart is one series: net words a day, as bars on a zero line**, growth in the accent color above it and a cutting day in the muted color below it, each bar's tooltip giving the date, the words, and the writing time. Time isn't a second series: two measures on one chart would need two scales. The SVG is hidden from assistive technology, and a visually hidden table (day, words added, writing time) carries the same numbers. Reading time for Markdown is 230 words a minute, to the nearest minute.
- **2026-10-27** — **Removing the open script is final for its writing stats (review fix).** Removal deletes the `stats-day:` rows and only then opens the next script, which used to write the removed script's last stretch back. A write now checks, in the same transaction, that the script is still in `projects`, and writes nothing for one that isn't; this also covers a script removed in another tab. `e2e/stats.spec.ts` removes the open script and checks no row is left.
- **2026-10-27** — **A stage play's scenes are its `##` sections (review fix).** A scene begins where the script's own template says: a block whose role is `scene_heading` (the screenplay's headings) or `scene_heading_play` (the stage play's depth-2 sections), from `resolveRole` (`sceneStart` in `stats/script-stats.ts`). The character table's Scenes and the Scenes figure use it, and so does `countScript`, so the Counts panel's Scenes for a stage play is no longer 0. In a stage play an INT. heading is not a scene (its template disables it); in a screenplay a `##` section is an outline marker.
- **2026-10-27** — **Find and Replace is writing; Drafter's whole-text changes are marked as its own (review fix).** CodeMirror's search replaces with the `input.replace` and `input.replace.all` user events, the same event the app's four whole-text replacements used, so excluding the event left a writer's replacements uncounted. Reset sample, Start blank, a file's text put in, and Revert to Saved now also carry `appChange` (typewriter scrolling already ignored them), and `writersEdit` excludes only `appChange`.
- **2026-10-27** — **Smaller review fixes to writing stats.** A burst stops at the midnight after its stretch's last edit, so a key at 23:59:45 doesn't make the next day one written. A write that fails hands its time back to the tracker (`giveBack`), and the next write tries again. The streak counts back over every kept day, not only the 30 shown. The chart's geometry is `chartBars` (`stats/history.ts`): a written day with no growth is a 1-unit sliver above the zero line, which never sits at the very top. The Stats window's contents are drawn only while it is open. Closing it gives focus back to what opened it, or, when that is gone (the palette's input, a menu item), to the editor when the Source shows. A read-only tab's window says another tab is editing the script and counts its time there, and shows the kept days, which `days()` now reads for a read-only tab too. The end of the `stats-day:` key range is written as an escape.
- **2026-10-27** — **Known, left for now: the last edits before midnight can miss yesterday's row.** Growth is stored when a write happens; edits after the last write before midnight (at most 5 s of them, or more in a window left open without being hidden) are taken into the goal's new start of the day instead of yesterday's growth. Writing at the midnight tick would close it, but the goal and the stats each have their own midnight timer, and making them run in order is a larger change than the loss (a few seconds of words, on a rare night) is worth now.
- **2026-10-27** — **Known, left for now: words and pages today include Drafter's own changes.** A file reloaded after an outside edit, Revert to Saved, or a template switch moves the day's growth, because growth is the counts now less the day's start, exactly as the daily goal measures it (design §3: "as the goal does"). Keeping the two numbers the same matters more than excluding these; excluding them would mean tracking each change's word count, for the goal too.
- **2026-10-27** — **Known, left for now: a Markdown↔Fountain switch can leave an earlier `pages` in today's row.** The growth is merged into the row, and a Markdown document's growth has no pages, so the row keeps the pages it had. Nothing shows a past day's pages yet; replacing the field instead of merging is the fix when something does.

## The modules folder (`apps/desktop`, `apps/web`)

Plan: [`docs/plans/2026-10-27-desktop-modules-folder.md`](plans/2026-10-27-desktop-modules-folder.md); design §6.

- **2026-10-27** — **Who a folder module is lives in `module.json`; its code in `index.js`.** Settings must show a module's name, description, and network origins before the writer turns it on, and "off until turned on" means its code hasn't run, so what consent needs is JSON the main process reads without running anything. `index.js` runs only once the module is on, and its default export repeats the `id`; a mismatch is refused. This differs from design §6's "an `index.js` that default-exports a `ScriptModule`" in form only: the export is the folder counterpart of `ScriptModule` (`action`, `commands`, `tab`, `settings`), with `commands` a list (each run gets its own context) and a tab or settings drawn with `mount(root, ctx)` into the frame's DOM, since a frame can't take a Vue component from the page.
- **2026-10-27** — **Each folder module runs in `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">`, whose origin is opaque, served from `drafter-module://<id>/`.** Without `allow-same-origin` the frame has no storage, cookies, or access to any other frame; the URL's host still names the folder, which is how the main process knows what to serve. Up to three frames per module that is on: a hidden one (its button and commands, and the report of what it offers), its tab's, and its settings'. Each loads the module afresh; they share state only through `ctx.storage`. The scheme is privileged (standard, secure, fetch, CORS; never `bypassCSP`); its files carry `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` because an opaque origin's module scripts are cross-origin requests. Another module can't use that to read them: its frame's CSP allows only its own folder.
- **2026-10-27** — **The frame's CSP is made per module by the main process:** its own folder for scripts, styles, images, fonts, and fetches; `connect-src` adds only the origins the writer agreed to; `frame-ancestors app://drafter`; `form-action 'none'`; and `sandbox allow-scripts`, so it is sandboxed even if the attribute were dropped. The app page's own policy gets `frame-src drafter-module:` from the desktop shell, and only when a module was loaded; drafter.dev's `_headers` is not changed.
- **2026-10-27** — **Origins in `permissions.network` are checked by a strict grammar, not only the URL parser.** `https://` and a lowercase host name (labels of letters, digits, and inner hyphens), an IPv4 address, or a bracketed IPv6 address, an optional port of 1–65535, and nothing else; `http://` only to `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, or `[::1]`, for a program on the writer's own computer. The value must also equal what the URL parser gives as its origin. The parser alone keeps `*` in a host (`https://*.example.com` compares equal to its origin), and an origin goes into the frame's CSP, where `*` is a wildcard and `;`, `,`, or a space would end or split the list; `moduleCsp` also drops anything that isn't such an origin. (From an automated security review.)
- **2026-10-27** — **The network for a folder module is allowed only from its own frame, and only to origins it declares and the writer agreed to.** The page sends `modules.setNetwork(id, origins)` when a module turns on (and none when it turns off), and its frames load only after that; the main process keeps the intersection with `module.json`'s list, puts it in that module's frame CSP, and lets the `webRequest` backstop pass a request only when its frame (`details.frame.url`) is that module's and its origin is one of them. Every other request a page makes is still refused, the app page's included.
- **2026-10-27** — **A folder module never shares state with a built-in module.** Its id in the app is `folder.<id>`: a built-in module's id has no dot, so their switches, network consent, storage rows (`module:folder.<id>:…`), tabs, commands, and problems can't meet. A folder module that takes a built-in's id is refused twice: by the main process at start (the built-in ids are baked into `main.cjs` by tsdown from the repository's `modules/` folder and the test module, `scripts/built-in-modules.mjs`, so the list can't drift; it is neither listed, served, nor given the network), and by the page where the list comes in (`setFolderInfos`), so no part of the page ever sees it. A folder module whose declared origins grew since it was turned on is off until the writer agrees again, as a built-in's is. (From an automated security review.)
- **2026-10-27, revised after the security review** — **A different module put in the folder under an id that was on does *not* stay on.** The earlier reasoning (it could reach nothing the earlier one couldn't) was wrong: a module that is on can read and change the script, so a swapped-in module inheriting that is a real escalation (security review I1). When the folder's modules arrive at start, Drafter drops the stored consent of any `folder.<id>` that isn't loaded now, or whose module changed — the consent carries a fingerprint of the folder name and the module's `module.json`, and a mismatch (or an absent module) turns it off, so the writer must turn it on again. This runs before any folder module's frame can load (`reconcileFolderConsent` in `folder.ts`, before `setFolderInfos`). A full fingerprint of `index.js` (so an edited `index.js` with an unchanged `module.json` also re-asks) is still the maintainer's call; today a change to the code alone, with the same `module.json`, keeps consent.
- **2026-10-27** — **The RPC is closed and bounded.** The page hears a frame only when `event.source` is that frame's window and `event.origin` is `'null'`; the runtime hears only `window.parent` from `app://drafter`, so a module that posts to another module's frame is ignored. Only `ModuleContext`'s calls exist; arguments are checked and copied by call; a message over 4 MB, a 65th call waiting at once, more than 10 000 changes or 5 000 problems, a message over 2 000 characters, or a stored value that isn't JSON or is over 1 MB is refused with a sentence saying so. A frame that hasn't said it is ready within 15 s, or a run that takes longer than 5 minutes, is reported and ended. `doc()` crosses as `DocJSON` (`toJSON`), the plain data `drafter build --json` writes.
- **2026-10-27** — **A folder module's icon is always the puzzle.** Icons are bundled when the app is built, and the page can fetch none.
- **2026-10-27** — **The folder is read once, when the app is ready, before the window opens.** Restart to add, remove, or update a module; the scheme serves exactly the folders read. At most 50 modules, a `module.json` of at most 64 KB, an `index.js` that is a regular file inside its folder (its real path checked), and files of at most 20 MB. Loose files and folders whose names start with a dot are passed over in silence; every other folder that isn't a module is listed in Settings with why.
- **2026-10-27** — **Help → Open Modules Folder** makes the folder if it is missing and shows it with `shell.openPath`; an unpackaged app run by the e2e suite writes the path to `DRAFTER_E2E_OPENED_FOLDER` instead. Its id, `modules-folder`, is one of the menu's own, which the page's commands can't take.
- **2026-10-27** — **The folder module host ships in drafter.dev's bundle, inert.** `registry.ts` imports `folder.ts`, so its few kilobytes are in the web build; with no desktop bridge the list is empty, no frame is drawn (the runners are a lazy component behind `isDesktopBuild()`), and the CSP is unchanged.
- **2026-10-27** — **The frame runtime** (`apps/desktop/src/module-runtime.ts`) is built by tsdown as a browser ES module to `dist/module-runtime.js`, packaged with the app (electron-builder `files`, and `check-asar.mjs` requires it), and served at `drafter-module://<id>/__drafter__/runtime.js`. It imports the message types from `apps/web/app/modules/folder-protocol.ts`, which is pure, so the two ends share one definition.


### After the security review (2026-10-27)

- **2026-10-27** — **WebRTC is closed for every renderer, since a module could otherwise exfiltrate the script over ICE** (STUN, or TURN over UDP or TCP) past the CSP and the webRequest backstop, whatever network the writer agreed to (security review C1). `RTCPeerConnection` can't be removed robustly (a `srcdoc` child frame gets a fresh one; `disableBlinkFeatures` and `--disable-webrtc` left the constructor in place in Electron 45), so it is closed at the network layer, in two parts, both proven against a non-loopback address: `setWebRTCIPHandlingPolicy('disable_non_proxied_udp')` on every webContents (`web-contents-created`) stops all UDP; and a proxy on the renderer session (`http://127.0.0.1:9`, which refuses every connection) stops TURN over TCP, with the hosts a module was granted bypassed so a consented `fetch` still works. The updater keeps its own session (electron-updater's `electron-updater` partition), so updates are unaffected. A module reaches the network only through the origins the writer agreed to; `webrtc.ts` holds the proxy rules. Residual, documented: a module could open a TCP connection (TURN) to a host some on-module was granted, since the proxy bypass is per session, not per frame — a consented destination, not an arbitrary one. Fully per-frame scoping would need each module in its own partitioned session (the review's option 1), deferred.
- **2026-10-27** — **A folder module's name may not be, or start with, “Drafter”**, so its notices and its confirm dialog can't pass as Drafter's own (security review minor 1). Its notices are also prefixed with its name in the toast.
- **2026-10-27** — **Defense-in-depth from the review, now in place:** a CSP on every `drafter-module://` response, the runtime and 404s included (minor 11); the modules folder skips a module folder that group or others can write on POSIX (minor 10); a message from a frame must be plain JSON data, not just small as JSON (minor 3); each module has a share of the browser's storage it can't exceed (4 MB, 500 keys; minor 4); the background frame may `goto`, `confirm`, `applyChanges`, or `openTab` only inside a run the writer started (minor 5); `goto` takes a whole number (minor 6); only the first `ready` per frame is taken (minor 7); and error text a frame controls is capped before it reaches a toast (minor 2).
- **2026-10-27, second review round** — **The WebRTC proxy bypass is qualified with scheme and port**, so a host that wasn't granted can never be reached over WebRTC, and a granted host is reachable only on its exact scheme's port. A bypass reads `https://host:443` (or `http://localhost:port`), and a TURN-over-TCP connection isn't an https request, so it doesn't match the rule and stays refused by the proxy — proven against a non-loopback address (the probe makes an HTTP fetch to the bypassed host:port succeed while a TURN-TCP connection to the *same* host:port is refused). The bypass is still session-wide, but a module reaches only its own origins over HTTP because each frame's CSP `connect-src` lists just that module's grants, so one module can't fetch a host granted to another. (The probe used an `http://` bypass; whether TURN over TLS reaches an `https://host:443` bypass was not proven either way, see the re-review entry below.) Fully per-frame scoping (a partitioned session per module) stays the deferred option.
- **2026-10-27, second review round** — **Drafter won't run a modules folder, or any module, that another user of the computer could change.** On Linux and macOS it refuses the modules folder itself, and skips any module with a directory or file anywhere under it that is writable by group or others, or not owned by the user running Drafter (the message names the path). A module's code and every file it serves live in that tree, so one writable file would let another local user change what Drafter runs.
- **2026-10-27, third review round** — **The writable/owner walk fails closed.** Checking that another user can't change a module's tree uses `lstat` on each entry and treats anything it can't be sure of as insecure, so the module is skipped (naming the path), not loaded: a path it can't `lstat` or a folder it can't read, a dangling symlink, and a symlink whose real target leaves the module's folder. It no longer returns "fine" on a `stat`/`readdir` error.
- **2026-10-27, second review round** — **A module's storage quota is held in one transaction.** The read-check-write that keeps a module to its share of the browser's storage runs inside a Dexie `rw` transaction, so two `storage.set` calls at once can't both read the old total and both slip past the limit.
- **2026-10-27, re-review** — **Accepted: WebRTC TURN (or TURN over TLS) to a granted host's exact port may go direct.** Chromium may resolve the proxy for a TURN-over-TCP connection as an `https://host:port` URL, which would match that origin's bypass. It reaches only a host the writer already granted to a module that is on, on the port they granted, which `fetch` can reach anyway; the module's own CSP keeps its HTTP to its own grants, but over WebRTC another module that is on could use that host too. Closing it fully needs a partitioned session per module (deferred).
- **2026-10-27, re-review** — **Group-writable is accepted only for a group that is provably the writer's alone, and only on Linux (re-review F1, then a follow-up review).** Ubuntu, Debian, and Fedora give each user a private group and a umask of 002, so the files a writer unpacks are `0664`/`0775`; refusing all group-write would refuse every module on a normal Ubuntu desktop (CI's umask 022 hides it). But the writer's own primary group isn't enough: on macOS every user's primary group is the shared `staff`, and on Linux a primary group can be shared too. So on Linux group-write is accepted only when the group's name is the user's name, `/etc/group` lists no members for it, and no other `/etc/passwd` account has it as its primary group; anything in doubt (the files unreadable, the group or the account not in them, as with LDAP or SSSD) is refused. On macOS and every other platform group-write is always refused. Also refused everywhere: anything writable by others or owned by another user. When a module is refused only for group-write, the message gives the fix (`Run: chmod -R g-w <its folder>`). Drafter makes the modules folder itself with mode `0700`. Still fail-closed.
- **2026-10-27, follow-up review** — **A folder's name never becomes a command.** The `chmod` advice single-quotes the path for a POSIX shell (each `'` written `'\''`), so a folder named `x; rm -rf ~` or `$(…)` is only ever an argument; a path with a control character (a newline, a terminal escape) gets no command at all, only "Remove group write access from it". Every folder name and path in a module message, in the main process and the page, is shown with its control characters replaced by `?`, so a name can't forge a second line or a terminal escape.
- **2026-10-27, re-review** — **The network grant waits for the proxy (re-review R2):** `modules.setNetwork` resolves only after the session's proxy has its new bypass, so a module's first consented fetch never meets the old proxy.
- **2026-10-27, re-review** — **Deferred:** a tab or settings frame may still call `confirm` and `goto` outside a run (R5: visible, and named; a per-module rate limit would finish it; R3 and R4 were fixed later, below); and round 1's minors 8, 9, and 12.
- **2026-10-27** — **Deferred, each noted here:** the serve-time symlink TOCTOU (minor 8) — only a local process that can already write the folder could win the race, and no module code can write files, so it is not closed now; a **hard** link in an unpacked archive isn't caught by the real-path check (minor 9) — noted in `modules/README.md` for the writer, not refused; DNS prefetch and `rel=preconnect` (minor 12) — a small channel the probe couldn't make leak from a loopback test, left for the per-frame-session work. The per-module partitioned session (review option 1 for C1) is the home for all three.
- **2026-10-27 (v0.6.1)** — **What the writer agreed to is what runs** (R3, and a later automated finding). The fingerprint is now a hash of the folder's name and of every file in it (by path and SHA-256), so any change to a module, its code included, turns it off until the writer turns it on again; a module update is something the writer installs, so asking again is right. The main process also keeps each file's hash from start and serves only those files, unchanged: a file changed or added while Drafter runs is a 404, so a module can't be swapped under a running consent. A module is at most 64 MB. **A module's folder may not be a symbolic link** (R4's simplest close): its real home, and the folders above it, would be outside what Drafter checks; the message says to put the folder itself in the modules folder. The folders above the modules folder itself remain the user's own (their home), as with any app's data.

## The Mac app, built on the writer's own Mac (`apps/desktop`)

Guide: [`docs/desktop-install-macos.md`](desktop-install-macos.md). Decided by the maintainer (2026-10-27): no Apple developer account for about a month, so no signing, no notarisation, and no published macOS release; meanwhile a writer builds `Drafter.app` on their own Mac with one command.

- **2026-10-27** — **`pnpm desktop:mac` (root and `apps/desktop`) builds the app, the web build, and then `electron-builder --mac --publish never`:** a `dir` target (`release/mac-arm64/Drafter.app`, or `release/mac/` on Intel) and a `.dmg` of it, for the processor of the Mac it runs on (`process.arch`), named `Drafter-<version>-mac-<arch>`. Signed **ad hoc** (`identity: '-'`): Apple silicon runs no unsigned code, and an app built on the Mac it runs on carries no quarantine, so Gatekeeper never assesses it (`spctl` rejects it, and that doesn't matter until it is copied to another Mac). **The hardened runtime is off**: with an ad-hoc signature, library validation would refuse Electron's own frameworks, and the hardened runtime is for notarisation, which comes with the account. `notarize: false` says so outright. The same fuses as the Linux packages (on macOS they live in `Electron Framework`, and asar integrity is enforced there, from `ElectronAsarIntegrity` in Info.plist). Nothing for macOS is published: the workflow's `publish` job takes only the Linux job's artifact, and a new `mac` job (macos-15, Apple silicon; free on a public repository) builds it the guide's way, checks the asar, runs `e2e/mac-packaged.spec.ts` and the shell's e2e on macOS, and uploads nothing but failed tests' traces. The `latest-mac.yml` electron-builder writes beside the `.dmg` stays in `release/`.
- **2026-10-27** — **File types:** the three `fileAssociations` gain a macOS `rank`: `.fountain` is `Default` (Drafter opens it when nothing else claims it), `.md` and `.markdown` are `Alternate` (in Open With, never taking Markdown over from the app that has it). macOS has no type for `.fountain` (a `dyn.` type that is only "data", so Quick Look shows nothing), so `mac.extendInfo` **imports** `dev.drafter.fountain`, conforming to `public.plain-text`, by extension and `text/x-fountain`. Imported, not exported: an app that declares the type as its own wins, and Drafter's document types name extensions, not types, so it still opens the file. Markdown already has the system's `net.daringfireball.markdown`. Linux ignores `rank`.
- **2026-10-27** — **The icon is `brand/icon-mac-1024.png`** (`brand/build.mjs`, from `icon-mac.svg`): the tile at 824 pixels on Apple's 1024-pixel grid, with a soft shadow, so it is the size of the other apps in the Dock. `icon-512.png` fills its square and would look a size too big there.
- **2026-10-27** — **`open-file` is listened for on the main process's first pass**, before the single-instance lock and the sandbox question (`heldOpenFiles`, `open-files.ts`), and held until `start` delivers it: `open -a Drafter x.fountain` and a Finder double-click send it before `ready`. Each path goes to `openFromSystem` after `--`, so it is a path whatever it starts with, and is granted then sent exactly as a launch's arguments are; a path that isn't a script is refused with the same status-bar message. This replaces "untested until the macOS build" above: `e2e/mac.spec.ts` emits the event, and `e2e/mac-packaged.spec.ts` (and a check by hand) opens a file with `open -n -a` into a packaged app that isn't running yet.
- **2026-10-27** — **On a Mac, closing the last window leaves the app running** (this replaces "on every OS for now" above). `window-all-closed` does nothing on macOS; `activate` with no visible window (the Dock icon, or `open -a Drafter`) brings the editor's window back (`bringForward`, which opens a new one), and so does a file opened from Finder. Closing the editor's window still asks the page first, as on Linux; Quit with no window open has no page to ask, so it asks nothing and goes. The window's `close` event is what asks: a page's own `window.close()` closes its window without it (Electron's rule; Drafter's page never calls it). `e2e/close.spec.ts` quits after the windows are gone on a Mac (`closedForGood`), so its tests mean the same everywhere.
- **2026-10-27** — **The Mac menu:** the app menu is About Drafter, **Settings…** (the page's own `settings` command, moved out of File, with its ⌘, shown and left to the page), Services, Hide Drafter, Hide Others, Show All, and Quit Drafter (⌘Q); File ends with **Close Window** (the `close` role, ⌘W, which goes through the window's `close` event and so asks); a **Window** menu (the `windowMenu` role) comes before Help, which has the `help` role (the system adds its search field). Linux's menu is unchanged. Read on the packaged app with System Events: every item shows its ⌘ key. Real key presses were not tried (the Mac's screen was locked during the check); the page's ⌘S, sent to the window, saved, and so did File → Save.
- **2026-10-27** — **The sandbox question has Mac words.** `sandboxNotice` takes the platform: on Linux the text is as before (the AppImage, the `.deb`, AppArmor); anywhere else it says what turned the sandbox off (`--no-sandbox` or `ELECTRON_DISABLE_SANDBOX`) and to start Drafter the usual way. Nothing Drafter ships on a Mac turns it off, so the question only comes if a person did.
- **2026-10-27** — **Updates on a Mac: check, then Get it, by Drafter's own check.** electron-updater can't help here: its Mac updater installs only a signed app, from a `.zip` and a `latest-mac.yml`, and every provider adds `-mac` to the channel's file name, so it can't read the Linux feed. `updateMode` is `mac` for a packaged Mac app, and `ReleaseCheck` (`updates.ts`) reads the newest release's `latest-linux.yml` (`releases/latest/download/`, the file the Linux app reads, one version for every system), compares its `version:` with the app's (`isNewer`: numbers part by part, a release newer than its own pre-release, anything that isn't a version never newer), and emits `update-available`; `Updates` then offers **Get it** (the release page), as for a `.deb`, and never downloads or installs. The request goes through a session of its own (`drafter-update-check`), as electron-updater's does, past the page's backstop and proxy. Unpackaged on a Mac, the e2e suite's test feed plays the Mac app (unless `APPIMAGE` asks for the AppImage), so `updates.spec.ts`'s Get it test runs `ReleaseCheck` there. Settings' **Check for updates** says, on a Mac, that Drafter can't update itself yet and the new version is built the way this one was (`whyOnMac` in `SettingsDialog.vue`); the Privacy page names the Mac app beside the `.deb`.
- **2026-10-27** — **The modules folder on a Mac is `~/Library/Application Support/Drafter/modules`** (`userData`, from `app.setName('Drafter')`). Checked on the packaged app: Help → Open Modules Folder makes it `0700` (owner, group `staff`); made `g+w` it is refused at the next start, with the `chmod g-w` advice naming that path, since every account on a Mac is in `staff`.
- **2026-10-27** — **The packaged Mac app is tested with `--user-data-dir`.** A packaged app ignores `DRAFTER_USER_DATA`, and on a Mac its profile folder doesn't follow `HOME`, so a test would otherwise use the writer's own recovery copies; Chromium's `--user-data-dir` moves the whole profile (the modules folder too). `e2e/mac-packaged.spec.ts` (run by `pnpm e2e:packaged` with `DRAFTER_PACKAGED` set to the `.app`) checks the ad-hoc signature, the document types and the Fountain type, the fuses, and a file opened with `open -n -a` before the app is ready, in a sandboxed page, then SIGTERM.
- **2026-10-27** — **Left as electron-builder writes them, for the signed build to settle:** Info.plist's `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` (Chromium's own network stack doesn't use App Transport Security) and its camera, microphone, and Bluetooth usage strings (`permissions.ts` refuses every such request before macOS would ask). The spellcheck dictionary is packed on a Mac too, unused: a platform's `files` replaces the top-level list rather than adding to it, so leaving it out would mean repeating the list.

### After the review of `mac-local` (2026-10-27)

- **2026-10-27, review** — **With no window, New, Open…, Open Recent, and Settings… stay on (M1).** On a Mac, once the editor's page is gone, the commands that start from a window (`opensWindow`: every `new-*`, `open`, `open-recent`, `settings`) stay enabled with their keys *registered* (there is no page to take ⌘O or ⌘,, so the menu must); the rest stay off. Choosing one parks its id (`ParkedCommand`) and calls `bringForward`; the new editor page's first command list that has it on gets it, once, as an ordinary `menuRun` (a list without it drops it). With a window, the keys go back to the page (`registerAccelerator: false`), so nothing runs twice. Linux is unchanged. `e2e/mac.spec.ts` checks one new screenplay, one Open dialog, and Settings, each from no window.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **The Mac update check gives up after 15 s and reads at most 64 KB (M4):** `AbortSignal.timeout`, a `content-length` over the cap refused before reading, and `ReleaseCheck` refusing any text over it (the file is under a kilobyte).
- **2026-10-27, review** — **The sandbox question names Mac places only on a Mac (M5);** any other non-Linux system (Windows, when it comes) gets neutral words.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **Reopening without a restart is tested (M7):** type, close with Don't Save, the Dock brings the window back in the same process, and the text is there, the editor takes typing (the closed page's lock was let go), and Quit asks.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **The `mac` job doesn't run for a tag (M8),** so a release's run is the Linux job and `publish` alone; `e2e/mac-packaged.spec.ts`'s comment says CI runs it.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **Held `open-file` paths are dropped when this Drafter doesn't start (M3):** said in a comment in `main.ts`, not changed; on a Mac LaunchServices hands a file to the copy already running, so it takes `open -n` or answering Quit to the sandbox question.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **Skipped: an `open-file` in the milliseconds while the editor's window is closing can be lost (M2).** The fix (forgetting the closing page in the inbox, and treating an approved-to-close window as gone in `bringForward`) touches the close path, and the race can't be made to happen in a test; the file is untouched on disk and opening it again works.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **Skipped: logging out, restarting, or shutting down with unsaved text on a Mac (M6)** needs a person at the Mac to check (expected: `before-quit` asks and macOS cancels the logout, or `powerMonitor` `shutdown` flushes and quits; no text is lost either way). The guide doesn't promise either until it is checked.
- **2026-10-27, review** — **Skipped: Info.plist's `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` and usage strings (M9)** stay as electron-builder writes them, for the signed build, as above.

## The Windows app (`apps/desktop`)

Guide: [`docs/desktop-install-windows.md`](desktop-install-windows.md). Design §7–8: an NSIS installer, unsigned at first, with SmartScreen's warning documented.

- **2026-10-27** — **An NSIS installer per processor, x64 and arm64** (`win.target`, `nsis.buildUniversalInstaller: false`), both built on the x64 runner (electron-builder cross-builds arm64) and each tested on its own processor (`windows`, `windows-arm` on `windows-11-arm`). One installer holding both would double everyone's download. The maintainer's Parallels VM on Apple silicon runs Windows 11 on Arm, which is what the arm64 build is for.
- **2026-10-27** — **Names: `Drafter-<version>-windows-<arch>-setup.exe`, version kept** (decided as D12 was for Linux, the other way round). electron-updater's NSIS updater downloads the new installer into its own cache folder and runs it there, so the writer's downloaded file is never replaced in place and a versioned name harms nothing; it says which version a file is. What the updater does need is the processor in the name: with both installers in one `latest.yml`, `findFile` takes the first `.exe` whose name holds `process.arch` (`x64`, `arm64`), so `check-update-feed.mjs latest.yml` requires each installer's name to hold exactly one processor, no two the same, and the feed's version.
- **2026-10-27** — **A one-click, per-user install** (`oneClick`, `perMachine: false`): `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\drafter`, no administrator and no UAC prompt, a Start menu and a desktop shortcut, the uninstaller in Settings → Apps, and the writer's data (`%APPDATA%\Drafter`) left on uninstall (`deleteAppDataOnUninstall: false`). No `elevate.exe` (`packElevateHelper: false`), since nothing installs per machine. Updates install the same way, without an administrator.
- **2026-10-27** — **The packed package.json is named `drafter`** (`extraMetadata.name`). electron-builder made folder names from the workspace's `@drafter/desktop`: the install folder would have been `Programs\@drafterdesktop` and the updater's cache `@drafterdesktop-updater` (on Linux too, which now becomes `drafter-updater`; it is only a cache). The app's own name and profile folder come from `app.setName('Drafter')` and are unchanged; Linux's window class comes from `desktopName`, also unchanged.
- **2026-10-27** — **`.fountain` is Drafter's; Markdown is only offered.** electron-builder adds a platform's `fileAssociations` to the top-level list, so each platform now names its own (`SCRIPT_TYPES` for Linux and macOS, unchanged). On Windows the association's `name` is its ProgID, so `.fountain` is `Drafter.fountain`; `.md` and `.markdown` get only `OpenWithProgids` entries for `Drafter.markdown` (`installer/windows.nsh`), as a Mac's `Alternate`: electron-builder's own macro would set the per-user default for `.md` and take Markdown from the app that has it. The uninstall also clears `.fountain`'s default when it still names `Drafter.fountain` (electron-builder's leaves it pointing at a ProgID it deleted), except during an update's reinstall.
- **2026-10-27** — **The icon is `brand/icon-windows.ico`** (`brand/build.mjs`): PNG entries at 16, 24, and 32 from the favicon's pixel grid, and 48, 64, 128, and 256 from the mark; the same file for Drafter.exe, its file type, the installer, and the uninstaller.
- **2026-10-27** — **No blockmaps** (`nsis.differentialPackage: false`, and `disableDifferentialDownload` in the updater): an update downloads the whole installer, and the release has a file per processor and `latest.yml`, nothing else. `disableWebInstaller` is on, so the updater takes only a full installer.
- **2026-10-27** — **Updates on Windows: check, download in the background, Restart** (`updateMode` `nsis`, `NsisUpdater`), the AppImage's rules: one check 5 s after the first load, only with **Check for updates** on; the download starts only after a check found a newer version and stops if the setting is turned off; "ready" with **Restart** and **Later** once it is downloaded; never automatic (`autoDownload` and `autoInstallOnAppQuit` off). Restart asks about unsaved changes, then `quitAndInstall(false, true)`: not silent, so the installer's progress bar shows while Drafter is away, and then the new version starts. Unpackaged on Windows, the e2e suite's test feed plays this mode unless `APPIMAGE` asks for the AppImage, and the `.deb`'s Get it test is skipped there.
- **2026-10-27, after the security review (the coordinator's ruling)** — **Unsigned updates are checked by SHA-512, not by signature, and that is accepted for now, at parity with the AppImage.** electron-updater's `NsisUpdater.verifySignature` checks an Authenticode signature only when `resources/app-update.yml` has a `publisherName`, and electron-builder writes one only for a signed build. So today a downloaded installer must match the size and SHA-512 in the release's own `latest.yml`, both fetched from the GitHub release over https, and nothing else: the same trust as a writer downloading the installer from the release page. What it doesn't defend against: anyone who can publish a release on the repository (the maintainer's GitHub account, or a workflow with `contents: write`) can publish an installer every Windows Drafter will offer, as for the AppImage; a local program running as the writer could also swap the cached download, but it could change Drafter itself anyway. The planned close is signing (Azure Trusted Signing or a certificate): electron-builder then writes `publisherName`, and electron-updater refuses an update not signed by it. To make sure turning signing on turns the check on: `win.verifyUpdateCodeSignature` stays unset (false would keep the name out), `updates-main.ts` never replaces `verifyUpdateCodeSignature`, and `scripts/check-update-config.mjs` (run on every packaged app in CI) fails a build that is signed — a certificate in `CSC_LINK`/`WIN_CSC_LINK`, or `azureSignOptions`/`signtoolOptions.publisherName` in the config — whose `app-update.yml` has no `publisherName`. It also checks the feed is this repository's.
- **2026-10-27** — **Windows names that aren't what they seem are refused** (closes "Windows, later: trailing dots and spaces" above; `src/windows-paths.ts`). On Windows `checkPath` (every path the page sends, and every grant, so the command line too) and `resolveRawPath` (app:// and drafter-module://) refuse a name ending in a dot or a space, a `:` after the drive (an alternate data stream: `x.exe:notes.md` has a script's extension), a reserved device name with or without an extension (`nul.md`, `COM1.fountain`, `CONIN$`), a character Windows forbids, and a device path (`\\.\`, `\\?\`). Refusing beats normalising: no script is named that way, and Explorer, the dialogs, and the command line never produce one. Long paths need nothing: Node adds `\\?\` itself, and a 300-character path is written and read in the unit tests on Windows. The 4096-character cap stays. Arguments are compared without case on Windows (the app and executable left out, and a file named twice opened once); a grant is still keyed by the path as the main process gave it, so a page that asked by a different case would be refused (fail closed). Extensions were already compared in any case.
- **2026-10-27** — **The modules folder on Windows is `%APPDATA%\Drafter\modules`, protected by the profile folder's own ACL, not by a check of Drafter's.** Node reports every file as mode 0o666 or 0o444 on Windows and has no owner to compare, so the POSIX walk's checks can't apply, and reading ACLs would mean spawning `icacls` or PowerShell and parsing their (localised) output at every start, or a native module. Instead Drafter relies on what Windows gives every profile folder (`C:\Users\<name>`, inherited by `AppData`): full control for the writer, SYSTEM, and Administrators, and no access for other standard users. Drafter adds: a modules folder that is itself a link (a junction or symlink) is skipped, since it would leave that protection; the walk still runs, without the owner and mode comparisons, so a module with a junction or link out of its folder, a dangling link, or anything unreadable is skipped as on Linux; and the files are pinned and served by the system's own real path (`realpathSync.native`, which agrees with `fs.promises.realpath` on 8.3 short names and case; the JavaScript `realpathSync` didn't, and CI found every module file refused on Windows). **The threat left:** Windows has ACLs, and Drafter doesn't read them yet, so a writer (or a program they ran) who loosens the ACL on their own profile or modules folder, or a profile redirected to a network share with looser ACLs, is not noticed; a hard link into a module (one file under two names, the other anywhere on the drive) was another way in, and is refused since the review below; a junction one level up (`%APPDATA%\Drafter` itself) is not detected, but only the writer, or an administrator, could make one; an administrator can change a module, as root can on Linux. Reading the ACL safely (by SID, through a small native call) is the follow-up if that matters.
- **2026-10-27** — **The sandbox question has neutral words on Windows** (already true since the Mac review: anything but Linux and macOS gets "start Drafter the usual way"); nothing Drafter ships on Windows turns the sandbox off, and the packaged test checks no Drafter process runs with `--no-sandbox`.
- **2026-10-27** — **The menu bar on Windows is Linux's:** `&File`, `&Edit`, `&View`, `&Go`, `&Help`, so Alt opens each by its letter, Alt or F10 focuses the bar, and Quit (Ctrl+Q) ends File.
- **2026-10-27** — **`build:web` runs pnpm portably** (`pnpmCommand`): the pnpm that is running it (`npm_execpath`'s JavaScript entry, with this Node), else `pnpm` from the PATH, through a shell only on Windows, where `pnpm.cmd` can't be started without one. CI found `execFileSync('pnpm')` failing there.
- **2026-10-27** — **CI: `windows` and `windows-arm` jobs, and a release waits for both** (`publish` needs `linux`, `windows`, `windows-arm`). `windows` (on `windows-latest`, `core.autocrlf false` so files are checked out as committed) runs the shell's unit tests, builds both installers, checks `app.asar`, `app-update.yml`, and `latest.yml`, runs `e2e/win-packaged.spec.ts` on the x64 installer (a silent per-user install with `/S /D=`, the uninstall entry and Start menu shortcut, the registered types, the fuses, a file named on the command line opening sandboxed and a second launch handing its file over, `start` opening a `.fountain` in the installed Drafter, a silent uninstall that removes the files, shortcut, and types and leaves the writer's), then a real update (two installers built with a local feed, 1.0.0 installed, 1.0.1 found, downloaded, and installed by Restart, and 1.0.1 running from the same folder), then the shell's end-to-end tests on Windows (the WebRTC and proxy hardening among them). `windows-arm` runs the packaged spec and the real update on the arm64 installer. The arm64 job skips the unpackaged end-to-end suite to keep its time down. `publish-release.sh` checks both feeds and that they give the same version, takes its file list from `check-release-assets.mjs --list` (exactly one AppImage, `.deb`, `latest-linux.yml`, x64 and arm64 installer, and `latest.yml`, or nothing is made), uploads, compares, and only then publishes. `test/publish-release.test.ts` runs the real script against a pretend `gh` on PATH: a full release is drafted, uploaded, checked, and published in that order; missing Windows files, feeds of different versions, or a file that arrives changed leave nothing published.
- **2026-10-28** — **What the Windows runs found, and fixed.** (1) electron-builder registers `.fountain`'s open command as `C:\…\Drafter.exe "%1"`, the program's path unquoted, which Windows resolves by guessing at the spaces when a user name has one; `installer/windows.nsh` writes it again quoted, as the Markdown command already was. (2) The e2e harness launched `"…\apps\desktop\"`, whose trailing `\"` Windows reads as an escaped quote, so Electron got a path that wasn't there and every unpackaged test timed out; `DESKTOP_DIR` drops its last separator. (3) The runner's screen is 1024 × 768, which clamps the 1280 × 860 window below the editor's two-pane width (`WIDE_QUERY`, 1024 px) and hides the Source pane, as xvfb's default did on Linux; the `windows` job sets 1920 × 1080 first. (4) Windows reports a product version in four parts (`1.0.0.0`). (5) A test's profile can stay locked for a moment after the app quits, so it is removed with retries.
- **2026-10-28** — **On Windows on Arm the installer runs emulated and is slow** (NSIS's stub and its 7-Zip plugin are x86): about 4 to 4½ minutes to install on GitHub's `windows-11-arm` runner, where x64 takes seconds, and the uninstall and an update's reinstall take minutes too. The packaged tests wait up to ten minutes for each. A native arm64 NSIS would fix it, but electron-builder's NSIS has none; the guide says the Arm install takes a few minutes. The x64 runner also showed what Restart does on Windows: the old Drafter quits, the new installer replaces it (`ProductVersion` 1.0.1), and the shortcut starts the new version with `--updated` about 20 seconds after Restart.

### After the review of `windows` (2026-10-28)

- **2026-10-28, review I1** — **Signing is detected by every setting that signs, and by the build itself.** `signingConfigured` now counts `signtoolOptions.sign` (a custom signer, such as an HSM or SSL.com eSigner, for which electron-builder writes no `publisherName`), `certificateFile`, `certificateSha1`, and `certificateSubjectName` (on `signtoolOptions` or, as older electron-builders read them, on `win`), beside the certificate variables, `azureSignOptions`, and a publisher name. And since a configuration can sign in ways a list misses, the `windows` job also reads the built files' Authenticode signatures (`Get-AuthenticodeSignature` on both `Drafter.exe`s and both installers): if any carries a signature (any status but `NotSigned`), `app-update.yml` must have a `publisherName`, and the signer's CN must be one of its names, or the job fails (`check-update-config.mjs --signatures`). Signing therefore can't land without electron-updater checking update signatures, nor with a name that would make it refuse every update.
- **2026-10-28, review I2** — **A release's `latest.yml` lists an installer for every processor.** electron-updater's `findFile` falls back to the first `.exe` when none holds `process.arch`, so a feed with only the x64 installer would have an arm64 Drafter install the x64 build over itself. `check-update-feed.mjs --release` (the `windows` job's check, and `publish-release.sh`) requires one for each of `WINDOWS_ARCHS`; the update test's feed, for one processor, is checked without it.
- **2026-10-28, review M1, M2** — `check-release-assets.mjs` compares both ways: an asset on the release that this build didn't make (an earlier upload, say) fails the check. `publish-release.sh` stops when the tag has more than one draft, so `gh release upload <tag>` and the comparison by id can't meet different releases.
- **2026-10-28, review M3, M6** — A device name with spaces before its extension (`nul .md`, `CON  .fountain`) is refused too: Windows drops those spaces. And a grant checks the real path a link leads to (`Grants.grant`, and `allowed` for a grant kept from before), not only the name asked for, so `x.md` leading to `evil.exe:x.md` is refused on Windows.
- **2026-10-28, review M4, M5** — On Windows a module with a hard-linked file (`nlink > 1`) is skipped, since nothing compares owners there. Not changed: `%APPDATA%\Drafter` itself made a junction isn't detected (the note above).


## The accessibility pass (`apps/web`, `apps/desktop`)

The last step of Phase 1 (Phase 1 design §1 item 5, §13, §16; Plan 5 Task 9). This is automated checking only. The NVDA and VoiceOver passes are still Phase 2's, and no user-facing text claims WCAG conformance.

- **2026-10-28** — **The accessibility tests run by default.** The `grepInvert` in `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` and the `A11Y` variable are gone. The whole web suite (2,850 tests over five projects) takes about 8.5 minutes on the maintainer's Mac with them, of which they are about two. That cost is too small to justify an opt-in that has already let twenty tests go unenforced since 2026-10-26. `pnpm e2e:a11y` still runs them alone, as a quick check. The desktop's axe tests run in `pnpm --filter @drafter/desktop e2e`, and so in CI on Linux, Windows, and macOS.
- **2026-10-28** — **The suite.** `apps/web/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` runs axe, with the same tags as the older tests (`wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa`, `wcag22aa`), on every major state in both themes and in every project, so at desktop and phone sizes. The states are listed in its tests. States other specs already check (the palette, the Scripts menu, the drop zone, the changed-file bar, the recovery warnings, the tabs' guard, a save) stay in those specs. The same file asserts the accessibility tree: one banner, main, and contentinfo; the Script region's scene headings (`toMatchAriaSnapshot`); the toolbar's names; an accessible name on every visible control on the page, in Settings, the Counts panel, the app menu, the Scripts menu, and the palette; and what the live regions say (the element under the cursor, the element bar's notice, a save, a sprint's end). `apps/desktop/e2e/a11y.spec.ts` covers what only the desktop draws: the modules folder in Settings, with a skipped folder, a module that couldn't start, a module's network question, and a folder module's button and tab; and the update prompt. It uses axe's legacy mode, because the normal run opens a page of its own and Electron's driver can't. Legacy mode doesn't go into a cross-origin frame, so the sandboxed frame a folder module runs in is not scanned: only Drafter's own page around it is. The update prompt's test switches the theme with the toolbar's button, not a reload. A reload doesn't lose an offer anyway: main keeps the offer and sends it again when the page listens again, which `updates.spec.ts` tests, including an offer made while the page was reloading. The native dialogs belong to the OS.
- **2026-10-28** — **axe waits for fades.** `clean()` waits for every finite animation on the page to finish before axe measures. Halfway through a fade, the colors on screen are the fade's and not the state's: focus mode fades in, and so do the tour's cards.
- **2026-10-28** — **The baseline.** With `A11Y=1`, 22 of the 120 runs of the twenty older tests failed, all on one rule and one node: `color-contrast` on the element bar's scene-heading mark (`.mark`, drawn at `opacity: 0.7`). The new suite then found:
  - `aria-hidden-focus` on every open menu (Reka's modal menus `aria-hidden` the page, buttons and all).
  - `color-contrast` on the welcome and intro cards (Nuxt UI's dimmed gray and amber).
  - `target-size` on the status bar's and the focus marker's buttons at phone width.
  - `scrollable-region-focusable` on the intro card's text box on a phone, and on the Fountain cheat sheet's wide examples.
  - `link-in-text-block` on the help pages' links (from an axecap audit of /docs/saving/).
- **2026-10-28** — **Menus are non-modal, through `MenuButton.vue`.** All six menus use `MenuButton`, a wrapper around `UDropdownMenu` with `:modal="false"`. A modal Reka menu hides the rest of the page with `aria-hidden` while its controls can still take focus. A non-modal one doesn't, but Reka 2.11 blocks Tab only in a modal menu (`MenuContentImpl.js`): in a non-modal one, Tab closed the menu and left focus on the page's body, and Shift+Tab sent it to the status bar (WCAG 2.4.3; found in review). So while its menu is open, `MenuButton` takes Tab and Shift+Tab in the capture phase and closes the menu, and Reka gives focus back to the button that opened it, as the ARIA menu button pattern has it. `keyboard.spec.ts` tests both keys on every menu, the narrow screen's sample-bar More and Modules menus included. Escape and a click outside still close a menu. A click outside also reaches what was clicked. One test locator, `getByRole('group', { name: 'Scripts' })`, also matched the closing app menu's "Recent scripts" group and is now exact everywhere. The Stats window no longer takes a menu's item as the place to give focus back to: the item can still be on the page, fading out, when the window closes.
- **2026-10-28** — **The tour cards' colors are set in `tour.css`, on the cards alone.** Every token the cards use is pinned there:
  - The text: `--ui-text`, `--ui-text-muted`, and `--ui-text-dimmed`.
  - The icon and the solid button: `--ui-primary`.
  - The button's text: `--ui-text-inverted`.
  - The button's hover and press: `--tour-primary-hover`. Nuxt UI draws the hover as the primary color at 75%, about 3.6:1 under white in the light theme, so the light hover is a darker shade (`#6b4500`).

  The dark card is a gradient from `#475569` to `#334155`, which axe reports only as "incomplete". `test/tour-colors.test.ts` measures the text at 4.5:1 and the icon at 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11) against both ends of the gradient, and the light ones against `#f8fafc` and white. It also measures the button text on the primary color and on its hover. The cards keep their look. On a phone, the intro's text no longer scrolls in its own box: the overlay scrolls instead, with the card's buttons in it, so a keyboard can reach it. A wider screen keeps the module's box, which scrolls past 60% of the screen's height.
- **2026-10-28** — **24px targets** (WCAG 2.5.8). The status bar's buttons (the counts, the recovery note, the sprint's Pause, Stop, Hide, and Show) and the focus marker's Leave are at least 24px tall. Their bars' padding shrinks to match, so the bars keep their height. Tight wrapped rows on a phone were the only failure.
- **2026-10-28** — **Help-page links are underlined in the text** (`.docs-main a`). The bar's links stand alone and keep no underline. The cheat sheet's `<pre>` examples are tab stops with a focus ring, as the Saving page's wide table already was, each a `role="group"` named for its element (an `aria-label` isn't allowed on a `<pre>` with no role).
- **2026-10-28** — **A skip link** (from DEFERRED): **Skip to the editor** is the first Tab stop, out of sight until focused. It runs Show Source, so on a phone the Source comes up first. It isn't there in focus mode, which has no toolbar to skip.
- **2026-10-28** — **Clicks in the keyboard journey.** `watchPointer` doesn't record `click` events. A pointer's click always comes after a `pointerdown` and a `mousedown`, which are recorded. Any other click is a key's or the app's own. Firefox gives a link's click from Enter a `detail` of 1, and the app calls `click()` on a download link to save, so neither a click's count nor `isTrusted` separates them from a pointer's.
- **2026-10-28** — **What remains.** No violations are known in the states checked. In WebKit alone, under parallel load, a menu opened by the tests sometimes closes at once: a click on the element bar's More that shows no menu, or the template chip's Markdown item chosen by keyboard with no question after it. Stress runs (eight workers) put it at about 2 to 4 percent with non-modal menus, and none in 60 runs with modal ones. Ignoring a focus move out of the menu made it worse, and the cause isn't found yet. Chromium and Firefox don't show it. On a wide screen shorter than the intro card, the end of the intro's text sits below its box's fold, and the box isn't a tab stop, so a keyboard can't scroll to it. axe doesn't report this. Some things are not checked: the exported PDF's own accessibility (outside this pass), a module's own frame contents beyond the fixtures, and colour contrast on gradients outside the tour cards (there are none in Drafter's own CSS).
## Phase 2, Plan A: the Outline (`@drafter/outline`, `apps/web`)

- **2026-10-28** — **Scene colors are header notes** (`[[Color: blue]]`), decided by the maintainer; the Phase 2 design's one open question is closed (§24).
- **2026-10-28** — `@drafter/outline` reads the keyed notes it needs itself (`readKeyedNote`, only `Color` for now), rather than wait for Plan B's `@drafter/notes`. Plan B moves the reader there; the reading is the same (any case, extra spaces, the head before the first colon, up to 40 characters).
- **2026-10-28** — A scene ends at the last block before the next scene start or any section, as folding does. In a play, a `##` scene closes the open sections of depth 2 and deeper, so a `###` inside a scene ends it; an `INT.` line isn't a scene there. The metadata block, and a `/* beat board` boneyard just before it, are never in a scene or a section.
- **2026-10-28** — A scene's words are its printed words (action, dialogue, and the rest that prints; not synopses, notes, boneyard, or sections), counted as runs of letters and digits. Its pages and eighths are the `scene-list` module's arithmetic, and only from a layout of the same parse (the block counts match). A section's length is the sum of its scenes' eighths.
- **2026-10-28** — **A move cuts the item with the gap before it and writes it back as a blank line and the item** after its target; the first item of a script with nothing before it is cut with the gap after it instead. That's the design's "one blank line before and after" (§6.3), and the property test checks that any move, moved back, restores the source exactly when items are one blank line apart.
- **2026-10-28** — **A section never takes in what isn't its own.** Fountain nests by depth, so a `##` written before a scene or a `###` would make them part of it. A section's move goes on past the scenes and deeper sections that follow its target, to the next section as shallow as itself or the end, and Move up passes only sections as shallow as itself (a section's parent's own scenes always come before it). Scenes go anywhere: a scene put after a section's last scene is in that section.
- **2026-10-28** — Crossing a section's edge is the same rule both ways, so it reverses: Move down from a section's last scene goes to the start of the section after (after its heading and intro); Move up from a section's first scene goes before that section's heading, which is the end of the section before.
- **2026-10-28** — **A scene's header** is the synopsis and note blocks right after the heading with no blank line between them. A new synopsis goes at its top; a new color after its synopses and before its first note. Taking a line out closes the lines up: the line break after it inside the header, or the gap before it otherwise, so a heading never loses the blank line under it.
- **2026-10-28** — **The writer's words go in on one line**, and can't open anything: a line break becomes ` / `, `[[` becomes `[ [`, and `/*` and `*/` get a space. A synopsis edited in place keeps a note that follows it on the same line.
- **2026-10-28** — Spans end before a CRLF's `\r`, so a script with Windows line breaks keeps them through a move, a synopsis, or a color (the editor's text is always `\n`; the CLI's needn't be).
- **2026-10-28** — **The printed script never changes** is one test file, `packages/outline/test/invariant.test.ts`: random scripts (Drafter-shaped ones, and the parser's general generator) printed as text with every scene colored, uncolored, and with an edited synopsis. Later plans add their notes and the Beat Board block to it.
- **2026-10-28** — **Stubs:** `INT. LOCATION - DAY` in a screenplay and `## New scene` in a play (where a scene is a `##` section), each with the line as its synopsis. `stub-heading` (info) matches either, in any case and spacing, and the play's only where the template makes `##` a scene. The lint rule keeps its own copy of the two strings (lint doesn't depend on outline); a test in `@drafter/outline` checks that the rule finds every stub Expand writes.
- **2026-10-28** — **Expand outline** turns `=` lines and paragraphs made only of `- ` items that aren't in a scene; a paragraph partly made of items is left alone, since splitting it would rewrite lines nobody asked about. A stub after a line with no blank between gets one, so its heading reads as a heading.
- **2026-10-28** — **A Markdown outline** is read line by line, with no Markdown library (the package has no dependencies beyond Drafter's): a first-line `#` heading is the title, other headings become sections of the same depth, list items (`-`, `*`, `+`, `1.`) become stubs with their indented continuation lines, other paragraphs and quotes become synopses (`= …`), and code blocks are skipped. Links and images keep their words. Nothing is dropped but code and Markdown's own marks.
- **2026-10-28** — **A treatment** is Markdown: the title (or "Treatment"), each section as a heading with its own synopses, each scene as a heading one level deeper with its synopses, and, as an option, its first paragraph of action as printed (no notes or emphasis marks). The writer's text is escaped, so it shows as written.
- **2026-10-28** — **Scene identity** (`sceneIds` in `cm-fountain`) covers every scene heading and every section, whatever the template, so a play's `##` scenes and acts and a screenplay's sections all keep their rows' focus. A heading's offset is mapped through each change; one whose first character survives keeps its id first, then one retyped from its line's start (a heading selected and typed over keeps its id). A heading pasted at another's very start takes that one's id: the text can't say which is which, and nothing about a scene is lost, since everything about it is in its text.
- **2026-10-28** — **The tab row** (design §5.8): Source, Script, Problems, and Outline, then one slot, then **More**. The slot shows the pane's own tab when it shows one of the others, else the one chosen last in any pane (kept for the visit), else the first. More is a button after the tab list (a tab list holds only tabs), opening a menu through `MenuButton`, as every menu is since v0.9.0; choosing a tab puts focus on its tab button, not back on More. Today the others are the modules' tabs; Cards, Notes, Stats, and the rest join them in later plans.
- **2026-10-28** — A Markdown document has no Outline tab: the row leaves it out, and a pane showing it when the document turns Markdown shows the first core tab the other pane isn't showing.
- **2026-10-28** — **The Outline is a flat ARIA tree**: one `role="tree"` list whose rows carry `aria-level`, `aria-setsize`, `aria-posinset`, and (sections) `aria-expanded`, rather than nested `group` lists. ARIA allows either; the flat one keeps one list for focus, filtering, and the drop line, and a closed section's rows are simply not drawn. Each row is named in words ("Scene 2, INT. HALL - DAY, blue, page 3, 1 3/8 pages"; "Act One, 2 scenes, 4 pages"), with its synopsis and who speaks as its description.
- **2026-10-28** — **Keys:** the tree pattern (Up, Down, Left, Right, Home, End, type-ahead), Enter goes to the row in the Source, Alt+Up and Alt+Down move it, and the context-menu key or Shift+F10 opens its menu. The ⋯ button is for a pointer (`tabindex="-1"`), so a row is one tab stop. Move scene up and Move scene down have no keys: CodeMirror's Alt+arrows move lines in the Source, and the palette and the Edit menu reach them.
- **2026-10-28** — **A row's menu has every move** (Move up, Move down, Move to…), so a phone, which can't drag, does everything from it. In a read-only tab the edits stay in the menu, off (`aria-disabled`), under a line giving the reason; Alt+arrows there say the reason in the live region instead of moving.
- **2026-10-28** — **Edit synopsis** saves on Enter and nowhere else: Escape and leaving the field both cancel, so the text never changes unless the writer said so (the design's anti-goal).
- **2026-10-28** — **Drag** is HTML drag and drop on desktop browsers, with a line above or below the row under the pointer; dropping below an open section puts the row at its start, below a closed one after all of it. A row moved into a closed section opens it, so focus can stay on the row.
- **2026-10-28** — **Announcements** go to one polite region that is always in the page (`OutlineAnnouncer.vue`), so a move from the Source pane is heard too: "Moved INT. HALL - DAY to scene 2 of 3, in Act One.", "Moved Act Two. It starts at scene 1 of 3.", "Act Two can't move further up.", and "Colored INT. HALL - DAY blue."
- **2026-10-28** — **A row's menu is a `MenuButton`** (merged with v0.9.0, which makes every menu one): a real ARIA menu, non-modal, with Tab closing it. Its ⋯ button is the trigger; the context-menu key and Shift+F10 on the row open the same menu. Closing it puts focus on the row, or where the chosen action wants it (the synopsis field, Move to…), unless another row's menu has opened meanwhile. **Color…** is a submenu of checkable colors (`menuitemcheckbox`), with No color. In a read-only tab the menu's first line says why, and the edits are disabled items.
- **2026-10-28** — Plan A's Tasks 7 and 8 (the Outline tab, and moving from it) landed as one commit: the row menu has the moves in it from the start.
- **2026-10-28** — **Condense to outline folds every scene, not the sections**: Fold all would fold an act around its scenes and hide them, so Condense folds each scene to its heading and synopsis (the existing fold placeholder) and leaves sections open, then shows the Outline beside the Source (on a phone, the Outline). **Expand all** unfolds everything, as Unfold all does.
- **2026-10-28** — **Export as treatment…** asks one question in a small dialog (the first paragraph of action, or not) and then **Save file** (`<title> — treatment.md`, through the same picker or download as every export, with the Export toast) or **Open as a document** (a new Markdown document; the script is untouched). It is in the Export menu, the File menu, and the palette.
- **2026-10-28** — **Expand outline** works on the selection, or on the whole script when nothing is selected, as one undo; the live region says how many stubs it made and that Problems lists them. **New screenplay from this outline** is offered only in a Markdown document: a new screenplay, titled from the outline's first `#` heading, with the rest as sections and stubs.
- **2026-10-28** — **An Outline row's name is one line of words**, in a visually hidden span ("Scene 1, INT. HALL - DAY, teal, page 1, 1/8 page"), and what is drawn (the number, the chip, `p. 1 · 1/8 p`, the ⋯ button) is hidden from screen readers, with the synopsis and speakers as the description. An `aria-label` on the row would differ from its visible text (axe's `label-content-name-mismatch`), and the drawn text alone reads badly ("p. 1 · 1/8 p").
- **2026-10-28** — The Outline's axe tests (`e2e/outline.spec.ts`, "passes axe …") run by default, in every project (desktop and phone width), in both themes: the tree, a filter that matches nothing, a row's menu and its colors, editing a synopsis, Move to…, the treatment dialog, the tabs' More, and a read-only tab's menu. They stay out of `a11y.spec.ts`, which the accessibility pass owns.

### After the whole-branch review of `phase2-a` (2026-10-28)

- **2026-10-28, review C1** — **Nothing structural changes while a `/*` is stray**: unclosed, or closed only by a later boneyard's `*/` (its text holds another `/*`, as when a writer's `/* oops` is closed by the metadata block's). A scene's span could then hold the metadata block, and a move would carry it into the script. `hasStrayBoneyard` gates every move, synopsis, color, and Expand outline in `@drafter/outline`; the Outline says why and disables its edits, as for a read-only tab.
- **2026-10-28, review C1** — **Moves leave lenient scripts alone.** A move is refused when the moved item, or the place it goes, isn't set off by a blank line as Fountain reads one (a heading read with no blank before it, or two-space lines between items): next to new neighbours those lines could read differently. Found by running the move properties over the parser's general generator, which they now do, checking the number of scenes, headings, and the non-blank lines too.
- **2026-10-28, review I2** — **Expand outline needs a selection** (design §6.5, "from a selection"), and a synopsis right under a section heading is the section's own and stays one. A line's notes and boneyard go into the stub's synopsis as they are.
- **2026-10-28, review I3** — **Taking out a hand-typed color inside a line removes only the note**: `waits [[Color: red]] by` becomes `waits  by`, which prints exactly as before. Removing a synopsis or color line is refused where a two-space line joins it to its neighbours, or where what follows isn't more of a header, since closing the gap would join lines that print.
- **2026-10-28, review I4** — **An action decided a moment ago looks its item up again**: a row's menu items, Move to…, and every row handler resolve the row by its id when they run, so none writes at offsets from before an edit. Move to… lists places by block, not by object (the index is made afresh after every edit).
- **2026-10-28, review** — **Undo and redo carry a moved scene's id.** A move's `sceneMovedEffect` is inverted by the editor's history (`invertedEffects`), so undoing a move gives the scene back its id; Mod+Z and Mod+Shift+Z (and Mod+Y) on an Outline row undo and redo the script with focus kept on the row. When the focused row goes anyway, focus moves to the Outline's tab stop rather than the page.
- **2026-10-28, review** — **Type-ahead**: one letter moves on to the next match; a longer prefix starts from the row it has found, which stays while it still matches.
- **2026-10-28, review (performance)** — **Typing with the Outline showing a 150-scene script** cost 17.3 ms a keystroke against 2.1 ms with Problems showing (Chromium, the maintainer's Mac, 60 keystrokes, three runs). Every row's menu got a fresh `content` object and every row was drawn again. Now one `content` object serves every row and each row is memoised on what it draws (`v-memo` on a signature): 6.2 ms a keystroke, no long tasks. What's left is the scene index and the rows' signatures, recomputed per parse.

## Phase 2, Plan FD: Final Draft files (`@drafter/fdx`)

- **2026-10-28** — **The XML reader is Drafter's own, strict, and takes no dependency.** A `.fdx` file is untrusted input (technical design §11), and a general XML library brings a DOM, entity handling, and DTD features Drafter must refuse anyway. The reader accepts no DOCTYPE, only the five built-in entities and numeric references to valid XML characters, no processing instruction but a leading declaration, and refuses mismatched tags, duplicate attributes, unclosed elements, text after the root, nesting deeper than 64 (self-closing elements count), and input over 5 MB. It is one pass over the string with an explicit stack, so it is linear in the input and cannot overflow the call stack.
- **2026-10-28** — **CRLF and a lone CR become a line feed before scanning**, as XML specifies, so an `XmlError`'s `offset` counts characters of the text after that normalization, not of the original. A leading byte order mark is skipped.
- **2026-10-28** — **Names are plain.** A name starts with a letter, `_`, `:`, or a BMP character from U+00C0 up, and continues with digits, `.`, `-`, and U+00B7 too; surrogates and U+FFFE/U+FFFF are never name characters. This is wider than a Final Draft file needs and narrower than the XML spec (no astral names), which is the safe side for untrusted input. An attribute named `__proto__` is kept as an ordinary key.
- **2026-10-28** — **The writer indents only elements that hold nothing but elements.** An element holding text is written on one line with its content exactly, because indenting inside text would change it. So reading what the writer wrote gives the same tree, apart from the whitespace-only text between indented elements, which callers ignore.
- **2026-10-28** — **The large-input test allows 3 seconds, not 1** (controller ruling), to stay stable on CI. The reader reads 5 MB in well under a second here. Cost if wrong: a slower reader passes.
- **2026-10-28** — **Export (`toFdx`) takes text from the lines' styled runs**, never the source, so emphasis markers, boneyard, and escapes stay the parser's business. The one thing put back is a note inside a line, as `[[…]]`: a run can straddle a note (the parser joins the text on either side), so the run's source is cut around the regions inside it, checked against the run's text, and split there; if an escape makes them differ, the run stays whole and the note follows it. A note that runs over lines is one stretch of text (no line break added where it ends), and a space the parser trimmed before a note at a line's end is not restored.
- **2026-10-28** — **Drafter's round-trip attributes** (controller ruling 1), all ignored by Final Draft: `DrafterSection="<depth>"` on a section's `General` paragraph, `DrafterNote="Yes"` on a note carried on its own line, `DrafterKey="<key as typed>"` on the first paragraph of a title-page field, `DrafterSynopsis="Yes"` on a synopsis no scene collects, and `DrafterLyric="Yes"` on a lyric, which is `Dialogue` with `Style="Italic"` (a lyric's runs are all italic, with bold or underline joined as `Bold+Italic`).
- **2026-10-28** — **A note on its own line is a `General` paragraph whose text is the note's content without brackets**; `DrafterNote` says it was a note, so import writes the brackets back. One paragraph per note when a block holds several.
- **2026-10-28** — **A stage play's `##` scene is a `Scene Heading` that also carries `DrafterSection="2"`** (the template's `scene_heading_play` role, found with `resolveRole` as the outline does; `@drafter/outline` is not a dependency, so the rule is mirrored, not imported). Import can then tell `## Scene 1` from `INT. HALL`. A screenplay's `##` section is a `General` section, an outline marker. A scene heading (`INT.`) in a play is still a `Scene Heading`, whatever the template's role for it.
- **2026-10-28** — **Synopses ride on the scene heading** as `<SceneProperties Title="" Summary="…"/>` (several joined by a line feed), first in the paragraph. They are collected from the heading to the next section or scene heading. A synopsis no scene collects (under a section, before any scene, between a play's act and its first `##` scene) is exported as `<Paragraph Type="General" DrafterSynopsis="Yes">` with its text, in document order (controller ruling 5), for import to write back as `= `.
- **2026-10-28** — **A page break sets `StartsNewPage="Yes"` on the next paragraph that is written**, whatever its type (on a dual pair, the first paragraph it lands on); several in a row are one, and one with nothing after it is dropped (controller ruling 3). The break is not a paragraph of its own.
- **2026-10-28** — **Dual dialogue** is the speech with `^` and the speech right before it, in one `<Paragraph><DualDialogue>…</DualDialogue></Paragraph>`. A `^` with no speech immediately before it (a note or action between) is written as an ordinary speech.
- **2026-10-28** — **Title page**: `Content` then `TitlePage` under the root (no `TitlePage` when the script has none); Title, Credit, Author, Authors, and Source are `Alignment="Center"`, every other key `Left`. A key with no value still writes an empty paragraph, so the key survives.
- **2026-10-28** — **Export never throws**: control characters XML forbids, U+FFFE/U+FFFF, and lone surrogates are dropped from text (the writer refuses them), checked by a property test over scrambled Fountain pieces.
- **2026-10-28** — **Export keeps a note in its place in a line that also holds an escape.** The run's text has its escapes read (`\*` is `*`), so the source cut around the note is compared with the escapes taken out too. Before, any escape in the run sent the note to the run's end, so `He \*x\* [[n]] goes.` exported as `He *x*  goes.[[n]]`. An escape a note splits (`\[[n]]*`) still keeps the run whole.
