- **2026-10-28** — **Import (`fromFdx`) reads each block back with the parser before writing it.** The plain text is tried first; when it reads as another kind (or prints differently, as `!Bang` would), the block gets its forced marker: `!` for action, `@` for a cue, `.` for a heading, `> ` for a transition. When no marker can hold the kind (a heading that starts with `(`, a transition ending in `<`, a heading ending in a literal `#1#`), the paragraph comes in as action with a `[[fdx: Type]]` note and is counted in `mapped`. So `parse(fromFdx(x).text)` has the file's kinds, or says which paragraphs it couldn't keep.
- **2026-10-28** — **A `Shot` is a scene heading forced with `.`**, even when it would read as a heading anyway. Fountain has no shot, and a forced heading prints like one.
- **2026-10-28** — **A paragraph type Drafter doesn't map** (`Cast List`, `New Act`, `End of Act`, and a `General` without Drafter's marks) is action with ` [[fdx: Type]]` at the end of its last line that holds anything; an empty one is the note alone. An element that isn't a paragraph and holds text (under the root, in `Content`, or inside a paragraph) is the same, with its element name, its text lines joined. The elements in `IGNORED` (page layout, element settings, header and footer, fonts, smart type, revisions, macros, window and text state, and the like), and any unknown element with no text, are listed once each in `ignored`.
- **2026-10-28** — **Drafter's marks (controller ruling 1)** are read first: `DrafterSection` on `General` or `Scene Heading` is a `#` section of that depth (a play's scene keeps its synopses after it), `DrafterNote` is `[[…]]` on its own line (a `]]` inside gets a space so it can't end the note early), `DrafterSynopsis` is `=` lines (ruling 5), `DrafterLyric` is `~` lines with italic taken off (export made every run italic). Without them, a paragraph has Final Draft's meaning: italic dialogue is `*…*` dialogue.
- **2026-10-28** — **Speeches**: the cue, its parentheticals, and its dialogue on consecutive lines; the second cue of a `DualDialogue` gets ` ^`. Neighbouring dialogue paragraphs read back as one block. A lyric paragraph that follows another lyric is written on its own after a blank line (two would read as one block); export still adds it to the same speech, so the file is unchanged. A parenthetical's outer parentheses are written plain (a style marker beside one hides it from the parser) and added when Final Draft stored the text without them. A dialogue or parenthetical with no cue before it is action with a note; a lyric with no cue is a `~` lyric, as in Fountain.
- **2026-10-28** — **Escaping (controller ruling 2).** In kinds with emphasis (action, dialogue, parenthetical, lyric, centered, title page) `*`, `_`, and `\` are written `\*`, `\_`, `\\`, which the parser reads back exactly. Elsewhere the parser reads no escapes, so text is written as it is, except `/*`, written `/\*` so it can't open a boneyard (the backslash shows in those kinds). `[[…]]` closed within its paragraph stays a note. A `[[` with no `]]` in its paragraph is written `[\[` in a speech or on the title page, whose paragraphs share a block, so it can't close in the next one. Fountain has no escape for a line starting with `#`, `=`, `(`, or `~`, so a later line of action starting with `#` or `=`, and a dialogue line starting with any of the four, gets a backslash that shows but keeps the line in its block.
- **2026-10-28** — **Emphasis is written as nested markers.** Where a style starts, the marker that covers the longest stretch opens (underline's `_` outermost on a tie, then `***` for bold and italic together), and what differs inside is written one level down. Spaces at a stretch's edges go outside the markers, which can't open or close beside a space. The line is then read back, and if its styles differ (two `*` markers that touch merge into one run, as in `*a***b**`), it is written plain: the words always survive, and only a style Fountain can't write is lost. Styles in kinds without emphasis (a bold heading) are dropped.
- **2026-10-28** — **Text is normalized**: `\r\n` and `\r` become `\n`, a tab becomes four spaces (as Fountain treats tabs in action). A blank line inside an action or dialogue paragraph is written as a two-space line, so the block holds together, and so is an empty Action paragraph, which prints a blank line. Other empty paragraphs are left out, though a `StartsNewPage` on one passes to the next. Blank lines at the edges of other kinds' text are dropped. A note that runs over lines is kept as it is unless a line holding only its end would stand apart as a note block; then, and always in one-line kinds, its line breaks become spaces.
- **2026-10-28** — **Page breaks**: `StartsNewPage="Yes"` writes `===` before the block; one on a parenthetical or dialogue inside a speech is left out, since Fountain can't break a page inside a speech. `SceneProperties` gives the heading's `= ` lines: its `Title`, when Final Draft has one, first, then each line of `Summary`. A `Number` the parser would accept as a scene number becomes ` #12A#`; any other (`12 A`) is kept as a `[[fdx: Number 12 A]]` note on the heading and counted as `Number`. A `SceneProperties` on any other paragraph is kept too, as `=` lines after that paragraph (after its speech, when it is inside one, since a speech can't be broken).
- **2026-10-28** — **Text in unexpected places is kept** (fix round 1). An element inside a `<Text>` gives its text to the run. Anything beside the `DualDialogue` in its paragraph, or between its speeches, a child of `TitlePage` other than its `Content`, and an element in that `Content` that isn't a paragraph, each go through the unknown-element path: a `[[fdx: Name]]` note with the text, counted, or `ignored` when it holds none. The title page's come after the script, since the title page holds only `Key: value` lines. A speech no marker can hold (a dual cue whose `(` never closes can't take its `^`) is action with a `[[fdx: Character]]` note, counted, as any other kind that can't be kept.
- **2026-10-28** — **A section's depth is 1 to 6** (final review). Import takes `DrafterSection` only as one digit from 1 to 6; any other value (`30000000` once turned a 284-byte file into a 90 MB script, and `999999999999` threw) comes in through the unmapped path, as action with a `[[fdx: Type]]` note, counted. The Fountain parser and the outline take any depth, so export writes a section deeper than six as six: nothing Drafter writes is refused on the way back, and a seventh level, which no template uses, becomes the sixth. Every other attribute import reads only becomes text of its own length (a scene number, a note's label), so no other value can multiply the output.
- **2026-10-28** — **Text directly in a paragraph is kept** (final review). With no `<Text>` in the paragraph, its own text is the paragraph's text; beside `<Text>` runs, it comes in as a `[[fdx: Paragraph text]]` note. Elements inside a `SceneProperties` come in as notes after the paragraph, as its text would (`[[fdx: SceneProperties]]`). The `Summary` on a dual pair's paragraph is a synopsis after the pair, as on any other paragraph.
- **2026-10-28** — **An XML error shows at most 40 characters of a name**, then an ellipsis, so a hostile file can't make a 200,000-character message.
- **2026-10-28** — **Import is linear in the file.** Finding notes searches for `]]` once per `[[` until a search fails, and then not again (none follows), so a paragraph of unclosed openers is one pass, not one per opener. Tested at 5 MB of `[[` in action and of `a [[ ` in dialogue, within the reader's 3-second budget (under 1.5 s here; it was minutes before).
- **2026-10-28** — **The title page**: with any `DrafterKey`, each keyed paragraph starts a field and the paragraphs after it continue it (as indented lines). Without, by order: the first centered block is `Title`; a centered `by` or `written by` is `Credit` and the centered block after it `Author`; every other line goes into one `Notes` field. Empty paragraphs only separate blocks. `title` in the result is the Title field's text without markup, or null.
- **2026-10-28** — **Two things the round trip can't tell apart, both stable as files**: notes on consecutive lines of one note block and notes in separate blocks both export as separate `DrafterNote` paragraphs, and import writes separate blocks; synopses under a scene are gathered onto its heading by export, so they come back right after it. Export → import → export writes the same file (property-tested over the Fountain generator, both templates); the blocks' order and grouping can differ in these two cases.
- **2026-10-28** — **The round trip is property-tested over the shared generator, in both templates** (`roundtrip.test.ts`): export, import, export gives the same tree, and the kinds and printable text come back, boneyard removed from the source first. Differences compared away (ruling 6, plus the consequences of rulings 3 and 5 that the property surfaced): consecutive own-line notes may return as separate note blocks; a scene's synopses (in a play, those after a `##` scene) return right after its heading; a page break with no paragraph after it is dropped, and a page break right after another is one, since export sets a flag on the next paragraph. Nothing else is normalized.
- **2026-10-28** — **A page break ends the speech before it** (found by the round-trip property): `MARGARET`/`the.`, `===`, `~lyric` exported the lyric inside the speech and lost the break. Export now closes the open speech at a break, and import honours `StartsNewPage` on a `DrafterLyric` paragraph even with a speech open (a break inside a speech stays left out for other dialogue). Cost if wrong: a lyric Drafter wrote after a break keeps a break Final Draft would show mid-speech.
- **2026-10-28** — **An imported `.fdx` is a new script with no file** (Task 5). `openFile` sends a `.fdx` (by its name, `isFinalDraftName`; never a kind of document) through the usual size, binary, and strict UTF-8 checks, then `fromFdx`, and opens the text with `openImported`: a new Fountain project with no `fileName`, handle, saved hash, or baseline, so it is unsaved and Save asks for a `.fountain`. It is never matched to an open script or to a same-titled one (a second import is a second script). A refused file says the `FdxError` message in the status bar and opens nothing. One switch makes and shows it (not `addScript` then `open`), so a switch already under way leaves nothing behind.
- **2026-10-28** — **The project keeps the file it was imported from (`importedFrom`)**, unindexed, so a script with no title page is called after the `.fdx` (`The Call`) and stays so as it is edited: `derive` falls back to `fileName ?? importedFrom` (`titleFileOf`). Save's suggested name follows. It is never a file to write to; once saved, `fileName` takes over.
- **2026-10-28** — **What an import says goes in the Export toast**, a polite live region already in the page: “Imported ‘The Call.fdx’.”, then, when `mapped` is not empty, “N items came in as notes; Problems lists them.” (“1 item came in as a note; Problems lists it.”). *Items*, not paragraphs: `mapped` also counts a scene number Fountain can't hold and elements that aren't paragraphs, and counting only paragraph types would make the number disagree with what Problems lists. `ignored` is not said: settings are not writing. *(Corrected in fix round 1: the first version said Problems listed no notes and left a rule for later; Ruling 8 added it.)*
- **2026-10-28** — **`fdx-note` (info) lists every `[[fdx: …]]` note in Problems** (Ruling 8), modelled on `stub-heading`: a closed note whose text starts with `fdx:`, its span the whole note, the message naming what came in (“This came in from Final Draft as ‘Cast List’, which Drafter has no element for. Check it, then delete the note.”). It reads the parser's regions, so a note in a boneyard or one left open is not listed. Advice, never counted as a problem, and in both templates. Lint keeps its own copy of the `fdx:` prefix (lint doesn't depend on `@drafter/fdx`).
- **2026-10-28** — **Export as Final Draft (.fdx)** has no ellipsis, as Export as PDF has none: it asks only where to save, which every export does. It runs `toFdx` in the export worker beside PDF and text, `application/xml`, and is off (hidden in the menus and palette, refused by `exportScript`) for a Markdown document.
- **2026-10-28** — **The desktop app opens `.fdx` but writes one only as an export.** `OPEN_EXTENSIONS` (scripts plus `.fdx`) decides what the command line, a second launch, and a drop may grant; `.fdx` is in `WRITE_EXTENSIONS` for the export, but the main process writes a `.fdx` only at a real path a Save dialog for `.fdx` chose in this run. So a `.fdx` that was opened is never written over, whatever the page asks. A script saved under a `.fdx` name gets `.fountain` after it (`saveTarget`). The desktop's “opened as a new script” notice is not said for a `.fdx`; the import says what happened.
- **2026-10-28** — **File associations: `.fdx` is offered, never taken**, as Markdown is. macOS: `role: Viewer` (Drafter never edits the file in place), `rank: Alternate`. Windows: an Open with entry (`Drafter.fdx` in `.fdx\OpenWithProgids`, installer/windows.nsh), removed on uninstall. Linux: the desktop entry lists `application/x-fdx` (no type is registered for `.fdx`, so the .deb's MIME file declares this one with the glob `*.fdx`). Linux has no rank, so where no other app claims `.fdx`, a file manager may open it in Drafter by default; that is the same as for `.md` on a system with no other Markdown app. Cost if wrong: a later rename of the MIME type.
- **2026-10-29** — **A synthetic Final Draft test set stands in for real files** (`packages/fdx/test/fixtures/realistic/`). The maintainer can't reach their Final Draft copy, so four files were written by hand with original text and invented values, from the element names the technical design documents, without consulting any real `.fdx` file, Final Draft, or converter (clean room, technical design §0; controller ruling: the settings blocks hold only a few generic element names with obviously made-up values, such as `LeftIndent="1.00"`, `Font="Courier"`, and `#000000`). Their text is original: a short film, edge cases, a revised draft, and a stage play, plus a generator for a 120-page feature that is never written to disk. They test the import's promises on files in the shape the design describes, not Final Draft's own behaviour: real files may still differ (DEFERRED keeps that item, with the guesses the set rests on). Byte-identical copies of two, each with a comment saying it is a synthetic test file, are in `samples/fdx/`, which the app doesn't ship (the web app imports its three samples by name); a test keeps them identical.
- **2026-10-29** — **A title-page paragraph that holds only spaces is a gap** (found by the synthetic set). A title page's blank line is likely written as `<Text></Text>` (a belief, not checked against a real file), and `byOrder` only treated a paragraph with no lines as a gap, so the line after the author (a centered "Based on…") joined the Author. The paragraph's lines are trimmed of blank ones first. Cost if wrong: a title written over two centered paragraphs with a blank one between would come in as Title and Notes; a title's lines are expected to sit together.
- **2026-10-29** — **`TagData` is in `IGNORED`** (found by the synthetic set). Breakdown tags are production data, not writing; in the set (invented, as all its settings are) they hold labels in attributes and reference ids as text, and the ids came in as a `[[fdx: TagData]]` note of meaningless strings. They are listed as ignored like the other settings. Cost if wrong: a file whose tags hold real writing would lose it from the script without a note; it is still named in `ignored`.
- **2026-10-29** — **Text directly inside a `DualDialogue` is kept** (review of the synthetic set: the nothing-dropped check now walks it). Import skipped the pair's text children, so text between its speeches was lost without a note. It now comes in as action with `[[fdx: DualDialogue text]]` in its place, ending the speech before it, as an element there (`[[fdx: Aside]]`) already did; whitespace between the pair's paragraphs is still skipped.
- **2026-10-29** — **The large-input tests prove linear time by growth, not by the clock** (controller request; the 5 MB dialogue test failed every full `pnpm test` at load 11 while passing alone). `scaling` (`test/scaling.ts`) builds the input at n and 4n, warms up, runs the two sizes in turn five times each (so a change in load falls on both) and keeps the best of each, makes the comparison again up to three times when it looks worse than linear (load only ever slows a run; quadratic work looks quadratic every time), and the test requires the 4n time under 8 times the n time; linear work grows about 4 times, quadratic about 16. A 30-second ceiling stays only as a hang guard, and the 5 MB files are still read once. Applied to every wall-clock test in `@drafter/fdx`: the reader's rows and its attributes and entities, the unclosed `[[` openers in action and dialogue, and the feature-length import; the section-depth test keeps the clock only as a hang guard, since its text check proves the depth isn't repeated. With the linear fix in `splitLines` reverted, the two opener tests fail (growth 14.9 and 10.1, after three attempts each). An early version without the turns and the second attempts failed once on the attributes and entities at load 5 (growth 8.6). Cost if wrong: an implementation between linear and quadratic (n log n) passes, which is fine at these sizes.
- **2026-10-29** — **Import follows what a real Final Draft 13 file showed** (the maintainer allowed Final Draft's own bundled sample script; it was inspected for its structure only, element and attribute names, and nothing from it is in the repository, its tests, or its docs; every new case is hand-made with invented text in `fd13-shapes.fdx` and `import.test.ts`). Most of the sample's imported text was one embedded image pasted as a note at the end. The rules:
  - **Images are ignored, never pasted.** `Images` and `Image` are in `IGNORED`; a `Picture` inside a paragraph is a `[[fdx: Picture]]` note in its place (after the open speech, inside one), counted, with nothing of the image.
  - **Outline paragraphs are sections.** A type that starts `Outline N` (any label in parentheses after it) is a section of depth N, or of its `OutlineLevel` when it carries one from 1 to 9; deeper than six is six. An empty one is left out.
  - **`Summary` is a synopsis in its place**, `= ` lines right where the paragraph was, after the item before it. Inside a scene that is after the scene's other blocks, which parses as a synopsis and keeps the order; export then gathers it onto the scene heading, as it does every synopsis in a scene (ruling 6), so a round trip moves it up to the heading.
  - **`Note` is a note on its own line** (`[[…]]`), as `DrafterNote` is.
  - **A scene's `AttributedTitle`** is the title again, styled: ignored (listed) when its text is empty or reads as the `Title` attribute, else a note. **`SceneArcBeats`**: each `CharacterArcBeat` with text is a `[[fdx: Arc beat, NAME: …]]` note after the heading; an empty one is listed as ignored.
  - **Alternates.** A paragraph's `Alts` names alternates by `AltId`; their texts are in the root's `AltCollection` (`Alt Id`), read before the script. The paragraph's own text is the script's text; every named alternate whose text differs from it is a `[[fdx: Alternate: …]]` note after it (after its speech, inside one). `Current` isn't trusted, since the paragraph's text is what Final Draft shows. Alternates no paragraph names come in after the script; `AltCollection` is consumed, not noted whole.
  - **Beat cards** (`ListItems`, `ListItem Type="Beat"`) are one note each after the script, `[[fdx: Beat, Title: text]]` (`Beat: text` with no title, `Beat, Title` with no text; a card with neither is listed as ignored). A `ListItem` of another type, or another element there, goes through the unknown-element path.
  - **Script notes** are one note each after the script: `[[WriterName: text]]` where the note has a writer, the reader's-note form Plan B reads, else `[[fdx: Script note: text]]`; both counted as `Script note`. Their `Range` is not used yet (DEFERRED).
  - **The settings and data blocks the sample carried are in `IGNORED`**: `DocumentRef`, `Actors`, `Cast`, `CastList`, `CharacterHighlighting`, `ScriptNavigatorPreferences`, `Writers`, `WriterMarkup`, `Navigator`, `Outlines`, `TargetScriptLength`, `Characters` (character charts and traits), and `TableColumnSettings` (a list view's columns, a display setting; it can sit inside `ListItems`), beside those already there. A `SpellCheckIgnoreLists` inside a `DualDialogue` was already ignored.
  - **`Version="6"` is accepted**, as any version is (tested); export still writes 5.
  Cost if wrong: a section from an `Outline N` paragraph that a writer meant as plain text now prints nowhere (sections don't print), though it is in the source and the outline.
- **2026-10-29** — **Each alternate comes in once** (security review). A paragraph's `AltId`s look an alternate up in the file's `AltCollection`, so a hostile file could name one large `Alt` from thousands of places and make a script far larger than the file, defeating the 5 MB input cap (the class of the `DrafterSection` fix). Import now brings an alternate in at its first reference only, within a paragraph and across paragraphs; a later paragraph naming it gets no note (its own text is unchanged). Tested: 10,000 references to a 1 KB alternate import to the size of one, and the references are read in linear time. Audit: this is the only place import resolves stored content by id. A `ScriptNote`'s `RefId`, a `ListItem`'s `SubordinateTo`, and a `Picture`'s `Id` are never looked up (the image is ignored), and an `AttributedTitle` is only compared with its own scene's `Title`; everything else import writes comes from the element it reads, so output stays in proportion to the file. Cost if wrong: an alternate genuinely shared by two paragraphs is noted after the first only.
- **2026-10-29** — **Review fixes to the Final Draft 13 shapes** (each test-first):
  - **A paragraph's own text is worked out once, and only when an alternate needs it** (security review). `alternates` read the whole paragraph again for every `Alts` child, even an empty one, so a long paragraph with many `<Alts/>` took time in the product of the two (a 2.5 MB paragraph could hang the page for hours). The paragraph's lines are already read once; the comparison text is made from them lazily, at most once. Tested by growth: the paragraph and its `Alts` grow together, and the time grows about 4 times from n to 4n (it grew about 20 times before).
  - **`ignored` is a set, and lists at most 50 names** (`MAX_IGNORED`); the new `ignoredCount` says how many different names were left out in all. A file of 80,000 different empty elements made the list with a search per name (3.3 seconds), and a 5 MB file could name some 650,000. The list keeps file order, so the first 50 are listed; the app doesn't show it. Tested by growth (about 12 times before).
  - **No setting's or image's content reaches the script by any path.** Every walk of an element's text (`textOf`, `paragraphsText`, and the unknown-element path's walk) skips what `IGNORED` names, and a `Picture`'s content: an `Images` inside an unknown element, or a `Picture` holding text inside a beat card's paragraph, came in with its bytes. The unknown-element path lists what it skipped as ignored.
  - **A note can't be closed early by its own text.** Text ending in `]`, or holding `]]]`, made `[[…]]]` or `] ]]`, which closes one early and leaves the rest as action. Every note import writes (a `Note` or `DrafterNote` paragraph, a script note, an `[[fdx: …]]` label) now gets a space after each `]` that a `]` follows and after a last `]`, as `@drafter/notes` writes a note's body (`safeBody`, `writeNote`; mirrored, not imported, as `fdx` doesn't depend on `notes`).
  - **A second `Alt` with an id already seen is kept** as a note after the script when its text differs from the first's (only the first is looked up by a paragraph); an identical one is left out, since its text is already there.
  - **A `Summary` or `Note` paragraph inside a speech waits for the speech to end** (`place`, as alternates and arc beats do), instead of breaking it: before, the cue came in forced and the dialogue after it as noted action.
  - **What a container holds beside its paragraphs is kept.** A script note, an alternate, a beat card's content, or an arc beat that has paragraphs kept only them; any other element or text beside them is now its own line of the same note.
  - **Stored text is normalized once, however often it is compared** (re-review). A later `Alt` with an id already seen re-normalized the whole first `Alt` to compare with it, even when it was empty, and each `AttributedTitle` re-normalized its scene's `Title`: one large stored text and many small references took time in the product of the two (a 0.3 MB file, 15 seconds; a 5 MB one, hours). The first `Alt`'s text on one line is kept by id when the collection is read (`altLines`), an empty duplicate is skipped before any comparison, and a `SceneProperties`'s `Title` is put on one line once, when its first styled title is compared. Tested by growth, the stored text and its references growing together (about 12 and 15 times before). Audit of every per-occurrence lookup in import after this: `alts`/`altLines` by `AltId` (each id read once, at its first reference, and its stored text normalized once), `usedAlts` and `seen` (sets), the lazy `shown` text of a paragraph (once per paragraph), the scene `Title` (once per `SceneProperties`), `mappedCounts` and `ignoredNames` (a map and a set, by name), and, since the next fix, the title page's Title and Notes fields (kept at hand rather than searched for). Nothing else re-reads a stored value: every other text import writes is read from the element in hand, once.
  - **Runs directly under an element are one line.** `paragraphsText` puts each `Paragraph` on a line of its own and joins what sits between paragraphs (`Text` runs, other elements, text) as it is, skipping whitespace-only indentation; before, each run was its own line, so `<Text>Hel</Text><Text>lo</Text>` read as "Hel lo" and a styled title written that way stopped matching its `Title`.
  - **The title page keeps its Title and Notes fields at hand** (re-review). `byOrder` searched every field for the Notes field at each later paragraph and for the Title at each centered one after a gap, and each centered `by` and author adds two fields, so a page of many credits then many lines took time in the product of the two (about 15 times from n to 4n). The audit above had said those searches stop at the first fields; they do only while the page has few. Tested by growth.
  - **An alternate's stored text is its first `Alt` with any text, across every `AltCollection`** (re-review). An empty first `<Alt Id="a"/>` used to be stored, so a later `Alt` with text and the same id, which a paragraph named, was dropped silently; and each collection kept its own list of ids seen, so an `Alt` in a second collection reusing an id with other text was dropped too. Now the stored `Alt` is the first with text, it comes in at its first reference (or after the script when nothing names it), and every other `Alt` with its id, in any collection, comes in as a note unless its text is the stored one.
  - **No spread of an unbounded array into a call.** `lines.push(...more)` passes each line as an argument, and a title-page paragraph of a million lines (2 MB) overflowed the call stack with a `RangeError` that `fromFdx` didn't catch; export had the same with a line of hundreds of thousands of styled runs. Both now add one item at a time. Grepped: no other call in `packages/fdx/src` spreads an array into its arguments (the remaining spreads build array literals, which have no such limit).
  - **Export walks only a note block's own regions** (last round). A note on its own line looked through every region of the script for the ones inside its block, so a script of many own-line notes exported in time that grew with their square (80,000 notes, 9 seconds). It now starts at the block's first region (`firstFrom`, a binary search) and stops at the first that starts past the block, as `blockPieces` does. Tested by growth; DEFERRED's "Speed on very large exports" item is done.
  - **Nothing loose is dropped.** An element other than `Alt` in an `AltCollection`, or other than `AltId` in an `Alts`, goes through the unknown-element path, as in the sibling containers. Text written loose (not inside an element; whitespace between elements is indentation and stays skipped) directly in the root, a `Content` (the script's or the title page's), a `TitlePage`, an `AltCollection`, an `Alts`, `ScriptNotes`, `ListItems`, or `SceneArcBeats` comes in as action with `[[fdx: Name text]]` in its place, as text in a `DualDialogue` does. The nothing-dropped check counts loose text in each of these.
  - **`mapped`'s description** now says it counts script notes, which come in as `[[Writer: …]]` when they name their writer, as well as `[[fdx: …]]` notes.
- **2026-10-29** — **Import follows what a real Final Draft 13 shooting script and outline showed** (two more of Final Draft's own bundled samples, inspected locally for structure only, as before: nothing from them is in the repository; each case is hand-made with invented text in `fd13-shooting-shapes.fdx` and `import.test.ts`). Both symptoms seen in the shooting script, an action line split around its tagged runs and an outline item after a transition run together with the text around it, had one cause: an **`OmittedScene`**, which Final Draft keeps inside the numbered `OMITTED` heading of a scene the shooting script omits, holding the scene's own paragraphs unprinted. Import didn't know the element, so the unknown-element path put every text run inside it on a line of its own, all as one block of action. The breakdown tags (`TagNumber`) and revision marks (`RevisionID`) on runs were never the cause: runs with them read as any others. The rules:
  - **An omitted scene is notes after its OMITTED heading** (Call). Its paragraphs are read as any others, then each block they make comes in as a `[[fdx: Omitted: …]]` note in its place, its Fountain kept (`# …`, `= …`, a speech's lines), counted as `Omitted`. Nothing of it prints, as in Final Draft, and none of it joins the outline or the strip (an omitted `Outline 1` is not a section); the writer can restore it by taking the note marks off. Not a boneyard: the nothing-dropped rule keeps text out of boneyards, since a boneyard is never listed and export leaves it out, while notes survive a round trip. A page break inside is dropped; an omitted scene inside another, or one loose in `Content`, is read the same way, so there is never a note inside a note.
  - **`HiddenText` runs are notes in their place** (Call): Final Draft doesn't print them, so in an action, a speech, a heading, or a title page each stretch of hidden runs is one `[[fdx: Hidden: …]]` note where it sat, and a blank one is dropped. In a kind that never prints (an `Outline N` item, a `Summary`, a `Note`) hidden text is the paragraph's text, as Final Draft hides outline items anyway, and so it is in an omitted scene, which never prints at all (one note, not a note in a note). Only an omitted scene's outline item carried it in the samples.
    - **The `fdx: Hidden:` label** (review round): a bare `[[…]]` note was a writer's note to Problems, and hidden text reading `Sound team: …` would have read as a reader's note by "Sound team". Labelled, `fdx-note` lists it with its own advice, and each is counted in `mapped` as `Hidden`, so the import's toast counts it among the items that came in as notes.
    - **A note is put in, never read again** (review round): the visible stretches either side of a hidden note are cut into lines each on its own and the note set between them. Inserted as text and read with the rest, a stray `[[` before it (`a [[ b ` then hidden `h` then ` c`) closed at the note, so `a [[ b [[h]] c` printed `a  c`. A `[[` in the stretch before a note that closes nowhere in that stretch is written `[\[` (`breakStrays`, whose backslash prints), and a lone `[` just before a note gets a space after it, since `[[[` would open the note one character early.
    - **A paragraph all of whose text is hidden keeps it as its text** (Call, review round) when its kind needs text to be read as it: a scene heading or shot (and its locked number), a cue, a parenthetical, dialogue, a transition. Made a note, the heading fell back to action with its number lost, and a cue to action with its speech after it. So the text prints, unlike in Final Draft, and a `[[fdx: Hidden paragraph: …]]` note quoting it comes after the paragraph (after its summary, and after its speech inside one), counted as `Hidden`, so the writer can find it and decide. An action all hidden is its `[[fdx: Hidden: …]]` note alone, which keeps it unprinted and loses nothing; any other kind is read as before.
    - **The title** (`title`, the script's name in Drafter) leaves out a hidden run's note with the spaces either side of it made one, and is in capitals where the title is styled All Caps.
  - **`AllCaps` runs come in in capitals**, as Final Draft prints them (each sample has 30-odd, mostly a character's first appearance in action typed in lower case). Upper-cased with `toUpperCase`, so `ß` is `SS`. Export writes no `AllCaps`, so a round trip is unchanged.
  - **`DeletedText` is in `IGNORED`**: its `DeletedTextLocation`s mark where a revision deleted text (offset and revision only, in the sample). Even text held there stays out: deleted is not writing. `LockedPages` already was. Before, it was ignored as an element holding no text, so nothing changes for the writer; listing it by name keeps any text a later file holds there out too.
  - **An omitted scene in or beside a `DualDialogue`** (review round) is read as an omitted scene, as in a paragraph or a `Content`, not by the unknown-element path.
  - Checked and unchanged: the shooting script's 35 printed headings keep their 35 locked numbers (the 36th heading is the omitted scene's own, inside `OmittedScene`, unnumbered); `Strikeout` runs keep their text, unstruck, since Fountain has none; `TableColumnSettings` stays ignored; the outline sample (outline items and summaries only) comes in with its 3, 11, and 35 sections and 49 summaries in file order and nesting, its 51 beat cards as notes after it, and its four `PeerLink` list items (links between cards, no text) listed as ignored.
  Cost if wrong: an omitted scene's paragraphs read as notes rather than script, so restoring one means taking the marks off each note, and since a note can't hold another, a note inside the omitted scene (a script note, an `[[fdx: …]]` note) has its closing `]]` written `] ]`: restored, it must be closed again by hand, or it runs on to the next `]]`. (Hidden text there is text, so it never needs this.) Making restore clean would need a note inside a note, which Fountain doesn't have. A hidden run a writer meant to print comes in as a note; a paragraph whose text is all hidden prints.

## Phase 2, Plan B: Cards and notes (`@drafter/notes`, `@drafter/outline`, `apps/web`)

- **2026-10-29** — **Package layering (Call 1).** `@drafter/notes` depends only on `@drafter/fountain`; `@drafter/outline` depends on it now, and `@drafter/lint` and `@drafter/cm-fountain` will. Design §21's "each package depends only on fountain, templates, layout" is read as a bar on third-party and upward edges; these are workspace edges downward.
- **2026-10-29** — **Shared helpers move down (Call 2).** `safeLine`, `safeBody`, `readKeyedNote`, `removeLine`, `isLineBreak`, `contentEnd`, `hasBlankLine`, `isPlainGap`, `hasStrayBoneyard`, `bodyEnd`, `lineBreakOf`, and the scene palette (`SceneColor`, `SCENE_COLORS`) moved from `@drafter/outline` to `@drafter/notes`. Outline re-exports everything it exported in v0.11.0 (`bodyEnd`, `hasStrayBoneyard`, `lineBreakOf`, `readKeyedNote`, `SceneColor`, `SCENE_COLORS`), so `apps/web` builds unchanged. The `readKeyedNote` cases moved from outline's scene-index tests to notes'.
- **2026-10-29** — **What a head is (Call 4, with controller ruling 1).** A head is letters, spaces, and `' ’ . - ? !`, starting with a letter, on one line, at most 40 characters (the name, for `Name on “quote”`), with spaces and tabs collapsed. Order: a field key, `Setup`/`Payoff`, `Card` (any case, no space needed after the colon), then all capitals (a category, lowercased), `Name re Name`, `Name on “quote”` (straight or curly quotes; the quote may hold a colon and is kept exactly), then a person. A category or person head needs whitespace or the note's end after its colon, so `http://…` and `10:30` are never heads. **A person's name is one to four words, each starting with a capital**, except the lowercase particles de, da, del, della, di, du, la, le, van, von, der, den, ter, bin, ibn, al between two of them (`Luis de la Cruz`), so a sentence with a colon (`Note to self: x`, `Why did she leave? Because: x`, `see: http://a`, `call me: later`) is no head. A single capitalized word (`Remember: x`) still reads as a person, which is accepted. A word with no cased letter (a name in Japanese) passes, so such a name reads as a person. ` re ` and ` on ` are matched in lowercase only (`SAM RE ANN` is a category), and each name in them follows the same rule; `Ann re Bob re Cy` is no head, since `Bob re Cy` isn't a name. **A key is never a person's name**, so `Goal on "x": y` and `Goal re Ann: y` are no head rather than a person called Goal (a field key wins over a person, §5.3, and the quote or reply form has no field meaning). The curly apostrophe is allowed beside `'` because names are often typed with one.
- **2026-10-29** — **Tags.** `#` at a word's start (not after a letter, digit, `_`, or `#`), then a letter, then letters, digits, or hyphens; lowercased, once each, in order. `#resolved` sets the status and is never a tag. Tags are read from the body only, and stay in it as text.
- **2026-10-29** — **Writing.** `writeNote` writes known keys in title case, any other head as given with its spaces collapsed (a quote's words exactly), then `: ` and the body; an empty body is `[[Head:]]`. A body ending in `]` gets a space before `]]`, which reading trims, since `x]]]` would close the note one early. `safeLine` and `safeBody` now break every `[[`, `]]`, `/*`, and `*/` in a run (`[[[` was `[ [[` before, which still opened a note); the output for single pairs is unchanged. **A blank head writes no head**, and **a head that wouldn't read back as one throws a `RangeError`** (`Note to self`, `Sam: Ann`, `10:30`, `Sam]]`): `writeNote` is called by Drafter's own views with heads it builds, so a bad head is a bug to surface, not text to quietly write as the writer's own note. **A head-less note whose body reads as a head gains one when written:** `writeNote(null, 'TODO: x')` is `[[TODO: x]]`, which reads as a `todo` category. Nothing can be written to stop that in Fountain; Task 2's Edit should expect that a note edited to start with `Head:` reads as that head.

- **2026-10-29** — **The Notes list.** `noteEntries` lists every closed note and boneyard in source order with the block holding its opener, never the metadata or Beat Board block. Left out: an unclosed boneyard (the edits refuse while one is stray anyway, and lint reports it) and a note on the title page, which no block holds. A note or boneyard is **alone** when only spaces and tabs share its first and last lines. **A stale action finds its note again (Call 16):** `findEntry` takes the entry with the same text at the nearest offset (the earlier on a tie), no further than 200 characters plus an optional `lengthChange` (how much the script grew or shrank since) from where it was, else null, so an action on a note that changed does nothing and never lands on a far-away twin of a note that is gone. **Replies (Call 7):** `Name re Name` nests under the nearest earlier note by the named person (names matched case-folded), or under the thread that note is in when it is itself a reply, so threads are one level deep; a reply with no such note stands alone.
- **2026-10-29** — **Resolve, Reopen, and Set category touch only the tags (Call 5).** Resolve appends ` #resolved` after the note's last non-space character; Reopen takes out every `#resolved` (any case) with the spaces before it; a tag that starts the body or a line with more after it goes with the spaces after it instead, so `[[Sam: #resolved x]]` keeps `Sam: `'s space and a line's indent stays; a tag alone on a later line of the note goes with that line and the break before it, so no empty line (which would end the note) is left. `setTags` takes out each dropped tag wherever it is the same way and adds new ones as ` #tag` at the body's end; tags are lowercased, and one that isn't a tag word (or is `resolved`) throws a RangeError. Each edit is checked by reading the note back: if the head, status, or tags would read differently (`[[Sam:#resolved x]]` reopened would become `[[Sam: x]]`, a person's note), it changes nothing. **Only notes with a status take these edits** (controller ruling 2): the writer's own (no head, or a category head) and a person's. A scene field, a `Setup`/`Payoff` mark, and a `Card` get `[]` from `setResolved`, `setTags`, and `resolveAll`, since a tag in one would become part of its value or its label. **Edit** (`rewriteNote`) writes the whole note with `writeNote`; a body that starts with a head of its own (`TODO: x` with no head) reads as that head afterwards, as it would if typed (see the writing entry above).
- **2026-10-29** — **Delete.** A note or boneyard alone on its line (or lines) goes with them, through `removeLine`, which now takes a range of a block's lines so a boneyard over several lines goes whole; `[]` where a two-space line beside it would join its neighbours. One that shares its line is replaced by the line breaks inside it (none for a note on one line), so the lines around it print as they did: `Tom sits. [[a⏎long note]] He waits.` becomes `Tom sits. ⏎ He waits.`, still two printing lines. The result is checked by parsing (each block's kind and printing lines' text); where it would print differently, as for a boneyard holding a blank line between two printing lines, Delete changes nothing.
- **2026-10-29** — **A new note goes at the end of the cursor's line, after one space (Call 6).** On a blank line (spaces only, a two-space line included), at the end of the nearest line above with text; with none above, at the very start on a line of its own, before the blank line (an extra line break when the first line is a two-space line). A line that ends inside a multi-line note or boneyard takes it after that region. The cursor goes before the note's `]]`. **The insert is checked by parsing:** if the space would change how the line reads (`CUT TO: ` with a space after the colon is action, not a transition), the note goes in without it (`CUT TO:[[x]]`); if neither keeps every block's kind, marker, and line count and the title page's keys as they were, nothing is inserted. Nothing goes into the metadata or Beat Board block.
- **2026-10-29** — **Setups and payoffs.** Labels match by `normalizeLabel`; a mark with an empty label marks nothing. A label is **unpaid** when its last setup has no payoff after it (only a later payoff pays a setup off); `early` holds the payoffs before every setup (all of them, for a label with no setup). A label with only payoffs isn't unpaid; its payoffs are early. Unpaid labels come first, then by first offset. `markLabels` gives each label once, as first typed, in source order.
- **2026-10-29** — **Category colors** (§5.5) are `"categories": { "name": "color" }` in the metadata block, names lowercased, colors from the scene palette; anything else there is ignored when read and dropped when Drafter next writes the entry. `setCategoryColor` writes through `setMeta` (one change, the block's other keys kept); clearing the last color removes the `categories` key.
- **2026-10-29** — **Scene fields in the index.** Each scene gains `fields` (the first note of each field key anywhere in the scene, read with `readNote` in any case and spacing, its body trimmed), `storylines` (every Storyline note's comma-separated labels, trimmed, once each by `normalizeLabel`, as first typed), and `marks` (every Setup and Payoff note with a label, in order). **An empty field is no field** for reading (`[[Goal:]]`), as Plan A's empty `[[Color:]]` is no color, so `fields.color` and `color` always agree on which note they mean, and `duplicate-field` skips it; Scene details fills the first empty note of a key in where it is when the scene has no field of that key (see the review-fixes entry below). `color` keeps Plan A's shape (the value lowercased).
- **2026-10-29** — **Writing the header stays in `@drafter/outline` (Call 3).** `setSceneDetails` needs a `Scene`; `@drafter/notes` owns the note format (`fieldNote`) and `FIELD_ORDER`. Each field is set on the text as the fields before it left it (synopsis first, then Color, Storyline, Goal, Conflict, Outcome, Shift), reusing `setSynopsis`, `deleteEntry`, and the single-field rules, and the steps are kept as pieces of the original text so the result is **one set of sorted, non-overlapping changes in the original offsets**, each trimmed of text it keeps, never splitting a CRLF. Done this way because two edits to neighbouring header lines (a field cleared and another added in its place, or two adjacent fields cleared) would otherwise overlap or leave an extra blank line. **A field already holding the value isn't rewritten** however it was typed (`[[goal :  win ]]` stays), and the same for a synopsis whose text is unchanged, so saving an unchanged form changes nothing. **A new field's place:** after the last synopsis or field before it in `FIELD_ORDER`, stopping at the first field after it; with none, at the top of the header (so above the writer's other notes there); with no header, after the heading's blank line. A note running over lines is never split. A cleared field goes through `deleteEntry` (its line when alone; only the note, with the spaces around it kept, beside other text). Text fields clear with `''` (the brief's interface types only `color` as nullable). `setColor(doc, scene, color)` is `setSceneDetails(doc, scene, { color })`.
- **2026-10-29** — **Scene details' Storyline is plain text (Call 12).** It is written as `[[Storyline: A, B]]` exactly as typed (one line, safe); storyline names and colors in the metadata block wait for Plan E.
- **2026-10-29** — **`targetBefore`** is the move target just before an item: after the item before it in source order, which for the first scene of a section is the section's start; `{ start: true }` for the first item; null for a block that is no scene or section.
- **2026-10-29** — **Scene details, review fixes.** **Clearing a synopsis keeps every note and boneyard on its line:** the `=` and every run of the synopsis's words before, between, and after them go, and the regions stay one space apart on the line (`= Syn. [[x]] more words.` becomes `[[x]]`; `= /* cut */ Syn. [[Sam: why?]] end.` becomes `/* cut */ [[Sam: why?]]`), a line that prints nothing. Only a synopsis line with no note or boneyard is removed whole (`removeLine`). The result is checked by parsing (the blocks that print, and their printing lines): where the line of notes would join lines next to it and print differently (no blank line or a two-space line around it), nothing changes rather than losing the notes. Changing a synopsis on such a line already rewrites only the text before the first note. **A color in another case is no change** (`[[color: Blue]]` saved as `blue` isn't rewritten). **An empty note of a key is filled in where it is** when the scene has no field of that key (`Action. [[Goal:]]` saved with a goal becomes `Action. [[Goal: Win.]]`, not a second Goal); for reading it is still no field, so `duplicate-field` skips it. **For the form (Task 5):** Storyline is pre-filled from `fields.storyline.body`, since `setSceneDetails` edits only the first Storyline note and `storylines` merges every one of them; and clearing the first of two Goals leaves the second as the scene's goal (`duplicate-field` flags the pair).
- **2026-10-29** — **Setup, payoff, and duplicate-field rules (Call 17).** All three are info. `setup-without-payoff` flags every setup with no payoff after it of its label (the setups after the label's last payoff), so a label is unpaid in `setupsAndPayoffs` exactly when one is flagged; `payoff-without-setup` flags `early` payoffs, with a different message when a setup comes later. Messages quote the note's own label as typed. `duplicate-field` finds scenes itself (a scene heading, or a play's `##`, to the next heading or any section; notes outside a scene are ignored, so a screenplay's `##` ends the scene before it) since lint doesn't depend on outline, and flags the second and later non-empty Goal, Conflict, Outcome, Shift, or Color note. **Storyline is never flagged**, though the index reads only the first Storyline note's body for `fields.storyline`, since every Storyline note adds to `storylines` (§5.4). A property test in outline's `scene-index.test.ts` keeps the rule and the index in step. `noteEntries` now finds whether a note is alone on its line by walking out from it rather than slicing the line, since two hundred thousand notes on one line made the lint quadratic.
- **2026-10-29** — **Scene details, the form (Task 5).** One menu for a scene or section (`scene-menu.ts`), which Outline rows use now and cards will: Go to, the moves, and for a scene Edit synopsis, Color…, and **Scene details…**. The view passes hooks for what touches focus or the view (go, edit, the moves, Move to…, focus); the menu makes a color change itself. The form starts from the first synopsis, the color note's word (one Drafter doesn't know is offered as it is, and kept unless another is chosen), the first Storyline note's body, and the four goal fields; Save writes `changedDetails` (values trimmed, an emptied field `''`, so cleared) through `setSceneDetails` in one apply, and says "Saved the details of INT. HALL." Saving an unchanged form changes nothing and says nothing; a change `setSceneDetails` refuses (a synopsis cleared from a line of notes that would join the lines around it) closes the form and says "Nothing was changed: saving these details would change how the script prints." The dialog holds the scene's id and finds the scene again when it saves; if it is gone, nothing is written and the live region says so. Focus goes back to the row it was opened from (the menu's `focus` hook), else where it was, else the editor. **Read-only, Scene details opens read-only (controller ruling 3):** its menu item and the palette's `scene-details` command stay on; the form shows every field (inputs `readonly`, the color select disabled), the reason above them, and Save `aria-disabled` and described by the reason. Save or Enter says the reason in the live region (as Alt+Arrow does) and the form stays open; Cancel and Escape close it. **The goal's first line** (up to the first ` / `, how a note writes a line break) is in a scene row's description, after the synopsis, as `Goal: …`; a slash inside words is not a break.
- **2026-10-29** — **Cards and Notes are not core tabs (Call 9).** The row stays Source, Script, Problems, Outline, then the slot, then More; `viewTabs(kind)` (Cards, Notes) come before the modules' tabs, first in More. **The slot holds only the pane's own tab or the one chosen last (controller ruling 4)**, at any width, so the row starts as the four core tabs and More, and a tab nobody chose never sits in the row; `tabRow` lost Plan A's fallback to the first other tab, and a single module's tab now waits under More too until it is chosen (or its button opens it). A Markdown document has neither, and a pane showing one when the document turns Markdown shows another tab (`dropTab` for `outline`, `cards`, and `notes`). `Show Cards` and `Show Notes` are commands, off for Markdown. A module's tab is now under More until it is chosen (or its button opens it); the module e2e tests find it there. The Notes tab's list landed in Task 8 (below). The tab row is `position: relative` now, so the More button's hidden words stay inside the row's own scrolling instead of widening the page on a 320px phone. **On a narrow screen (under 480px) More is its chevron alone**, still named "More tabs", and the tabs' side padding is 7px, so the four tabs (with a two-digit Problems count), the slot, and More fit a 375px phone; an e2e test checks More ends inside the screen there, with and without a tab in the slot.
- **2026-10-29** — **Cards are one listbox with a group per section (Call 10).** A group is each run of scenes in the same innermost section, named by its sections outermost first (`Act One, Sequence A`); scenes before the first section are a group with no name. Each card is an option named by `cardName` ("Scene 12, INT. KITCHEN - NIGHT, blue, 1 3/8 pages"; no page number, unlike the Outline row) and described by its synopses; the drawn card is hidden from screen readers. One tab stop, the card last focused (`aria-selected` follows it). **The arrow keys by position:** `gridKey` steps Left and Right, goes a row with Up and Down by the column count measured from the drawn cards (the cards level with the group's first), Down from above a short last row's end goes to its last card, and Down on the last row stays. Over sections, `cardsKey`: Left, Right, Home, and End go through every card in script order; Down from a section's last row goes to the next section's first row in the same column (or its last card), Up to the last row above. On a phone (not wide) the cards are one column. **Enter edits the first synopsis in place** (Enter saves, Escape and leaving cancel, as in the Outline); in a read-only tab Enter says the reason in the live region and opens nothing. Undo and redo keys work on a card, as on an Outline row. The card's ⋯ menu is `sceneMenuItems`; its Move up, Move down, and Move to… run `runMove` and `MoveToDialog` as the Outline's do, and Task 7 adds Alt+arrows, drag, and their tests. The card shows its color as a stripe with the color's name on it, the number, heading, every synopsis, `Goal: ` and the goal's first line, and the length (`lengthLabel`); storyline labels wait for Plan E.
- **2026-10-29** — **Card size (Call 11)** is `cardSize`, `small`, `medium`, or `large`, default `medium`, chosen on the Cards slim bar (a `Card size` group of pressed buttons beside the Outline's scene count and length) and kept in the settings table like the other settings. A size sets the card's least width (the grid fits as many as the pane holds), its height, how many lines of text show, and the text size. The Cards keys are listed under Other keys on the Keyboard shortcuts page.
- **2026-10-29** — **Moving cards (Call 10, Task 7).** Alt+Left and Alt+Right are the Outline's one place earlier and later (`upTarget`, `downTarget`), crossing a section's edge, so a first card can leave its section for the end of the one before. **Alt+Up and Alt+Down move by a row as drawn** (`rowMoveTarget`): before the card a row up in the same column, after the card a row down, by the same rows the arrow keys use (`cardsKey`), so from a section's first row it goes before the card in the last row above; in one column (a phone) a row is one place, `upTarget` and `downTarget`. **The column count is the grid's tracks** (`grid-template-columns` as computed), not the cards level with the first, so a section of one card counts the row as wide as drawn; that fixes Up from such a card, which went to the last card above instead of the same column. **Drag** shows a drop line in the gap on the side the card will land: the card's left or right half in a row of cards, its top or bottom half in one column; a drop before a card is `targetBefore` (the section's start for its first card), after it is just after it. Drag is a pointer's only, on a wide screen and not while read-only (`draggable="false"`); on a phone the card's menu moves it. Every move goes through `runMove`, one undo, focus on the moved card, and `movedMessage` once.
- **2026-10-29** — **What the Notes tab lists (Task 8).** Every `noteEntries` entry but a scene field or a `Card` note (§8, Call). **Groups:** All; My notes (no head, or a category head); Readers (a person's head); Setups & payoffs (the marks); Boneyard. **Filters:** a category (a category head or a tag), a person (case-folded), a status, a scene (the one whose span holds the note's opener), and text (in the note's inner text, any case). **Status belongs to notes that have one** (controller ruling 2): with Open or Resolved chosen, boneyards and marks aren't shown; with All they are. **A thread shows when its note or any reply matches,** with every reply under it, so filtering by a person finds their replies in context. Setups & payoffs is drawn by label (`setupsAndPayoffs`): a group named "the key: not paid off" (or "paid off", or "no setup", with ", a payoff comes before its setup" when one does), its marks in script order, each with its page (`pageOf`), scene, and line; only the text filter (on the label) and the scene filter apply there. `categoriesInUse` lists the categories of notes with a status as first used, then the starting set's others; the filters start at All, any, and no text, and are kept for the visit.
- **2026-10-29** — **The Notes tab's keys (Call 13).** The list is one listbox, every note and reply an option, one tab stop (the row last focused). **A row's identity is its kind and text and which of the shown notes with that text it is** (`note:Check the dates#0`), not its place, so a note going in above leaves every row, its edit fields, and an open menu where they were; a note whose text changes is a new row (an edit open on it closes, saying the note changed). Every key and menu item acts on the entry its row was drawn for, through `act`, and the menu's items are built once as it opens. After an action focus goes to the row again, or, when it is gone (resolved under the Open filter, deleted, rewritten), to the row now in its place. Up, Down, Home, End move; Enter goes to the note in the Source (`goto`); **E** edits in place, a Head field and a Note field (Enter saves, Escape or leaving both fields cancels; moving between them doesn't); **R** resolves or reopens; **C** opens Set category…; **Delete** deletes, and so does **Backspace**, since a Mac's delete key is Backspace; the context-menu key or Shift+F10 opens the row's menu (Go to, Resolve or Reopen, Edit, Set category…, a Color… for each of its categories, Delete); Ctrl/⌘+Z and Y undo and redo from a row, as on a card. The row's Resolve button, ⋯ button, and category chips (whose menu has Color…) are a pointer's (`tabindex="-1"`); double-clicking a row goes to it. The option is named "Sam, Why does she wait? #character, character, open, Scene 1, line 11" (a reply starts "Reply, Ann re Sam", a boneyard "Boneyard", a mark "Setup" with its page); the drawn row says "Scene 1 · line 11". The keys are on the shortcuts page under Other keys.
- **2026-10-29** — **Edit, Set category, and New note (Task 8).** Edit writes the head and text with `rewriteNote`; a head that wouldn't read back as one (`Note to self`) is said in the live region and the fields stay open. With the head left empty, a text starting `TODO:` reads as that head once written, as it would typed, and the live region says so ("Saved the note. “TODO:” at its start reads as its head."). A boneyard isn't edited in the tab (it says so). **Set category…** is a dialog of checkboxes, the categories in use and the starting set, plus a field for new ones (spaces or commas between, a leading `#` dropped); it writes only the tags (`setTags`), and a category head (`TODO:`) shows checked and can't be unchecked there, since it is the note's head. A category's color is chosen from its chip's menu or the row's (`setCategoryColor`, the metadata block); a category head that isn't a tag word (`CUT?`) gets no color. **New note…** (the tab's button and the palette's `new-note`, Edit group, off while read-only with the reason) is a dialog with the text and an optional category (offered from those in use), written `[[text #category]]` by `writeNote` and put in by `insertNote` (Call 6); the editor's cursor goes inside the new note, and focus goes back where the dialog was opened from. Where it can't go in, nothing is added and the live region says so.
- **2026-10-29** — **Resolve all shown** asks "Resolve 7 notes?" (the open notes among the rows shown, replies included) in a dialog whose Cancel has focus, then writes `resolveAll` in one undo and says "Resolved 7 notes." It isn't offered in Setups & payoffs or Boneyard, which have no status; with nothing open shown it says so.
- **2026-10-29** — **A stale note action (Call 16, Task 8).** Every action goes through `useNotes().act`, which finds the row's note again with `findEntry`, passing how much the script grew or shrank since that note was listed (the length is kept for each listed entry, looked up through `toRaw`, since a dialog's ref would otherwise hand back a reactive copy the map doesn't know), so a note is still found after a long insert above it. The Fixture test module gained **Fixture: put in a long note after a pause** (a 312-character note at the cursor) for the e2e test of that. When it isn't found, nothing changes and the live region says "That note has changed since; the list is up to date now." Resolve all shown changes nothing when any of its notes is gone. A refused edit says why: a delete that would change how the script prints, a tag edit that would make the note read differently, an unchanged edit or category.
- **2026-10-29** — **On a phone, the filters are a sheet (Call 14):** a Filters button (it reads "Filters (2)" with two filters set) opens a `<dialog>` like Move to…, the filters apply as they change, and Done gives focus back to the button. The group chips stay in the tab at every width.
- **2026-10-29** — **The open-notes badge (Call 15)** is `openCount`: open notes in My notes and Readers, never marks, boneyards, or fields. A tab with a badge is named with it, **"Notes, 3 open"**, by `aria-label` on the tab (a visually hidden span gave "Notes , 3 open" in Chromium, which puts a space before an out-of-flow element) and as the More item's text; the badge itself is hidden from screen readers. No badge at zero. The Notes slim bar says how many notes are shown and how many are open.
- **2026-10-29** — **Setup and Payoff on the bar (Call 18, Task 9).** `mark-setup` and `mark-payoff` (Edit group, no keys) run `insertMark` in `@drafter/cm-fountain`, a state command over `insertNote` that puts the cursor before the mark's `]]` and asks for completion, through `withCompletion`; they are on only for a Fountain script that can be edited, so a Markdown document has neither button. **The labels come from the editor's completion,** not a picker: `fountainCompletionSource` has a target inside an open `[[Setup:` or `[[Payoff:` on the cursor's line (any case, any spacing) that offers `markLabels`, so typing in a mark gets them too. As with every target the cursor's own line is left out of the parse it reads, so a label used only in the same paragraph (a block on that line) isn't offered, which keeps the label being typed from counting as one in use. When `insertNote` refuses, nothing is added and the bar's notice says so. `@drafter/cm-fountain` now depends on `@drafter/notes`.
- **2026-10-29** — **No Notes tab for Markdown documents (Call 8).** Design §5.7 says notes work in Markdown, but a Markdown document has no `[[…]]` parse to list, so Plan B gives it no Cards, Notes, or Scene details and no Setup or Payoff buttons; the Show Cards and Show Notes commands are off there (`isFountain`). Recorded in `docs/DEFERRED.md` for the maintainer to decide.
- **2026-10-29** — **One live region (Call 19).** Cards and Notes speak through `sayOutline`, the Outline's polite region (`OutlineAnnouncer.vue`), so a move, a resolve, or a stale-action message is one voice for every structure view and never doubles.
- **2026-10-29** — **Menus and help (Task 10).** Show Cards and Show Notes are in the desktop View menu, and Scene details, New note, Mark a setup, and Mark a payoff in Edit; they were already data in `commands/data.ts`, so a test now keeps them there. The Fountain cheat sheet gains Keyed note and Setup and payoff sections, parsed by the same test as the rest. `modules/notes` and `modules/scene-list` READMEs say the Notes and Cards tabs now cover the same ground.

## Phase 2, Plan C: Snapshots and Compare (`@drafter/compare`, `apps/web`)

- **2026-10-29** — **Linear-space Myers on interned lines (Call 22).** `diffLines` interns each line to an integer, sets aside every line found on only one side (it can only be an edit, so the shortest script is unchanged), then diffs the rest with Myers' middle-snake divide and conquer, matching each box's common prefix and suffix before it splits. Ties prefer a removal, and between two same runs the removed lines always come before the added ones, so a changed line reads as removed then added and `changesOf` pairs them into one `changed`. Written from the published algorithm; no diff library's code was read or copied. Two unrelated 5,000-line scripts diff in about 60 ms locally, against a 2-second budget in the tests.
- **2026-10-29** — **No move detection (Call 18).** A moved scene is removed where it was and added where it is, which is what a line diff gives and what revision marks (Phase 4) will mark.
- **2026-10-29** — **Lines and spans.** `\n` and `\r\n` both end a line and the break is in neither line, so the two compare equal; a lone `\r` stays in its line. A text ending in a break has a final empty line. A change's `span` runs from its first line's start to its last line's end, without that line's break; a removal's span is empty at the start of the line it sits before, or at the text's end when it was the last.
- **2026-10-29** — **Package layering (Call 26).** `@drafter/compare` depends on `@drafter/fountain` for the `Span` type only; `@drafter/layout` joined it with the page marks (Task 2), also for types only (`Layout`, `LayoutLine`). Its tests lay their scripts out with `paginate` and the built-in templates, so `@drafter/templates` is a development dependency.
- **2026-10-29** — **Changes on the pages by span.** `markPages` reads the newer text's layout only. A line prints source text when its span is non-empty, which leaves out blank lines and `(MORE)`; title-page lines and a dual dialogue row's `right` part count. A printed line is marked when its span shares a character with an added or changed change's span, so every wrapped line of a changed block is marked, and a repeated `(CONT'D)` cue is marked with the cue. A removal is a gap before the printed line with the smallest span start at or after its place (the nearest line by source, so a removal inside the right column of dual dialogue sits before that column's next line, not the left one's), on that line's page; with none after it, it sits at the end of the last page (`before: null`).
- **2026-10-29** — **A change that prints nothing gets a marker (Call 19).** An added or changed change whose span meets no printed line (blank lines, a note, a boneyard, a synopsis, the metadata block) is an `unprinted` gap placed as a removal is, holding its new lines, with an `unprinted` stop, so the Changes list and Next change reach it. To hold those lines, `Change` gained `added`, the newer lines it put in, beside `removed`. Each change has exactly one stop, in order: its page and block are its first mark's (a gap's are the line it sits before, or the last printed block at the end; title-page lines have block -1), and its line count is the lines removed, for a removal, or put in, for the rest. `stepChange` wraps both ways from a stop index, starting from either end when nothing is focused.
- **2026-10-29** — **No Dexie version bump (Call 1).** A history snapshot is a row in the existing `snapshots` table (`'id, projectId, createdAt'`, version 1), with new unindexed, optional fields: `gz` (the text, gzip-compressed, as a `Uint8Array`, which structured-clones in IndexedDB, not a `Blob`), `reason`, `detail`, `hash`, and `words`; `source` became optional. The `projectId` index is all History queries. A row written before these fields existed reads as it did, and a history row missing them is listed as `manual` with its words counted from `source` (or 0), and is unreadable when it has no text.
- **2026-10-29** — **The saved row stays Phase 1's (Call 2).** `saved:<projectId>`, uncompressed, labeled "Saved", is what Revert to Saved and "unsaved changes" read; History never lists it, thinning never deletes it, and Label and Delete leave it alone. Thinning keeps the newest history snapshot whose `hash` is the project's `savedHash`, which is how the snapshot of the last save stays in History.
- **2026-10-29** — **Never two alike in a row.** A take hashes the text (`hashText`, SHA-256) and adds nothing when it is the newest history snapshot's hash. Takes for one script run one at a time (a queue per project), and the second check and the write share one transaction, so neither two takes in this tab nor one from another tab can both find the newest different. A take dated no later than the newest is dated a millisecond after it, so the newest is always the last taken.
- **2026-10-29** — **Thinning keeps the last of each hour and day, by local time, and always the newest (Call 8).** `thin` is pure, with `now` passed in. Kept: every labeled; the ids it is told to keep (the last save's); the newest; everything at most 24 hours old or dated after `now`; then, among the unlabeled, the last of each local hour (`dateKey` and the hour) to 7 days and the last of each local day (`dateKey`) to 90 days; then none. The last holds the most work; between two dated alike, the greater id wins, so the choice never changes and thinning twice deletes nothing the second time. A clock set back an hour at the end of summer time makes one hour bucket of two. Thinning runs after every take, in one transaction with the project read.
- **2026-10-29** — **Plain text where `CompressionStream` is missing (Call 10),** in `source`, so snapshots still work in an older browser. A `gz` row in a browser without `DecompressionStream` is listed as unreadable; a damaged one is listed and its text reads as nothing (`gunzip` decodes UTF-8 strictly, so damage that survives the gzip check still fails).
- **2026-10-29** — **Storage full (Call 24, the store's half).** A write failing with `QuotaExceededError` (or a Dexie error whose inner error is one) thins the script's snapshots and tries once more; a second failure, any other failure (IndexedDB down, a compression error), and a failed thinning after a take are `'failed'`, or ignored for the thinning, and never thrown.
- **2026-10-29** — **The 10 minutes are active writing time (Call 7).** `createSnapshotSchedule` feeds the writer's own edits (`writersEdit`: not a fix, a template block, or a reload) to a writing-time tracker of its own, so a stretch ends after a 2-minute pause and the pause isn't counted, and a timed snapshot is taken at the edit that brings the active time since the last snapshot to 10 minutes. Any snapshot of the open script (saved, opened, manual, before a bulk operation) starts the count again, even one that found the text unchanged or failed, so no timed one follows straight after a save and a failing store isn't asked again at every key. Hiding or closing the page ends the stretch. Each script keeps its own schedule for the visit, so minutes written on it carry over a switch away and back (6 on A, 6 on B, 4 more on A is A's timed snapshot); a switch only ends the stretch on the script left. Leaving a script takes no snapshot of it: a short burst then would be labeled "Every 10 minutes" without being one, and a writer moving between scripts would leave a snapshot at every switch. The minutes written since are in the recovery copy, and count toward the next.
- **2026-10-29** — **Opening a file takes an "Opened" snapshot (Call 3),** with the file's name as its detail: the opened text is the saved text, so History starts with the file as it opened. ◆ §11.1 doesn't list it.
- **2026-10-29** — **How the session hears of saves and opens.** `useScript().onRecorded` calls its listeners after a save is recorded (`saved`, and a save that finished after a switch, on the script it started on; a saved sample's on the new script it became) and after `openedFile` opens a file as a script. It hands over the text written, so the "Saved" snapshot hashes to exactly the script's `savedHash` and thinning keeps it as the last save's. A listener that throws is caught: the save has happened. A save whose record couldn't be written (the database is down) takes no snapshot either.
- **2026-10-29** — **Nothing for a sample, in a read-only tab, or before a script is open.** `useSnapshots().notKept` says which; `take` resolves `'failed'` and `before` does nothing there, without the status bar's line, since nothing failed. A save finishing on another script after a switch is still that script's, whatever the open one is.
- **2026-10-29** — **A snapshot never blocks typing or saving (Call 9, Call 24's session half).** Every take captures the text synchronously and writes it in the background; the caller never waits. A `'failed'` write shows the status bar's line once per visit (an alert, as a failed save is), and a take that works clears it; a later failure in the same visit says nothing more. It says storage is full only for a quota error (Ruling 1); any other failure (IndexedDB down, a compression error) says "Couldn’t keep a snapshot in this browser. Your script is safe, and saving still works." The store tells them apart with `keepSnapshot`, which answers `{ result, full }`; `takeSnapshot` stays the plain `TakeResult` for every other caller.
- **2026-10-29** — **A take for a script that is gone adds nothing.** The store's write transaction reads the project with the history, so a take racing the script's removal leaves no orphan row; it answers `'same'` (there is nothing to keep), not `'failed'`, so the writer isn't told storage failed.
- **2026-10-29** — **One whole-text replacement for Revert to Saved and Restore.** `replaceText` (`editor/replace-text.ts`) replaces only the part between the common prefix and suffix, in one transaction with `isolateHistory('full')` and `appChange`, so the cursor and folds stay, it is one undo of its own, and it counts no writing time; the same text dispatches nothing; it refuses (false) when the editor is read-only. `revertToSaved` now calls it; its behavior and tests are unchanged.
- **2026-10-29** — **History is one listbox grouped by day (Call 12),** the Notes tab's pattern: one tab stop, Up, Down, Home, and End move, and on the focused row Enter compares with now, C compares with…, R restores, L labels, S saves as a file, Delete or Backspace deletes, and the context-menu key or Shift+F10 opens the row's menu (`historyKey`). A day is a group named Today, Yesterday, or "Tuesday, 27 October" (with the year when it isn't this one), by local time; a row's words change is against the next older snapshot, across days. Each row draws Restore and a ⋯ menu for a pointer (`tabindex="-1"`). Compare with now and Compare with… are in the menu (Task 6 turned them on). The row's change in words is `change` beside the count in `words`, since one field can't be both.
- **2026-10-29** — **Take snapshot lives at the top of History (Call 13),** with an optional label; the palette's Take snapshot… opens History with the label field focused. A take that finds the text unchanged says so with the newest snapshot's time, and that a label goes on it with Label…; a take for a script that is gone (the store answers 'same') says the script isn't in this browser any more.
- **2026-10-29** — **The status bar's save text is a button that opens History (Call 14),** named "History, Saved 4 min ago" (or whatever it says: "Unsaved changes", "Not saved to a file yet", a sample's line).
- **2026-10-29** — **Restore** takes `before-restore` (captured synchronously by `useSnapshots().before`) and then `replaceText`s the snapshot's text; it reads the row again by id, and the script and tab are checked again after the reads, so a snapshot gone since, unreadable, or a switch or a lock meanwhile changes nothing and says why. History closes first, so what Restore says is heard from the page's live region, and the script gets focus. Restoring text equal to the script's says so and takes nothing.
- **2026-10-29** — **What History says is shown in the dialog too.** A modal `<dialog>` makes the page behind it inert, which takes `OutlineAnnouncer`'s live region out of what a screen reader hears; History draws `outlineNotice` in a polite status line of its own while it is open, so `sayOutline` stays the one source of what is said. A row's ⋯ menu is portaled into the dialog for the same reason, and an Escape that closes the menu doesn't also close History.
- **2026-10-29** — **Save as a file (Call 23)** writes "Title (snapshot 2026-10-29 1042).fountain" (`.md` for Markdown; the title is the snapshot's own, cleaned and cut as Save's is, so the time is never cut off), encoded with the script's line break and BOM, through `pickToSave` where the browser can, else as a download. The script's file and binding are untouched.
- **2026-10-29** — **A read-only tab's History (Call 25)** lists and saves snapshots; Restore, Label…, Delete, and Take snapshot are `aria-disabled` with the reason shown and said: another tab is editing the script, or Drafter is checking its file (the Outline's words). Not the Outline's unclosed `/*` reason: a whole-text replacement doesn't depend on the parse. A sample's Take snapshot says why samples keep none.
- **2026-10-29** — **History's keys are on the Keyboard shortcuts page under Other keys** (`pages/docs/shortcuts.vue`, where the Notes and Cards keys are), not in `commands/shortcuts.ts`, which only groups registry commands.
- **2026-10-29** — **"Now" in Compare is the text when it opened (Call 15),** with **Update** on the slim bar ("Comparing 10:42 with now (10:58)"), so the marks don't move while the writer reads. Update is there only when one side is now; between two snapshots there is nothing to update.
- **2026-10-29** — **Compare is an "other" tab, first while open (Call 16).** `COMPARE_TAB` is put first in a pane's other tabs only while a comparison is open, so it takes the slot of the pane `comparePane` picks: the one not showing Source, the right one when neither does (on a phone, the one pane). Like Cards and Notes, the slot is shared, so the other pane's row holds it too, and choosing it there swaps. Close comparison (its slim bar, or `close-compare`), opening another script, or the document changing kind takes it away with `dropTab`, and the slot empties.
