Focus in its pane, or from its Close comparison button (Safari doesn't focus a clicked button), goes to the panel of the tab that replaces it. Opening a comparison while one is open replaces it.
- **2026-10-29** — **Compare lays out with a worker of its own (Call 17),** a second `createLayoutClient` on `workers/layout.worker.ts`, made when a comparison opens and ended when it closes, so the Script pane's layout and its pages are untouched. Its pages are drawn with `@drafter/render-html`'s `buildPage`, now exported, at fit width as the Script pane's, and marked by `markPage` (`compare/draw.ts`), which matches a mark's span to each line's `data-from`/`data-to`. The asterisk is a line of its own five columns in from the page's right edge, hidden from a screen reader; the hidden "changed, " or "added, " is the line's first child. A gap is a thin rule at the top of the row of the line it sits before, with its button in the left margin and its lines in a box under it when open; in reading order it goes before the outermost speech or block that line begins, never inside a heading. Text reaches the page only through `textContent`.
- **2026-10-29** — **A stop's scene is read by source, not by its block.** The Changes list names a change by the scene it is in: the last scene starting at or before the change's place in the newer text, and for a removal strictly before it, since the removed lines came after the line before their place. The stop's block is the next printed line's for a removal (another scene's heading, or -1 on the title page), which would name the wrong scene. The title page is "Title page", not "Page 0". An unprinted change says "1 line changed that doesn’t print".
- **2026-10-29** — **Markdown documents get Compare as marked source lines (Call 20),** `CompareLines.vue`, which also draws a script whose layout failed (or whose worker couldn't be made). Each line has its number (hidden from a screen reader), the marks, and the removed-lines buttons the pages have; the Changes list says "Line 40", a removal naming the line it sits before (the last line at the end).
- **2026-10-29** — **Next and Previous change are N and Shift+N in the Compare tab (Call 21),** heard on its panel, which has no text input, and `next-change` / `previous-change` in the palette (View) from anywhere while a comparison is open, which show the tab first. Each moves focus to the change's first mark (a marked line, focusable with `tabindex="-1"`, or a gap's button) and says its Changes-list label once through `sayOutline`; a change asked for before the pages are drawn gets focus when they are. They wrap. The keys are on the Keyboard shortcuts page under Other keys.
- **2026-10-29** — **Focus anywhere but the editor lets go of the editor's selection (Ruling 2).** Safari types into a contenteditable that holds the page's selection even when focus is elsewhere, so keys pressed in the Outline, Cards, Notes, Problems, or Compare tab, on a tab in a tab row, on a button (the status bar's History, the toolbar's Focus, the template chip), or with nothing focused changed the script. One place covers it all, `guardEditorSelection` (`utils/focus.ts`, installed once in `pages/index.vue`): a document `focusin` outside the editor's content clears a page selection lying in it (`releaseEditorSelection`), and so does the content's `focusout` to nothing (`relatedTarget` null, a click on the background), on the next tick if focus hasn't come back (a window losing focus keeps it, since the editor is still the active element). CodeMirror keeps its own selection and puts it back when the editor has focus, so the cursor stays where it was. With the selection gone, WebKit's Backspace outside a text field would go back a page, so a document `keydown` prevents that Backspace's default (`backspaceGoesBack`); it runs after the element's own handlers, so a tab's own Backspace (Notes' delete, History's) still runs, and inputs and contenteditable are left alone. The tab panels' own handlers in `AppPane.vue` are gone. Three tests had been typing with focus elsewhere and leaning on the selection left behind (Playwright's `insertText` writes at the page's selection, and Firefox's Ctrl+Z went to the editor from a closing menu): `save.spec.ts` now puts focus in the editor after clicking Save and Save As, and `revert.spec.ts` waits for the menu to be gone before its undo. Compare also prevents the default of a character, Backspace, Delete, and Enter off a button. `e2e/panel-keys.spec.ts` (each tab, a tab-row tab by click, the status bar's History button, a blurred editor, and Backspace with a page to go back to) and Compare's read-only test check it on every desktop browser.
- **2026-10-29** — **History's Delete question ignores a close that comes after it opened again.** A dialog's `close` event is queued after `close()`; Chromium could deliver Cancel's after a Backspace had already opened the question again (the browser had already given focus back to the row), so `onAskClose` emptied the open question and focus fell to the body. `onAskClose` now returns while the question is open. A label or delete the store throws on says "Couldn’t change the snapshot." and reads the list again.
- **2026-10-29** — **A snapshot before every bulk operation (Task 7; Calls 4, 5, 6, 9).** Expand outline (once it has changes), Fix all (once it has changes), Revert to Saved (once it can revert and the editor is writable), and `replaceWith` (the changed-file bar, and Open over a script, after the held and busy checks) call `useSnapshots().before(reason)` just before they change the text, so the snapshot holds the text exactly as it was and the operation never waits for it. ◆ Reloading a changed file takes `before-reload` (Call 5). It is a duplicate of the newest snapshot, so none, when the script was unchanged since its last snapshot; the reload's own save then adds "Saved" above it, so it is the second row. FDX import has no "before" (Call 4): `useSnapshots().afterImport(text, name)` keeps the imported text as `imported` with the file's name as its detail, once the new script is made. Start blank and Reset sample take none (a sample keeps none), nor does a single scene or card move (Call 6); a reader merge (Plan G) will call `before('before-merge')`. `storage/script.ts` and `use-snapshots.ts` import each other, both only at call time.
- **2026-10-29** — **The change in words (Call 11).** History shows each snapshot's change as `wordsIn` of its whole text against the next older snapshot's, the same count the status bar uses; the oldest shows none.
- **2026-10-29** — **History and Compare in the desktop menu (Task 8).** History… and Take snapshot… are in File, after Revert to Saved. Next change and Previous change moved from Go to View, beside Close comparison, so all of Compare's commands share one menu; they apply only while a comparison is open. The menu is still built from the command registry, so the palette and the Keyboard shortcuts page list them as before.

## Help: About, samples, and updates

The maintainer asked (2026-10-29) for a Help menu with version info, "some sample scripts (all tiny and synthetic) for each template", the GitHub link, and a "check for update". The choices made while building it:

- **2026-10-29** — **One version, the desktop app's (Call).** `apps/web/scripts/version.mjs` reads `apps/desktop/package.json`'s version, the one a release tag must equal and the one Electron reports as `app.getVersion()`, and `nuxt.config.ts` defines it as `__DRAFTER_VERSION__`; the web package gets no version of its own to forget to bump. `test/version.test.ts` checks it is the desktop app's and every package's. In the desktop app, About shows the version the bridge reports (Electron's), which is the same number.
- **2026-10-29** — **The build's commit and its date, when git can say (Call; optional).** `git log -1 --format='%h %cs'` at build time gives `__DRAFTER_COMMIT__` and `__DRAFTER_COMMIT_DATE__`; About shows "Build abc1234 (2026-10-29)", and a build from a source archive, with no git, leaves the line out. The commit's date, not the build's, so building one commit twice gives the same files.
- **2026-10-29** — **About Drafter is a dialog in the page, in the desktop app too (Call).** The `about-drafter` command (Help) opens `AboutDialog.vue`, shaped like the other `<dialog>`s: "Drafter", the version, a one-line description, which app this is (with the platform and processor, and Electron's and Chromium's versions, from the preload's `runtime`: `process.arch` and `process.versions`, which a sandboxed preload has), the build, the MIT licence, the links, and the help pages. In the desktop menu (Help, and the Mac's Drafter menu) About Drafter runs that command while the page can; with no page to show it (before it has sent its commands, or a Mac with no window) it is the native box it was (`menu.ts` `ABOUT_COMMAND`). The page's command never takes the native `about` id, so `parseCommands` is unchanged.
- **2026-10-29** — **About's links leave the app the safe way.** GitHub, Releases, the Changelog, and drafter.dev are `<a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">`: a new tab on the web; in the desktop app the window-open handler (`window.ts` `guard`) gives an https address to the system browser and never navigates the app's window. The help pages run their Help commands, as the menu does, so the page keeps its opener and Back to Drafter closes it.
- **2026-10-29** — **Copy version info copies a few plain lines, nothing personal (Call).** Version, app, build, platform, and the browser (`Chrome 142`, from the user agent) or Electron and Chromium: no paths, names, or script text. It uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, and where that is refused, the browser's own Copy of a hidden field, so the desktop app needs no new permission (`permissions.ts` still allows only `persistent-storage`). It says "Copied." in a polite status line, or that it couldn't.
- **2026-10-29** — **Sample scripts are new files, not the annotated samples (Call).** `apps/web/app/editor/sample-scripts/` holds a screenplay, a stage play, and a Markdown document, written for Drafter (no line from any film, play, show, or other app). Each is under a page in its template. The screenplay has every Fountain element kind once (a lyric, a boneyard, and a page break included; the test checks every block kind is there); the stage play its acts, scenes, cues, parentheticals, and stage directions; the Markdown document headings, emphasis, a list, a quote, and a link. `test/sample-scripts.test.ts` checks they parse with no Problems errors or warnings and lay out on one page. The annotated samples (File → Open sample) were not reused: those are shared records that Reset sample puts back, and the first New of a kind opens with one. A sample script opens as a new script of the writer's own (`addScript`, then `open`, as New screenplay from this outline does), titled from its text ("Sample: Screenplay"), with its text as the baseline, so it opens unchanged and with no file, and the script that was open is kept as any switch keeps it.
- **2026-10-29** — **A sample script is not a meeting with the annotated sample (Call).** The project carries `sampleScript` (unindexed, optional: no database version), and `metSample` ignores it, so opening Help's stage play doesn't stop the writer's first New stage play from opening with the annotated one.
- **2026-10-29** — **Check for updates on the web asks the service worker, and nothing else (Call).** `update/check.ts` gets `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration()` and calls `registration.update()`: the browser's own request for `/sw.js`, made once because the writer asked. No GitHub request, and no other new network call. A worker already waiting, or one that installs within 60 seconds, is "A new version of Drafter is ready. Reload to use it." with Reload; otherwise "You’re on the latest version (v…)". No service worker (a dev server, the desktop build, a browser without one, or a registration that failed) says so. A failed `update()` (offline) says so plainly. This is the one exception to "no periodic update check" (2026-10-26): it is not periodic, and only the writer starts it.
- **2026-10-29** — **What the check finds is the offer the app already has.** A found version sets `needRefresh` (and on the desktop `updateKind` and `availableVersion`), so the toast in `UpdatePrompt.vue` is the offer; the dialog's Reload, Restart, or Get it is the toast's own choice (`takeUpdate`: Later undone, then the update flow), so changes that aren't in a file still get Save first, Reload anyway, or Cancel, and the recovery copy is flushed before anything goes. The toast's question takes focus when it appears after the dialog closes. Close leaves the offer up.
- **2026-10-29** — **Check for updates in the desktop app checks now, whatever the setting (Call).** A new invoke channel, `drafter:update:check`, answered only for the editor's own page (`fromEditor`), runs `Updates.checkNow()`: it makes the updater if the start hasn't, and runs one check (a check under way, the start's included, is that check). An asked-for check may offer and download with "Check for updates when Drafter starts" off, and the setting doesn't stop a download it began: the page says the setting at every start (a reload, a Mac window opened again), and the dialog has already said Drafter will offer to restart. A download the start's own check began still stops when the setting is turned off. Only an asked-for check that worked counts as the start's check, so a failed one before the start's wait is over doesn't cost the session its automatic check. It answers `latest`, `found` (offered too), `downloading` (the AppImage or Windows app; Restart is offered once it has downloaded), `off` (an unpackaged build without the test feed: "This copy of Drafter can’t check for updates"), or `error` (logged; "You may be offline"). The page keeps only the fields and values it knows from the answer.
- **2026-10-29** — **Where they are.** The commands are in Help, so the palette and the Keyboard shortcuts page list them: `sample-script-screenplay`, `-stage-play`, `-markdown` ("Sample script: Screenplay"…), `check-for-updates` ("Check for updates…"), and `about-drafter`. The web menu's Help section has Take the tour, the help pages, a **Sample scripts** submenu, Reset sample, Check for updates…, and About Drafter. The desktop Help menu has the help pages, Take the tour, a **Sample Scripts** submenu, **Check for Updates…** (title case, as the desktop's other labels), then Open Modules Folder, Open the Releases Page, and About Drafter (on a Mac, About is in the Drafter menu). No new axe tests (the accessibility work comes last); the existing ones stay green.

## Phase 2, Plan D: Spellcheck (`@drafter/spell`, `@drafter/cm-fountain`, `apps/web`)

- **2026-10-29** — **The dependencies (Call 1).** `nspell` 2.1.5 (MIT; brings `is-buffer` 2.0.5, MIT), `dictionary-en` 4.0.0 and `dictionary-en-gb` 3.0.0 (`(MIT AND BSD)`: SCOWL's permissive notice, Geoff Kuenning's BSD licence for the Ispell affix data, and the WordNet, UKACD, and VarCon notices), and `@types/nspell` 2.1.6 (MIT, development). Checked again at install with `npm view` and `npm pack`: each is the latest release, `nspell` has `is-buffer` as its only dependency, and neither dictionary's `license` mentions the GPL, the LGPL, or copyleft (the two files differ only in their title and build line). Pinned exactly (no `^`), and `packages/spell/test/licences.test.ts` fails on a changed version, a changed licence, an extra dependency in `@drafter/spell`, or a dictionary licence naming the GPL or LGPL (§21).
- **2026-10-29** — **A Third-party licences help page (Call 3).** `/docs/licences/`, prerendered and in `HELP_PAGES` (so the palette and the app menu's Help list it), names nspell, is-buffer, and each dictionary with its version, its declared licence, its copyright lines (SCOWL's, Ispell's, UKACD's in bold, WordNet's, and VarCon's), its terms in a line, and a link to its licence file; and Courier Prime and Inter Tight under the OFL. Each licence file is served verbatim at `/licenses/<package>.txt`, because the UKACD notice requires that its copyright notice be "prominently displayed" and its text "included verbatim". Inter Tight's OFL text isn't served on its own: the page points to Courier Prime's, the same licence. The desktop app's native Help menu lists it too, after Privacy, as **Third-party Licences** (see the final-review entry below).
- **2026-10-29** — **The dictionaries are static files, copied and precached (Call 4).** The packages' `exports` is only `./index.js`, which reads the files with `node:fs`, so they can't be imported or be Vite assets. `scripts/sync-assets.mjs` finds each package's folder as `@drafter/spell` resolves it (`packageDir`: `createRequire(...).resolve`, then up to the folder whose `package.json` has that name; `is-buffer` from nspell's folder) and copies `index.aff` and `index.dic` to `public/dictionaries/<language>/` and the licence files to `public/licenses/`, Git-ignored as the fonts are. The service worker precaches `**/*.{aff,dic}` with the rest of the app (four files, 1.11 MB; each `.dic` is 552 KB, under Workbox's 2 MiB limit). The licence `.txt` files were at first not precached; the final review changed that (see below). `scripts/check-build.mjs` checks that the worker caches each `.aff` and `.dic`, as it does the other app files, and fails a build (the web's and the desktop's) without any of the four dictionary files or the four licence files.
- **2026-10-29** — **What's checked in a scene heading, and which words are known (Call 6).** A scene heading's prefix and location are never checked; its time (`NIGHT`, `MOMENTS LATER`) is, found after the heading's last ` - ` (or ` – `) with the scene number taken off first, as `parse` finds it. Every word of every cue's name and every scene heading's location is harvested (`harvestedWords`), lowercased, with a possessive's base form too (`ZEPHYRINE'S TRUCK` gives `zephyrine's` and `zephyrine`), and never saved. A word is known when its lowercased form, or that form without a final `'s`, is in the set (`lookupForms`, `isKnown`), so `MARGARET` as a cue knows `Margaret’s` in action, and the script's and My dictionary's words match in any case too. A hyphenated word is checked part by part, and `’` is read as `'`. Emphasis never splits a word: a run of `*` with a word character on both sides joins it, so `un*believ*able` and `*un*believable` are each one token, `unbelievable`, whose `from` and `to` span the whole source run (the mark covers `un*believ*able`), while a `*` at a word's edge ends it (`*did*` is `did`). A token's `word` is its source with those `*`s removed and `’` read as `'`, so `word.length` may differ from `to - from` (Task 3's checker and suggestions take `word`, the marks take `from` and `to`). `_` never joins, so `snake_case` is `snake` and `case`, not `snakecase`. An address is a whitespace-delimited chunk with `://`, `www.` at its start (so `Awww.` is a word), a letter@letter, or a dotted domain ending in 2–6 letters, after its surrounding punctuation is taken off; the whole chunk is skipped.
- **2026-10-29** — **What's never checked (Call 7).** `fountainUnits` gives a unit per action, dialogue, parenthetical, centered, lyric, section, and synopsis block, and a scene heading's time; never a cue (with its extensions and `^`), a transition, a page break, the title page (it isn't a block), a scene number, or a forcing mark (an action's first `!`, each lyric line's `~`, a centered line's `>` and `<`, a section's `#`s, a synopsis's `=`). A unit's text is its source, the same length, with what isn't checked turned into spaces (and each CRLF's `\r`), so a token's offsets are the source's, astral characters included. Notes and boneyard are blanked in every block; with **Check notes and boneyard**, each is a unit of its own, its `[[`/`]]` or `/*`/`*/` blanked, and a keyed note's head blanked (the text before its first colon, on one line, at most 40 characters, starting with a letter, as Plan A reads a head). `[[Color: …]]` and `[[Card: …]]` are left out whole, since their bodies are values Drafter reads, not the writer's words. The metadata block is never a unit, even when its JSON can't be read: any `/* drafter` boneyard in the last block. A note or boneyard in the title page isn't either. A unit's key is its kind and its blanked text, so equal blocks share a cache entry.
- **2026-10-29** — **The script's dictionary (§5.5).** `dictionary` in the metadata block is a list of strings; `scriptDictionary` reads its strings and ignores anything else (a string where the list should be reads as empty). `addToScriptDictionary` writes through `setMeta`, as the note categories do: one change, the block's other entries kept, the list sorted case-insensitively (an English collator at base strength, then by code unit, so the order is the same in every browser) and without a word already there in any case, the damaged entries dropped; [] when the word is there already or a `/*` is stray (unclosed, or inside a boneyard).
- **2026-10-29** — **Markdown is read by a small scanner (Call 16).** `markdownUnits` gives a unit per paragraph and per ATX heading line, and blanks front matter (`---` or `+++` on the first line, to its closing line), fenced code (at any indent, so a fence under a list item counts; an unclosed fence runs to the end), indented code (four columns, only where it can't continue a paragraph), link reference definitions, HTML comments (across lines), code spans (an unmatched backtick run stays text), autolinks, HTML tags, link destinations and titles (`(…)` after `]`) and full references' labels (`[…]` after `]`), each bracket's partner found in one pass with a stack, so a paragraph full of unclosed `](` is linear, and entities (`&amp;`). Link text and image descriptions are checked. An indented list continuation after a blank line reads as indented code and isn't checked: a missed word, never a false mark.
- **2026-10-29** — **Other scripts (Call 21, in part).** A word with no Latin letter (`Ελένη`, `東京`, Deseret) isn't checked. Trying an accented word without its accents is the checker's (Task 3).
- **2026-10-29** — **Accents (Call 21, the rest).** `createChecker`'s `correct` is nspell's, or nspell's for the word decomposed (NFD) with its combining marks removed, so `café`, `naïve`, `résumé`, and a decomposed `cafe\u0301` are known (both dictionaries have them only without accents), while an accented word that isn't a word without its accents still is not. Suggestions are nspell's own for the word as written, the first five. nspell reads a string or a Node Buffer only (it tests `is-buffer`), so a plain `Uint8Array`, as the worker's `fetch` gives, is decoded with `TextDecoder` first. Loading a dictionary takes some 20 to 40 ms in Node; a checker is made once per language and kept.
- **2026-10-29** — **One- and two-letter words are never marked.** The pieces a split leaves (`e.g.` as `e` and `g`, `rock 'n' roll`'s `n`, `'em`'s `em`) and words like `ok` are left out of a unit's tokens before anything is checked. Both dictionaries already know every single letter and most two-letter pieces, but not `ve` or `ok`; a missed check is better than a false mark. A word of three letters or more is checked as before (`Teh`).
- **2026-10-29** — **The session (Call 5).** `createSpellSession(checker)` parses a Fountain script itself (`parse`), or scans a Markdown document, so the worker sends only the text. It keeps two caches: each unit's tokens by its key (kind and blanked text, offsets from the unit's start, so a block moved by an edit above it reuses them), and each word's verdict, by the word exactly as written, since nspell's answer depends on case. The known set (harvested names and locations, the script's dictionary, `mine`) is built again on every check and filters the cached verdicts without changing them, so adding a word to either dictionary, or removing it, checks nothing again; a Markdown document's known set is `mine` alone (it has no cues or headings). Misspellings are sorted by `from`, because with notes on a note is a unit after its block's. Both caches drop the least recently used entry past their bound (20,000 unit keys, 200,000 words; a test passes smaller ones), and a fast-check property shows that a session after any edits, with any notes setting and My dictionary, and with tiny caches, gives what a fresh session gives. `work()` counts units tokenized and words sent to the checker, for tests. Measured in Node on a 213,000-character script whose every action and speech line is different: the first check about 35 ms, a check after a one-letter edit about 10 ms (mostly `parse`); the tests' ceilings are 3 s and 50 ms (the best of five edits).
- **2026-10-29** — **A planted misspelling in each fixture.** Each of Task 2's fixtures now holds one misspelled word (`kettel`, `windoe`, `stvoe`, `stikker`, `dragz`; the notes fixture's `recieve` is in a note) and `never-flagged.test.ts` checks that it's the only word a session marks there. The skips fixture's action gained `ORMSBY` (in its dictionary), `LN`, `44B`, a domain, and an e-mail address; the notes fixture gained a metadata block with `Odalys` in its dictionary, since it has no cue for her. Task 2's tests changed only in those words.
- **2026-10-29** — **The marks (Calls 5 and 17).** The spelling marks are a state field in `@drafter/cm-fountain` (`spell.ts`), not lint diagnostics, and outside `fountain()`, so a Markdown document has them. They are a `DecorationSet` of `Decoration.mark`s, each carrying its word; a keystroke maps the set (RangeSet's own map) and filters only the changed ranges, dropping each mark an edit overlaps or touches, so typing at either end of a word or inside it takes its mark away until the next check. A word's mark is made in the reply's dispatch, never in a keystroke. `ignoreHere` keeps a second set of ranges, mapped and dropped the same way, and `setSpellMarks` leaves out a mark whose range is one of them exactly; `unmarkWord` compares lowercased words. `stepSpellMark` steps over a mark touching the position (the one the cursor is on): next is the first that starts after it, previous the last that ends before it, wrapping. The mark is `text-decoration: underline dotted 2px var(--spelling)` with a 3 px offset, added to any underline of the word's own; `--spelling` is `#ff6b6b` dark and `#c62828` light, and `tokens.test.ts` asserts 3:1 on `--bg` in both themes.
- **2026-10-29** — **The worker and its client (Call 5).** `spell-compute.ts` holds one session per language, made when that language's dictionary is loaded (`fetchDictionary`: `index.aff` and `index.dic` from `DICTIONARIES[language].folder`, which `connect-src 'self'` allows); a language change makes a new session, since a session's verdicts are cached by word without the checker's identity. A request before the load has finished waits for it; a failed load is one `failed` reply (whoever sees it first) and is forgotten, so the next request tries again. The client (`spell/spell-client.ts`) numbers every request, shows only the newest check's reply, reports `loading`, `ready` (on `ready` or any `checked` reply), and `failed` (a `failed` reply, an `error` or `messageerror`, or a `postMessage` that throws), and answers a waiting suggestion with none when the worker fails. Like the Markdown client it takes a delay, which `useSpell` sets to 0: `useSpell` takes the 300 ms pause itself, so a keystroke never reads the whole text (`doc.toString()`) and never calls the client; at the pause it takes the text and starts composing every change since into one `ChangeDesc`, through which the reply is mapped (`mapSpellMarks`), so a reply to text typed over since lands on the words it was made for.
- **2026-10-29** — **When the spellcheck runs (Calls 8 and 20).** `useSpell` makes its worker once the settings are read, the Spellcheck setting is on, and the first script is open (`useScript().ready`: on a first visit the sample is already in the editor, with no `load`), and ends it when the setting goes off; the setting's compartment holds `spellMarks()` when on and nothing when off, so the marks go with it. A load (`onChange(null)`), a change of kind, or a change of language clears the marks and checks at once; Check notes and boneyard and My dictionary check at once without clearing. A failed load is said once per visit in the status bar ("Spellcheck couldn’t load its dictionary, so no words are marked. Everything else works."), clears the marks, leaves the setting on, and sends nothing more until the next script, kind, language, or setting change, so a dictionary that can't be fetched isn't fetched again at every pause. That retry ends the worker and makes a fresh one (the old one may be what failed, an `error` from a worker that died), and a retry that fails stops the checks again: the client reports every `failed`, even one after another. A later success clears the line; turning Spellcheck off and on makes a failure news again. A browser that won't make the worker is treated as a failed load. A load changes the kind and the text at once, and its `onChange(null)` checks: the kind watcher checks only when the kind changed without a load.
- **2026-10-29** — **The settings (Calls 8 and 9).** `spellLanguage` (`'en-US'` by default, one of `SPELL_LANGUAGES`), `spellNotes` (off), and `personalDictionary` (`[]`; valid only as a list of non-empty strings without whitespace, so one bad entry makes the whole stored value fall back to the default rather than be half-read). `set` copies a list before keeping it, since a list read from the settings is a Vue proxy, which IndexedDB and a worker can't clone; `useSpell` copies My dictionary again before posting it. `personalDictionary` is typed `readonly string[]`, as the settings are read through `readonly`. Settings' controls for the three come in Task 6; Task 4's tests set them in the settings table.
- **2026-10-29** — **The browser's spellcheck is off in the Source pane (Call 18).** `spellcheck="false"` is a fixed content attribute of the editor, outside the compartment, so it is off with Drafter's spellcheck on and off; the Spellcheck setting's description says "Underline misspelled words in the Source pane, with Drafter’s own dictionary, which works offline." Other text fields keep the browser's spellcheck.
- **2026-10-29** — **The long-task tests.** `spell.spec.ts` types into some 120 pages of the sample with a misspelled line at the top of each copy (marks on screen), and again with every `.dic` request held by a route until the typing is done, then released (the marks arrive, still without a long task). Each types a phrase with a misspelling at the start of the script and waits out the pause and the check; it is the best of three runs, so a busy machine's one stray long task doesn't fail it, while a long task in every run does.
- **2026-10-29** — **The script's dictionary, incrementally.** A session test adds `dictionary` to the metadata block after a check and finds the word unmarked with nothing tokenized or checked again, then takes the block out and finds it marked again. `parse` reads the block only with its JSON on the lines after `/* drafter` (as `setMeta` writes it), so the test's block is written that way; a one-line `/* drafter {…} */` is ordinary boneyard.
- **2026-10-29** — **The spelling menu (Calls 12 and 19).** `SpellMenu.vue` is one `MenuButton` whose trigger, a 1×1 button (`tabindex="-1"`, named "Spelling suggestions", so the menu is named by it), is in the page only while the menu is open (unmounted once it has closed), placed under the word's first character (`coordsAtPos`) or where a request says (the phone's button, Task 6), and opened as a request arrives in `spellMenu` (`spell/menu.ts`). It lists the suggestions ("Finding suggestions…" until `useSpell().suggest` answers, "No suggestions" when it has none; at most five), then Add to this script's dictionary, Add to my dictionary, and Ignore here. Each action runs once the menu has closed, on the word it was opened for, if it is still marked where it was (else it says the word isn't there now; Add to my dictionary takes the word regardless); then the editor gets focus back (also after Escape and Tab), with the cursor where it was, or after the word a suggestion put in. A right-click opens it only on a drawn `.cm-misspelled` element in the Source (`preventDefault`); anywhere else the editor's `contextmenu` handler does nothing, so the browser's or the desktop app's menu is untouched. Where the browser sends `contextmenu` with a button still down (macOS, Linux, and a Mac's Ctrl+click: `buttons !== 0`), the menu opens on the release, if the word is still marked where it was (one right-click waits at a time, and a key cancels it): Reka's items take a `pointerup` as a choice, and a menu opened under the pointer (it did on iPhone SE) chose its first item on the release. In a read-only tab (`useOutline().locked`: another tab has the script, or its file is being checked) the reason heads the menu, as the Outline's does, and the suggestions and Add to this script's dictionary are off (`aria-disabled`); Add to my dictionary and Ignore here work, since neither edits the file. Add to this script's dictionary also says why it's off as its description: a Markdown document (no metadata block, Call 16) or a stray `/*`. `whyNot` returns these sentences rather than codes, since the menu and the commands show them as they are. The item builder is `spell/menu-items.ts`, apart from `menu.ts`, so the editor's extensions (which open the menu) don't import the actions, which import `useSpell` and through it the editor session.
- **2026-10-29** — **Add to this script's dictionary: one undo, unflagged at once.** `addToScript` applies `addToScriptDictionary`'s change with `applyTextChanges` and, in the same transaction, `unmarkWord`, so every case of the word is unmarked before any check. `invertSpellMarks` (`@drafter/cm-fountain`, in the history's `invertedEffects` as `spellMarkHistory`) makes the undo put those marks back (`restoreSpellMarks`, positioned in the text before the edit and mapped through later edits) and the redo unmark the word again; an unmark with no edit (Add to my dictionary) is no undo step. A check sent before the word was added may still be on its way: the marks field remembers the words unmarked since the last `setSpellMarks` and leaves them out of the next one, so that reply can't mark the word again. A possessive is added as its word (`Quorravel's` → `Quorravel`, since a dictionary's words match as possessives too), and both forms are unmarked. Add to my dictionary adds the word (if it isn't there in any case) to `personalDictionary`, then unmarks it; the setting's change sends a fresh check.
- **2026-10-29** — **Ignore here is one occurrence, for the visit (Call 10).** It is `ignoreHere` on the mark's range: nothing is written to the file or the settings, an edit that touches the word ends it, and the same word elsewhere stays marked. A word to leave alone for good goes in a dictionary.
- **2026-10-29** — **The commands and their keys (Call 11).** Edit: Spelling suggestions (Mod-.), Add word to this script’s dictionary, Add word to my dictionary, Ignore misspelling here; Go: Next misspelling (Mod-;) and Previous misspelling (Mod-Shift-;). Each is on only while spellcheck is on ("Spellcheck is off in Settings."), shows the Source and focuses the editor first, and the four on a word act on the mark at the cursor, or say "No misspelled word at the cursor." Next and Previous select the word (wrapping) and say "phnoe. Suggestion: phone." or "drowr. No suggestions." (the word as the checker read it, without emphasis `*`s), or "No misspelled words." The keys are the editor's own (a key whose command is off goes to the browser); none clashes with the registry, CodeMirror's keymaps, the browsers, or the systems, and F7 would turn on caret browsing in Firefox. The titles use ’, as the app's other text does. Announcements go through `sayOutline`'s live region.
- **2026-10-29** — **A suggestion changes only the letters that differ.** The word is asked about as the checker read it (`un*believ*eable` as `unbelieveable`), and `replacement` finds what it shares with the suggestion at the start and the end, maps those offsets back onto the source (stepping over `*`s; ’ is one character, as its `'` is), and replaces only the middle: `un*believ*eable` becomes `un*believ*able`, `**reciev**ed` `**receiv**ed`, and `Brannik’s` `Brannick’s`, keeping the emphasis and the curly apostrophe. A `*` inside the changed letters goes to their start (`reci*e*ved` → `rec*ei*ved`); letters only added go after the letter before them. The cursor goes after the word's new end.
- **2026-10-29** — **The Spelling group is the app's, from the marks (Call 13 ◆).** §21's table puts "the Spelling group" in `@drafter/lint`, but §12 says spelling marks aren't lint diagnostics, and the lint package would need the worker's results; `@drafter/lint` is unchanged. `spellingRows` (`spell/rows.ts`) reads the marks from the editor's state, with `useProblems` reading the diagnostics, once a frame, as `spelling`: never in `problems` or `count`, and empty while spellcheck is off. Each row is keyed `word:n`, as `occurrenceKeys` keys a rule's problems, so a row (and focus in it) survives an edit above. The Problems pane shows a **Spelling** heading (an `h2`, `tabindex="-1"`, "Spelling: 3 words", "Spelling: 1 word", "Spelling: none found") whenever spellcheck is on, and the list after it. **Check spelling** (Edit) is on with spellcheck off too, so that it can say "Spellcheck is off. Turn it on in Settings." (it changes no setting); on, it awaits `useSpell().settled()` (a check waiting out its 300 ms pause is sent at once, and the newest reply is awaited; at once when off or halted after a failure; at most 10 s, for a dictionary still loading), saying "Checking spelling…" once the wait passes 300 ms, then shows Problems as Show Problems does, focuses the heading, and says "3 misspelled words.". It never says a count it hasn't got: after the 10 s, or with the state not `ready`, it says "Still checking spelling. Problems will list the words when it’s done.", and after a failed load the failed line. A second Check spelling while one waits is ignored, so focus moves once (`makeCheckSpelling`, `spell/check.ts`, a closure the registry runs). The heading follows `useSpell().state` too: "Spelling: checking…" while loading, "Spelling: the dictionary couldn’t load" after a failure. `useProblems` reads the rows through `spellingReader`, which reuses its list while `spellMarksKey` (new in `@drafter/cm-fountain`: the marks' decoration set, the same object until the marks may have changed) and the doc are unchanged, so a cursor move or a lint update costs no walk over the marks. With spellcheck on, a Markdown document's empty Problems says "No script checks for Markdown documents." above the Spelling group; off, "No checks for Markdown documents." as before.
- **2026-10-29** — **One Spelling row per occurrence (Call 14).** The word and its line, in one button that goes to the word and selects it (`goto` with `select`), so Mod-. opens its menu from there; no fix buttons in the row, which keeps it the shape of the other rows. The rows take the mark's color as their bar. The pane's focus-after-a-fix query is limited to the problem list, so a Spelling row is never where a fix sends focus.
- **2026-10-29** — **The phone's Spelling button (Call 15).** `SPELLING_BUTTON` runs Spelling suggestions' command id (its tooltip has that command's key), and shows on the element bar only while `!useWideScreen()`, spellcheck is on, and the cursor is on a mark (read on every state change and every update, since marks arrive as an effect), after the element buttons and before More, in the toolbar's arrow-key order. A press keeps the editor's focus (`mousedown.prevent`, as the other buttons) and opens the menu under the button (`openSpellMenu` with the button's lower-left corner). The bar is hidden in a read-only tab, so the button needs no disabled state; the menu says why its edits are off.
- **2026-10-29** — **Settings' spelling controls (Calls 3, 8, and 9).** After the Spellcheck switch: **Spelling language** (a select of `SPELL_LANGUAGES`, named by `DICTIONARIES`), **Check notes and boneyard** (a switch, off by default), and **My dictionary**: its words as typed, each with a Remove button named "Remove <word>"; a word removed changes `personalDictionary`, which checks again at once, and focus goes to the Remove that took its place, the one before it, or the "No words yet." line. More than 10 words and the heading says how many ("My dictionary: 12 words"), and the list scrolls. Words are added only from the spelling menu. The language's description links to Third-party licences (`/docs/licences/`, in a new tab, as the help pages open from the app), underlined so it isn't told apart by color alone. The words are keyed by index (a list of strings, no ids).
- **2026-10-29** — **Two bundled dictionaries, and no download mechanism (Call 2 ◆).** Drafter bundles US and British English (Phase 2 design §12 asks for no more, and defers other languages to Phase 4's packs). Technical design §10's downloadable dictionary packs are not built, which departs from §10, not from §12. `DictionaryInfo` in `@drafter/spell` is the shape a pack will take, and DEFERRED says so; technical design §10 and architecture §9 now carry a sentence saying what was built.
- **2026-10-29** — **Axe with misspellings on screen waits (Call 22).** The Done-when item "axe passes with misspellings on screen and the menu open" waits for the final accessibility steps (the maintainer, 2026-10-28) and is in DEFERRED with screen-reader testing of the marks and the menu. Plan D adds no axe tests and the existing ones stay green.
- **2026-10-29** — **The desktop app's menu gets the spelling commands, and its Chromium spellcheck leaves the editor.** `apps/desktop/src/menu.ts` lays out Check spelling, Spelling suggestions, Add word to this script's dictionary, Add word to my dictionary, and Ignore misspelling here in Edit (after Find), and Next and Previous misspelling in Go (after Next and Previous problem), the groups `COMMAND_DATA` gives them. The dictionaries are static files under the web build the app packages (`web/**/*`), so Drafter's checker works offline there, with nothing downloaded. Because the Source pane's browser spellcheck is off (Call 18), Chromium no longer marks the editor, so the two desktop tests that drove its marks and its native right-click menu are replaced by one on Drafter's: a misspelling is marked, right-click opens Drafter's menu and not the native one, a suggestion replaces the word, nothing is downloaded, and every request is to the app's own files. Chromium's bundled English dictionary stays for the other text fields (Call 18), so `src/spellcheck.ts` and its dictionaries are unchanged.
- **2026-10-29** — **Inter Tight's own licence is served and linked (the OFL's second condition).** `sync-assets.mjs` copies `apps/web/app/assets/fonts/inter-tight/OFL.txt` verbatim to `/fonts/inter-tight/OFL.txt` (the font files themselves are Vite assets, hashed), the Third-party licences page links it, and the build check requires it and Courier Prime's licence.
- **2026-10-29** — **The craft's own words (final review).** Standard screenplay vocabulary was marked in nearly every script: SCOWL-60 has no `intercut`, `voiceover`, `offscreen`, `sotto`, `voce`, `walkie`, or `pre` (checked part by part, `PRE-LAP` and `Pre-title` marked `pre`). `packages/spell/src/screen-words.ts` is a short list Drafter writes itself (clean room: from general knowledge of the craft, from no app or list), 19 words, joined into every known set (`createSpellSession`, Fountain and Markdown alike), so they are never marked. **The rule:** a word goes in only when it is a term of the screenwriting or stage-play craft that scripts use in their own text (action, directions, parentheticals, notes) and at least one bundled dictionary doesn't know it; `screen-words.test.ts` fails on a word both dictionaries know, on an entry not lowercase, sorted, and once, and past 25 entries. Plurals and possessives need no entry (the lookup's plural rule, below), nor hyphenated forms whose parts are known (`pre-lap`, `slo-mo`), and the extensions (`V.O.`, `O.S.`, `POV`) are skipped already as short capitals. Words both dictionaries already know (`montage`, `flashback`, `insert`, `establishing`, `dissolve`, `continuous`, `onscreen`, `talkie`, `contd`) aren't listed. A fixture (`screen-terms.fountain`) uses them all, in both languages, with notes off and on, and only its planted `lanturn` is marked.
- **2026-10-29** — **A hyphenated compound is known when the whole of it is.** `tokenize` gives each part of a compound (words joined by one `-`, with any emphasis `*`s beside it; `--`, a dash, or a hyphen at an edge joins nothing) the whole as its `compound`, read as `word` is. A part is unmarked when the compound, as written or with its hyphens taken out, is known (`isKnownCompound`: My dictionary's `zub-zarn` covers `zub-zarn`, a known `prelap` covers `pre-lap`); a part alone (`zub`) is still checked. The dictionary is never asked about the compound, so this only ever unmarks.
- **2026-10-29** — **A name's plural (final review).** `lookupForms` tries, after the word and its possessive's base, the base as a plural: without a final `es` only where what remains ends in s, x, z, ch, or sh (`Velloxes` as `vellox`, `Boxes` as `box`), otherwise without a final `s`; a plural possessive's `'` is taken off first. So with a cue `HALVORSEN`, `Halvorsens` and `Halvorsens'` are known but `Halvorsenes` is marked, and `kitchen` doesn't make `Kitchenes` known. It applies to every known word (names, locations, the script's dictionary, My dictionary, the craft's words), never to the dictionary's own verdicts, and not to a word of three letters or fewer. `harvestedWords` keeps only the name and its possessive's base, so a name ending in s (`ODALYS`) is never cut; `Odalyses` is now known, as the plural it is.
- **2026-10-29** — **The licence files are precached (final review).** The Third-party licences page is precached, so its links to `/licenses/*.txt` and both fonts' `OFL.txt` (six files, about 42 KB) are too, and work offline. `globPatterns` adds `licenses/*.txt` and `fonts/*/OFL.txt` by path, not `**/*.txt`, so no other text file is cached; `check-build.mjs` treats the `.txt`s of `REQUIRED_FILES` (`LICENCE_FILES`) as precached files and fails a worker that lacks one. The offline e2e test opens each link offline, and no longer uses `OFL.txt` as its example of a file not cached.
- **2026-10-29** — **The dictionaries are sent as text (final review).** Netlify compresses a file by its type and doesn't know `.aff` or `.dic`, so `_headers` (written by `scripts/csp.mjs`, as the other headers are, not `netlify.toml`) sends `/dictionaries/*` with `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`. Gzipped, the four files are about 384 KB, so "about 0.4 MB over the wire" holds. `scripts/serve.mjs` now lets a rule's Content-Type win over its own by-extension type, as Netlify does, so the e2e server sends the same.
- **2026-10-29** — **The desktop's Help menu lists Third-party Licences (final review).** After Privacy, labelled **Third-party Licences** as the other help pages are labelled for a desktop; it had fallen to the end of Help with its web title. The hardening e2e test again asserts that the other text fields keep Chromium's checker with the app's bundled dictionary (`spell.mode` is `bundled` on Linux and Windows, `system` on a Mac), as the README says.
- **2026-10-29** — **The `spellcheck` setting is Phase 1's key, reused.** The Spellcheck setting is the same `spellcheck` key Phase 1 used for the browser's spellcheck in the Source pane, not a new one, so a writer who had turned the browser's off has Drafter's off too, and turns it on in Settings; everyone else has it on, the default. Neither behaviour needed a migration.

## Structure strip (`@drafter/outline`, `apps/web`)

- **2026-10-29** — **The geometry is a pure function in `@drafter/outline`** (Call 20): `structureStrip(index, perPage)`, `linesPerPage(layout)`, and `rulerMarks(strip, widthPx)` in `src/strip.ts`, over the scene index the Outline already builds, so the strip adds no parse and no layout, and Plan E's Timeline and pacing view can reuse it. One pass over the scenes and one over the sections.
- **2026-10-29** — **The axis is in pages from each scene's page and line** (Call 4): a scene starts at `(page − 1) + (line − 1) / perPage`, ends where the next one starts, and the last one ends at its start plus `eighths / 8`, so the scenes tile the axis with no gap or overlap. `perPage` is a body page's line count (the first page numbered above 0), as the scene index's own eighths use. A section runs from its first scene's start to its last scene's end, found from each scene's `sections`, so it sits exactly over them; a section with no scenes is a tick (`to === from`) at the next scene's start after its block, or at the last scene's end. Sections deeper than `###` (`STRIP_DEPTHS`) get no row, and a depth the script doesn't use gets none either.
- **2026-10-29** — **Words when pages can't size every scene** (Call 6): with no line count, a scene with no pages, or a scene that starts where the next one does (a scene that prints nothing, which pages would make zero wide), the whole strip is by printed words, each scene `max(1, words)` long, end to end from 0, and `rulerMarks` gives none. Every scene block is then always wider than nothing.
- **2026-10-29** — **Ruler spacing: the step whose numbers fall nearest 40 px apart** (Call 25). The plan said "the smallest of 1, 2, 5, 10, 20, 50 that keeps numbers 40 px apart", but its own test case (a 120-page strip numbered 1, 10, 20… at 400 px, 3.3 px a page) has numbers 33 px apart, which that rule would make every 20 pages. Choosing the step nearest 40 px fits both of the plan's cases (every 5 at 1,200 px, every 10 at 400 px) and the design's "about 40 px apart" (§3); ties go to the larger step. Page 1 is always numbered; a tick at every page from 6 px a page up, else only the numbered marks. A page's mark is at its top, or at the axis' start for the page the first scene starts in, so page 1 shows even when the first scene starts a few lines down.
- **2026-10-29** — **The 2 ms budget is the median of 20 runs after one warm-up** (Call 19), on a generated script of 3 acts, 15 sequences, and 150 beats with a scene each, which the test first checks lays out to more than 120 pages. The strip takes under a tenth of a millisecond there (about 0.06 ms on the maintainer's machine), so a loaded machine has a wide margin, and the median ignores the odd slow run.
- **2026-10-29** — **One band in the shell's `.top`, above both panes** (Call 1): `StructureStrip.vue` is the last child of `.top` in `pages/index.vue`, shown when `structureStrip` is on, the screen is wide (`useWideScreen`), and the document is Fountain. `.top` is outside `.panes`, so the panes' four shared rows (the v0.13.1 alignment) are untouched, and focus mode, which drops `.top`, drops the strip with it (Call 15). Phones never draw it (Call 16).
- **2026-10-29** — **Keep the last paged strip while the layout catches up** (Call 6): `useStrip` builds `{ strip, ids }` in a `computed` over `useOutline().index`, `linesPerPage(layout)`, and each item's session id, and folds it into `view` through the pure `nextView`, which keeps the last view when it was by pages and the new one is by words, unless the layout has failed (then words, and no ruler). The ids are taken with the strip, so a kept block still goes to its scene as it is now, and one whose scene has gone says "That scene isn’t in the script any more." A load (`onChange(null)`) clears the view, so one script's strip is never kept over another's. The session, once made, lives for the visit, as `useOutline` does; while `structureStrip` is off its `computed` builds nothing (no strip, no id map) and the view is null, so a hidden strip costs no work after a parse.
- **2026-10-29** — **The cursor's blocks are the last scene starting at or before the cursor's block, and its sections** (Call 10): `currentBlocks` searches the scenes by block, so a blank line (which `cursorBlock` reads as the block before it) is still that scene, and a section heading after a scene's end still reads as that scene until the next one starts. Nothing before the first scene is current.
- **2026-10-29** — **Section blocks alternate two neutrals; a color is a 35% tint over `--page` with a 3 px edge** (Calls 8, 26): `blockFill` gives `color-mix(in srgb, <swatch> 35%, var(--page))` and the swatch from `SWATCH` (a word Drafter doesn't know gets none, so it is drawn neutral). Neutral is `--chrome-2`; every second section along a row is `--chrome`; each block has a 1 px `--line` right border and a 2 px minimum width, so a section's tick is never lost. The fill's text is only ever from `SWATCH`, never from the note.
- **2026-10-29** — **18 px a row plus 14 px of ruler, at most five rows** (Call 27): three section rows, the scenes, and the ruler at most, 86 px. The labels are 11 px, the ruler's numbers 9 px. A block narrower than `LABEL_MIN_PX` (28 px) shows no label (Call 7), from the track's width as one `ResizeObserver` reads it; the positions themselves are percentages, so a resize moves nothing but the labels and the ruler (Call 19).
- **2026-10-29** — **The labels** (Calls 3, 11): the row marks are `#`, `##`, `###`, and Scenes, `aria-hidden` beside groups named "Sections marked #" (and so on) and "Scenes"; a block's name is `sceneName` or `sectionName` plus ", goal: …"; `cardLines` gives the card's lines ("Scene 12, INT. KITCHEN - NIGHT", "page 12 · 1 3/8 pp · blue", "Goal: …", the first synopsis; for a section its title and "5 scenes · 12 pp"), leaving out a line with nothing in it. The card itself, going to a block, and the roving focus come with the next change.
- **2026-10-29** — **The panes never move for the strip's own sake** (Calls 6, 27): a strip by words keeps the ruler's 14 px row, drawn empty (`.ruler-space`), so the layout's first answer after a load or while typing only fills it; while the view waits for a load's index the band draws as many empty rows as it last had (one before any), plus that row. The track's width is read from `clientWidth` when it mounts, so the labels are there before the `ResizeObserver`'s first answer; each row clips its blocks, and a section's 2 px tick (no padding) is placed at most 2 px from the right edge, so one at the end of the script never pokes past the track.
- **2026-10-29** — **Going there is the Outline's go** (Call 9): a block's `click` (which a button also gets from Enter and Space) calls `useStrip().go(id)`, which calls `editor.goto(item.span.from)` for the item as it is now: the Source tab is revealed and focused, and the Script pane follows `cursorBlock`. A block whose scene has gone says so through `sayOutline` and goes nowhere. Read-only tabs go there too: `goto` moves only the cursor.
- **2026-10-29** — **One Tab stop, the arrows by `stripKey`** (Calls 12, 28): `strip/keys.ts` is pure over the `Strip`. Left and Right along a row and Home and End stop at its ends; Up and Down go to the next row's block covering the focused block's middle (a block covers from its start up to, not including, its end, so a point where two meet is the later one's; a tick covers its own place), else the one nearest by distance (the first of equals). Arrow keys, Home, and End are always `preventDefault`ed on a block, so the page never scrolls; with Alt, Ctrl, or Meta they are left alone. The one `tabindex="0"` is the block last focused this visit (kept in the component's module scope, so turning the strip off and on keeps it) while it is drawn, else the cursor's scene, else the first scene.
- **2026-10-29** — **Where you are** (Calls 10, 29): blocks in `useStrip().current` get `aria-current="location"` and a 2 px `--heading` outline inside the block (a 2 px `Highlight` border in forced colors). Focus is a 2 px `--text` outline, with a heading-colored inner ring when the focused block is current too, so the two never look alike. The in-view marker is a 4 px-wide `--heading` triangle pointing down at the ruler, `aria-hidden`, at the top of the page `scriptTopPage` names (`strip/in-view.ts`; the title page at the axis' start), drawn only while that is non-null and the strip is by pages. `ScriptPane.vue` sets it once a frame at most, on its scroll events and after each draw: the scroll-sync pane's `offsetAtTop()`, then `blockIndexAt` over the editor's parse, then `pageOf` the layout; unmounting sets it back to null, so the marker goes when the Script tab does.
- **2026-10-29** — **One card** (Call 11): a single `aria-hidden` element in the strip, for the block last hovered or focused, gone on that block's `pointerleave` or `blur`. It sits just below the band (so it never covers any block, and `pointer-events: none` keeps it out of the pointer's way), its left edge at the block's, moved in so it stays inside the strip's width; at most 360 px wide, the lines `cardLines` gives (the first in bold), over `--page`. The band is `z-index: 10`, so the card draws over the panes (none above 6) and the toolbar's Scripts and Export menus (20) open over the strip; at 20, later in the page, it covered their items (found by Task 4's Markdown test).
- **2026-10-29** — **One setting, three controls** (Calls 14, 30): `structureStrip` (off by default, per device) is set by the toolbar's **Structure** button, the palette's **Toggle the structure strip** (View, after the element bar's pair; also on the Keyboard shortcuts page and, by itself, in the desktop app's View menu after Show/Hide the element bar), and the Settings switch **Structure strip** (after Element bar). The button runs the registered command, so the three can't disagree. It is the fifth round action, after Focus, `i-lucide-panel-top` on `#b7a6e6`, a `<button>` with `aria-pressed` true while the strip shows (the setting on and a Fountain script). - **2026-10-29** — **Five actions fit at 1024 px:** the Structure button made the wide toolbar a few pixels too long between 1024 and 1033 px (1029 px of page at 1024) in a Markdown document or a fallback screenplay (the longest chip names). - **2026-10-29** — **Markdown, focus mode, phones** (Calls 15, 16): in a Markdown document the button is `aria-disabled="true"` with the title "A Markdown document has no scenes." (`NOT_FOR_MARKDOWN`, `strip/labels.ts`) and does nothing, and the command is off with that as its `why` (the palette hides it; a menu that lists a disabled command shows the reason). - **2026-10-29** — **Mod+Alt+O, by the key it types** (Call 31): Mod+. went to Spelling suggestions (Plan D), so the strip's key is Mod+Alt+O (controller ruling).
